1. **List the required placements.** Lists every aspect-ratio and placement version the platform set needs: feed square, story vertical, in-stream widescreen. Skill: `crea/derivatives/resize-for-placement`. Output: required placement list. Check: list matches the current, not stale, platform-spec sheet.
2. **Recompose, don't just crop.** Recomposes the master for each placement, repositioning subject and text rather than a blind crop, so nothing important is lost. Skill: `crea/derivatives/resize-for-placement`. Output: resized and recomposed asset set. Check: no important element, CTA, product or face, is cropped out in any version.
3. **Cut length derivatives.** If the master is long-form video, cuts a 15-second and a 6-second bumper version, each keyed to a different hook from the hook bank. Skill: `crea/derivatives/video-cutdown`. Output: length derivative set. Check: each cut opens on a distinct hook, not the same first 6 seconds repeated.
4. **Burn in captions.** Adds accurate burned-in captions to every video version, since most are watched muted. Skill: `crea/derivatives/caption-and-alt-text`. Output: captioned video set. Check: captions match the spoken line exactly, correctly timed.
5. **Write alt text.** Writes descriptive alt text for every static image version. Skill: `crea/derivatives/caption-and-alt-text`. Output: alt text set. Check: alt text describes what is actually shown, not the ad's selling line.
6. **QA the full derivative set.** Checks every derivative against the master for message-match drift before it ships. Skill: `none`. Output: QA'd derivative set. Check: every derivative says the same thing as the master, no drift.
7. **Name, tag and hand off.** Names and tags every derivative under the standing schema and hands the full set to the launch checklist. Skill: `crea/production-ops/naming-and-tagging`. Output: named, tagged derivative set. Check: every derivative has a stable id before handoff.

Outputs: resized and recomposed asset set, length derivative set, captioned video set, alt text set. QA: no important element cropped out; captions accurate and correctly timed; no message-match drift across derivatives. Read point: each derivative reads at its own placement's read point inside CREA-W01. Duration: agent time: 1 to 2 hours per master.

### CREA-W23 Clear stock footage, fonts and music rights before a video ships

Trigger: event: before a video ad using licensed assets ships. Human role: Rights clearance checker. Capabilities: CREA-C49. Needs: every licensed stock footage, font and music asset used in the video, each asset's actual license terms.

1. **List every licensed asset used.** Lists every piece of stock footage, font and music track used in the finished video. Skill: `crea/compliance/rights-clearance`. Output: licensed-asset list. Check: list is exhaustive, cross-checked against the actual timeline, not memory.
2. **Pull each asset's real license terms.** Pulls the actual license terms for each asset: commercial use, territory, term length, exclusivity. Skill: `crea/compliance/rights-clearance`. Output: license terms per asset. Check: terms are the actual document, not a remembered summary.
3. **Match use to terms.** Checks the video's actual use, paid ad, territory, duration of the campaign, against each license's real limits. Skill: `crea/compliance/rights-clearance`. Output: match or mismatch result per asset. Check: every asset gets a clear pass or a named mismatch.
4. **Fix or swap a mismatch.** Swaps out any asset whose license does not cover this use, before the video ships. Skill: `crea/compliance/rights-clearance`. Output: cleared asset set. Check: no mismatched asset remains in the final video. Ask the founder if: a needed asset has no compliant replacement and the founder must decide to reshoot, re-license or drop it.
5. **Log the clearance.** Logs the clearance record for each asset so a later renewal or takedown risk can be tracked. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: clearance log entry. Check: log entry includes each license's expiry or renewal date.

Outputs: license terms per asset, cleared asset set, clearance log entry. QA: every asset in the final video has a matching, logged license. Read point: not applicable directly; a compliance gate before CREA-W01's launch checklist. Duration: agent time: 30 to 60 minutes per video.

### CREA-W24 Build catalogue-scale ad variants and DPA overlay templates from the feed

Trigger: event: new catalogue campaign, or on feed refresh. Human role: Catalogue and DPA creative template builder. Capabilities: CREA-C43, CREA-C52. Needs: current product feed: price, image, SKU fields, brand kit, a small set of approved copy blocks.

1. **Audit the feed for dirty data.** Checks the feed's price and image fields for gaps, wrong formats or stale data before building anything on top of it. Skill: `crea/make-creatives/catalogue-dpa`. Output: feed audit result. Check: audit flags every SKU with a missing or malformed required field. Ask the founder if: more than a set share of SKUs fail the audit.
2. **Design the overlay template.** Designs the price, discount and badge overlay template so it renders correctly across every SKU's real image dimensions and price-string length. Skill: `crea/make-creatives/catalogue-dpa`. Output: overlay template. Check: template is tested against the longest real price string and the smallest real product image in the feed.
3. **Write the copy block set.** Writes a small set of reusable copy blocks, headline and primary-text patterns, that plug into any SKU's feed fields. Skill: `crea/write-copy/primary-text`. Output: reusable copy block set. Check: every block reads correctly with a real, varied sample of SKU data plugged in.
4. **Generate the full variant grid.** Combines the template, the feed's fields and the copy blocks to produce the full grid of ad variants across every SKU. Skill: `crea/make-creatives/catalogue-dpa`. Output: catalogue-scale variant grid. Check: spot-check a random sample of SKUs for correct price, image and copy pairing.
5. **Flag catalogue and DPA risk.** Flags anything that will break at scale: a badge that overlaps a price string on a particular SKU, a missing image, a discontinued item still live. Skill: `crea/make-creatives/catalogue-dpa`. Output: risk flag list. Check: every flagged risk names the specific SKU.
6. **Run the claim pass on the copy blocks.** Runs the claim-tier check once on the reusable copy blocks, since they apply across the whole grid. Skill: `none`. Output: audited copy block set. Check: no block claim outside the approved or site-backed tier.
7. **Hand off to MEDIA and Product and Supply.** Hands the finished grid and the risk flag list to MEDIA for the DPA campaign build and to Product and Supply for feed fixes. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff package. Check: every flagged risk has a named owner before handoff closes.

Outputs: overlay template, catalogue-scale variant grid, risk flag list. QA: template tested against real longest and shortest feed values; every SKU-level risk named, not generalized. Read point: next full week's DPA performance after go-live, read by MEDIA. Duration: agent time: 2 to 4 hours for the first build, about 30 minutes on a routine feed refresh.

### CREA-W25 Prepare the bulk upload sheet and hand off for draft-upload

Trigger: event: creative batch approved for launch. Human role: Launch operations preparer. Capabilities: CREA-C35, CREA-C48. Needs: approved batch of ad copy and creative filenames, the target platform's current bulk-upload or Ads Manager bulk-editor spec.

1. **Confirm the batch is fully approved.** Confirms every item in the batch cleared CREA-W01's launch checklist before building the sheet. Skill: `none`. Output: confirmed batch. Check: no item without a passed checklist enters the sheet.
2. **Pull the current bulk-upload spec.** Pulls the exact column and format spec the target platform's bulk-upload tool or Ads Manager bulk editor currently expects. Skill: `crea/production-ops/bulk-upload-sheet`. Output: current spec. Check: spec checked within 30 days.
3. **Build the sheet, one row per ad.** Builds the exact spreadsheet or CSV, one row per ad, matching every required column. Skill: `crea/production-ops/bulk-upload-sheet`. Output: bulk-upload sheet. Check: every row has every required column filled, no blank required field.
4. **Spot-check against the creative brief.** Spot-checks a sample of rows against their original creative brief for a copy or filename mismatch. Skill: `crea/production-ops/bulk-upload-sheet`. Output: spot-check result. Check: sample shows zero mismatches, or every mismatch is fixed before handoff.
5. **Draft-upload as paused, unpublished.** Where the founder has approved it, drives Ads Manager's own web interface to create one paused, unpublished draft ad per approved creative, so a human reviews a real draft inside the platform before anything goes live. Skill: `crea/production-ops/draft-upload-browser-agent`. Output: paused draft ads inside Ads Manager. Check: every created draft is confirmed paused and unpublished, never activated. Ask the founder if: the browser-agent step has not yet been approved for this account.
6. **Hand off to MEDIA for review and go-live.** Hands the sheet and the draft-ad list to MEDIA, who reviews each real draft and decides go-live. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff package. Check: MEDIA confirms receipt of both the sheet and the draft list.

Outputs: bulk-upload sheet, paused draft ads inside Ads Manager. QA: no draft created ever gets activated by CREA; spec checked within 30 days. Read point: not applicable directly; read happens once MEDIA activates and CREA-W01's read point applies. Duration: agent time: 1 to 2 hours per batch.

## Media buyer (MEDIA), 30 workflows

### MEDIA-W01 Audit the Meta ad account

Trigger: monthly. Human role: media buyer running the monthly account health check. Capabilities: MEDIA-C01. Needs: brand brain: products and margin, connector: meta ads read (gallery cache + MCP re-pull), metric contract: cpa, roas_blended, spend.

1. **Pull 12 months of account data.** Refresh the gallery cache and pull account, campaign, ad set and ad level data for the trailing 12 months. Skill: `media/account-ops/audit-meta`. Output: 12-month account dataset. Check: data covers all 12 months with no platform gap unmarked.
2. **Run the structure checklist.** Check campaign purpose lines, ad set count against spend tier, naming consistency, CBO vs ABO use. Skill: `media/account-ops/audit-meta`. Output: structure checklist result. Check: every campaign has a stated purpose or is flagged.
3. **Run the creative review in fixed order.** Score creative volume, then diversity, then quality, in that order, never skipping ahead to quality. Skill: `media/account-ops/audit-meta`. Output: creative review scorecard. Check: volume was scored before diversity, diversity before quality.
4. **Score readable-data and commercial-alignment tests.** Check whether results can be read without guessing, and whether spend matches the business's real priorities. Skill: `media/account-ops/audit-meta`. Output: two test scores. Check: both tests have a pass/fail, not a vague impression.
5. **Validate proposed structure changes.** Run any candidate structure change through the 4 fixed validation questions before it can enter the roadmap. Skill: `media/account-ops/audit-meta`. Output: validated change list. Check: no change entered the roadmap without passing all 4 questions.
6. **Rank findings by money at stake.** Order every finding by estimated spend or revenue impact, not by ease of fix. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked finding list. Check: the top finding has the largest number behind it, verified in code.
7. **Write the 90-day roadmap.** Turn the ranked list into dated actions, each naming the metric it should move. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: ranked 90-day roadmap. Check: the roadmap names one single biggest constraint, not ten equal items.
8. **Package the founder brief.** Write the one-paragraph plain-English summary and attach the full audit and change list. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder-facing audit summary. Check: summary uses short sentences, digits, no jargon.

Outputs: audit report, ranked 90-day roadmap, change list for structure fixes. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; the roadmap names the single biggest constraint, not a list of equal findings. Read point: read at hand-off: founder reviews the roadmap; each named fix is re-measured at the next monthly audit. Duration: agent time: about 45 minutes; waiting time: none, all data is already connected.

### MEDIA-W02 Design Meta account structure and naming

Trigger: event: spend tier change, after an audit, or on demand. Human role: media buyer restructuring the account to match current spend. Capabilities: MEDIA-C09, MEDIA-C10. Needs: brand brain: catalogue, personas, metric contract: spend, cpa, MEDIA-W01 audit output.

1. **Place the account in its spend tier.** Compute trailing 30-day spend and classify it small, mid or large. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: spend tier label. Check: tier matches the fixed dollar bands, not a guess.
2. **Pick the matching structure template.** Apply the small, mid or large template exactly as defined. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: template selection. Check: the chosen template matches the tier's written rule.
3. **Check for campaigns to merge or consolidate.** Flag campaigns too thin on conversions to optimise alone; check for full consolidation eligibility. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: merge/consolidate list. Check: no live campaign is left below the thin-data threshold unflagged.
4. **Write one purpose line per campaign.** Draft a one-line, defendable purpose for every existing and new campaign. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: purpose line per campaign. Check: every campaign has exactly one purpose line, not two competing ones.
5. **Set the ad set naming pattern.** Define persona, angle, offer, content type, launch date as the fixed naming fields. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: naming convention doc. Check: the pattern applies to every existing ad set without exception, or exceptions are listed.
6. **Draft the rename and restructure change list.** List every campaign or ad set that needs a rename, merge or split, with the exact before and after. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-structure`. Output: change list. Check: every row has a current value and a proposed value.
7. **Run pre-launch QA on any new structural element.** Check any newly created campaign or ad set against the trafficking checklist before it is marked ready. Skill: `media/account-ops/trafficking-qa`. Output: QA pass/fail. Check: every new element passed or is held with a reason.

Outputs: structure diagram, naming convention doc, change list for campaign consolidation, split or rename. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; every campaign has a purpose line and every ad set follows the naming pattern. Read point: read point: re-checked at the next monthly audit or the next spend-tier change. Duration: agent time: about 30 minutes; waiting time: none.

### MEDIA-W03 Launch a new Meta concept ad set

Trigger: event: a new concept (SKU + pain point + offer) is ready to test. Human role: media buyer launching a new creative test, the founder's own worked example. Capabilities: MEDIA-C11, MEDIA-C12, MEDIA-C13, MEDIA-C14, MEDIA-C15, MEDIA-C59. Needs: brand brain: personas, offers, SKUs, reference scout: creative references, approved creative from CREA/ADC, MEDIA-W01 audit output.

1. **Pull the objection map and references.** Read the objection map and creative references the paid creative specialist built for this concept. Skill: `none`. Output: objection map and references on hand. Check: every objection has at least 2 quoted sources.
2. **Define the concept.** Fix the concept as one SKU, one pain point, one offer; confirm no persona overlap with a live ad set. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-concept`. Output: concept definition. Check: a change to SKU, pain point or offer would start a new ad set, confirmed.
3. **Size the test.** Compute the test budget (target conversions x CPA / test days) and the kill-rule threshold (3-5x target CPA). Skill: `media/pace-budget/meta`. Output: test brief with budget and read window. Check: the ad set's planned budget can reach the read-spend threshold inside the window.
4. **Build the audience.** Go broad in cold targeting; exclude existing customers sized to the repurchase cycle; cap any interest test as a labelled sandbox. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-concept`. Output: audience plan. Check: existing customers are excluded and cold targeting is broad.
5. **Set placement and creative-edit controls.** Remove weak placements and turn off Advantage+ creative edits and dynamic crop. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-concept`. Output: placement and creative-edit change list. Check: every product ad has automatic edits turned off.
6. **Choose bid strategy.** Default to CBO; switch to ABO only at high AOV with low daily order volume; set any cap at ad-set level. Skill: `media/build-campaign/meta-concept`. Output: bid strategy setting. Check: no cap is set at ad level.
7. **Brief and receive the ad set.** Send the concept, audience and offer to CREA/ADC for scripts and creative; receive back the concept ad plus 3 hook variations. Skill: `none`. Output: concept ad + 3 hooks ready. Check: the ad set has enough ads for 2-6 exposures, minimum 3.
8. **Run a feedback and reference round.** Check the creative against the brand's references and the founder's past likes/dislikes before it is called final. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: feedback notes or approval. Check: every claim in the ad traces to an approved source.
9. **Run the pre-launch trafficking QA.** Confirm pixel fires on the landing page, targeting matches the plan, UTM is correct, and creative meets spec. Skill: `media/account-ops/trafficking-qa`. Output: QA pass/fail. Check: every check passed or the launch is held.
10. **Hand off the launch checklist.** Package the full build (concept, audience, placement, bid, creative, QA result) as a change list for a human to apply in Ads Manager. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: launch checklist / change list. Check: the checklist has every setting a person needs, nothing left implicit. Ask the founder if: Meta writes are switched on and the launch itself could be applied directly instead of handed off.
11. **Set the read point.** Confirm the date and metric at which this test will first be judged, per the test brief. Skill: `media/read-results/meta`. Output: scheduled read date. Check: the read date matches the sized window, not an arbitrary check-in.

Outputs: ad set build plan, test brief with budget and read window, launch checklist / change list, kill-rule sheet. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; the ad set has one SKU, one pain point, one offer; the trafficking QA checklist passed before hand-off. Read point: read point: at the test brief's read window (never under 5 days below $50k/month spend), first on the kill-rule threshold, not before. Duration: agent time: about 1.5 hours across research, build and QA; waiting time: 1-2 days for creative from CREA/ADC.

### MEDIA-W04 Run the daily Meta read and kill/keep/scale calls

Trigger: daily. Human role: media buyer doing the morning account check. Capabilities: MEDIA-C45, MEDIA-C46, MEDIA-C17. Needs: metric contract: full, connector: meta ads read, gallery cache.

1. **Pull yesterday's full-day numbers.** Blend spend, orders and revenue into MER in code for the last full day and the trailing 14 days. Skill: `media/read-results/meta`. Output: daily numbers. Check: the window ends on the last full day, never a partial day.
2. **Check for anomalies.** Compute the trailing 14-day median for spend and ROAS; flag any day 50% or more off it. Skill: `media/read-results/meta`. Output: anomaly flag list. Check: swings under about $1,000/day are treated as noise, not flagged.
3. **Diagnose a flagged anomaly.** For any flagged day, check pixel/CAPI/feed health first, then name the 3 ads most responsible. Skill: `media/read-results/meta`. Output: anomaly root-cause note. Check: tracking health was checked before bidding was blamed.
4. **Read the ad-set-level table.** Apply the keep/yellow/kill table at 3-5x target CPA, at ad-set level, never on one ad's own number. Skill: `media/kill-scale/meta`. Output: keep/kill/scale call list. Check: every call was made at ad-set level.

5. **Size the budget to platform benchmarks.** Size proposed spend against the platform's own category CPC/ROAS benchmark, not a Meta or Google number. Skill: `media/pace-budget/tiktok`. Output: budget size proposal. Check: the number traces to this platform's own benchmark.
6. **Flag stockout risk before scaling.** Check dark-store inventory against the proposed spend; flag any risk of ad delivery outpacing stock. Skill: `media/kill-scale/marketplace`. Output: stockout risk flag. Check: the flag exists before any scale-up is proposed.
7. **Write the retail media brief.** Package everything into a platform-specific brief for a human to execute in the brand console. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: retail media brief per platform. Check: the brief keeps quick-commerce reporting separate from Meta/Google.

Outputs: retail media brief per platform. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; no SKU with a negative or unproven contribution margin is briefed for spend. Read point: read point: before any spend, and at the founder's review of the brief. Duration: agent time: about 1 hour; waiting time: none.

### MEDIA-W28 Evaluate and pilot a new ad surface or DSP

Trigger: when retargeting frequency/reach flatten, or existing channels are past their scaling gates, or a quarterly channel review. Human role: growth marketer evaluating channel expansion. Capabilities: MEDIA-C54, MEDIA-C62. Needs: current retargeting pool size and frequency, current channel saturation state, a shortlist of candidate surfaces or DSPs, brand CAC/ROAS targets.

1. **Confirm saturation with data.** Check frequency and reach data to confirm existing channels are actually saturated, not assumed to be. Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: saturation confirmation. Check: saturation is shown with data, not assumed.
2. **Shortlist candidate surfaces or DSPs.** Compare options by data-source fit against the brand's category (shopping data vs streaming vs marketplace-native). Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: shortlist with fit rationale. Check: every shortlisted option has a stated fit reason.
3. **Estimate cost against incremental reach.** Estimate minimum spend and management fee against expected incremental reach for each shortlisted option. Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: cost/reach estimate. Check: every option has a cost estimate, not just a feature list.
4. **Size the smallest usable pilot.** Size the pilot budget as the smallest spend that can produce a statistically usable read. Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: pilot budget size. Check: the budget is sized to produce a read, not a token amount.
5. **Set stop and scale thresholds.** Set the pilot's stop and scale thresholds before it starts, matching the existing creative kill-rule discipline. Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: stop/scale thresholds. Check: thresholds exist before any spend starts.
6. **Build minimum viable creative and tracking.** Specify the creative sizes and tracking the pilot needs without diverting the main creative pipeline. Skill: `media/partnerships/channel-pilot`. Output: creative-spec brief. Check: the brief does not pull resources from the main pipeline.
7. **Route for founder approval.** Present the full pilot plan and cost before any spend commits. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: the founder approved before any spend started. Ask the founder if: always, this capability is founder_gate.
8. **Read the pilot against brand targets.** At the stop/scale point, read the pilot against the brand's own CAC/ROAS targets, not the new platform's benchmarks. Skill: `media/read-results/marketplace`. Output: pilot read. Check: the read used the brand's own targets.
9. **Log the result.** Write the learning to the experiment registry whether the pilot won or failed. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: the result is logged regardless of outcome.

Outputs: go/no-go recommendation with DSP shortlist and creative-spec brief, pilot test plan, registry entry. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style. Read point: read point: at the pilot's pre-set stop/scale threshold. Duration: agent time: about 1.5 hours to evaluate and plan; waiting time: the pilot's full read window.

### MEDIA-W29 Track and resolve ad policy rejections and account quality

Trigger: any new ad disapproval, or a daily account-quality check. Human role: ad ops specialist keeping the account in good standing. Capabilities: MEDIA-C55. Needs: platform disapproval reasons and account-quality dashboards, the brand's claim tiers.

1. **Pull today's disapprovals.** Check Meta, Google and TikTok for any new ad disapproval and its stated reason. Skill: `media/partnerships/policy-appeals`. Output: disapproval list. Check: every disapproval from every connected platform is captured.
2. **Map the reason to the real cause.** Match the stated reason to the specific policy clause and the likely real cause: claim, image text ratio, restricted wording, or landing page mismatch. Skill: `media/partnerships/policy-appeals`. Output: cause mapping. Check: every disapproval has a named clause, not a guess.
3. **Propose the smallest fix.** Draft the smallest edit that clears the violation without changing the offer or claim tier. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: fix proposal. Check: the fix does not change the claim tier without founder sign-off.
4. **Draft an appeal where warranted.** For a rejection believed wrongly applied, draft the appeal text for a human to submit. Skill: `media/partnerships/policy-appeals`. Output: appeal draft. Check: the draft is ready for a human to review and submit, never auto-submitted. Ask the founder if: always, before an appeal is submitted.
5. **Watch the account-quality trend.** Check the account-quality or account-health score for a downward trend. Skill: `media/partnerships/policy-appeals`. Output: account-quality trend flag. Check: a downward trend is flagged before it causes a restriction.
6. **Log the pattern.** Add the rejection to the running log by category to catch a repeating pattern. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: rejection pattern log. Check: a repeating pattern across ads is flagged, not treated as separate incidents.

Outputs: daily rejection log with cause and fix, appeal drafts, account-quality trend flag. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; every rejection has a named clause and a proposed fix. Read point: read point: daily. Duration: agent time: about 15 minutes on a normal day; more on a day with a new disapproval.

### MEDIA-W30 Build and maintain automated bid, budget and pause rules

Trigger: once thresholds are established, then continuously. Human role: performance marketing manager standing up rule-based automation. Capabilities: MEDIA-C63. Needs: existing kill/keep/scale thresholds, platform automation/rule-engine capability.

1. **Pull the brand's existing thresholds.** Read the kill/keep/scale thresholds already in use by hand on each platform. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: threshold list. Check: every threshold traces to an already-approved rule, not a new invention.
2. **Translate thresholds into if-then rules.** Write explicit if-then rules per platform: pause at 3-5x target CPA, cut budget X% on Y-day underperformance, alert on frequency threshold. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: draft rule set. Check: every rule maps one-to-one to an existing threshold.
3. **Keep the rule set consistent across platforms.** Adapt each rule to Meta, Google and TikTok's own automation options while keeping the underlying logic identical. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: cross-platform rule set. Check: the same underlying logic applies on every platform.
4. **Route for founder approval.** Present the full rule set before it goes live on any surface. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: the founder approved the rule set before activation. Ask the founder if: always, this capability is founder_gate.
5. **Hold every rule inactive until writes are on.** Confirm no rule can apply a live change on a surface where the founder has not switched writes on. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: activation gate check. Check: a rule cannot fire live on a surface without writes switched on.
6. **Log every firing.** Record the before/after state for every rule firing once active. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: firing log. Check: every firing has a before and after state recorded.
7. **Watch for over-firing.** Auto-pause a rule that fires far more than expected, since that usually means tracking broke. Skill: `media/partnerships/rule-automation`. Output: over-firing pause. Check: an over-firing rule is paused, not left running.

Outputs: documented rule set per platform, firing log. QA: every number in the output came from code, not the model's own arithmetic; no metric shows a computed 0 where the source was actually missing; no write is proposed on a surface the founder has not switched on; the output is a change list instead; no competitor text, logo, product or face appears anywhere in the output; reading level and sentence length match house style; no rule applies a live change on a surface without writes switched on. Read point: read point: continuous once live; founder review at setup and at any rule change. Duration: agent time: about 2 hours to set up; waiting time: none once live, ongoing log review daily.

## Lifecycle (LIFE), 30 workflows

### LIFE-W01 Audit and sequence the lifecycle build

Trigger: monthly. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager doing the monthly program audit. Capabilities: LIFE-C01. Needs: brand brain: products and markets, ESP flow list and status, 90 days of order/event history, registry: past flow builds and results.

1. **Pull the flow inventory.** Reads every live and draft flow in the ESP (welcome, abandon, post-purchase, win-back, sunset, replenishment, loyalty, back-in-stock) with its status and last edit date. Skill: `life/lifecycle-audit`. Output: flow inventory table. Check: every flow type in the standard set has a row, even 'missing'. Ask the founder if: ESP connector not reachable.
2. **Read the flow-vs-campaign revenue split.** Pulls attributed revenue by flow and by campaign for the last 90 days from the metric contract. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: revenue split with window. Check: figures are totals, never averaged from breakdowns.
3. **Score each required flow's health.** For every flow that exists, checks the trigger actually fires, entry/exit/re-entry filters are set, and it does not claim the same customer stage as another flow. Skill: `life/lifecycle-audit`. Output: health score per flow with the exact gap named. Check: every gap cites the specific setting missing, not a general complaint.
4. **Flag missing flows against the standard set.** Lists any of the 8 standard flow types with no live version. Skill: `life/lifecycle-audit`. Output: missing-flow list. Check: list matches the standard set exactly, no invented category.
5. **Check the segmentation and deliverability layers.** Confirms the segmentation model and the sender health check both ran this month, since a broken flow is often a broken input underneath it. Skill: `life/lifecycle-audit`. Output: two pass/fail checks with dates. Check: a stale segment or a red deliverability flag outranks a missing flow.
6. **Rank the backlog by expected revenue impact.** Orders missing or broken flows and segmentation/deliverability gaps by expected marginal revenue, sized off order volume and AOV. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked backlog with score parts visible. Check: no arithmetic performed in the model, only in code.
7. **Check the registry for repeat items.** For any backlog item that matches a past rejected or parked idea for this brand, pulls the prior reason. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: backlog items flagged with prior history. Check: a repeat item states the new reason it is proposed again.
8. **Draft the monthly build-sequence report.** Assembles the ranked backlog, health scores and flagged repeats into one report. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: monthly lifecycle backlog report. Check: matches the report-section output shape.
9. **Self-QA and post.** Runs the LIFE QA checklist, then posts as an agent_does report since nothing external changes yet. Skill: `none`. Output: final report. Check: every number traces to the metric contract or an ESP read.

Outputs: ranked backlog report, flow health scorecard. QA: every score traces to a metric-contract field or an ESP read; missing flows checked against the full standard set; no arithmetic in the model. Read point: next month's audit compares the same scorecard month over month. Duration: 45 minutes agent time, no waiting.

### LIFE-W02 Design and test the on-site capture pop-up

Trigger: on demand. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager / CRO owner for capture. Capabilities: LIFE-C02. Needs: brand brain: products, current offer ladder, site audit access, metric contract: current opt-in rate and full-site conversion, approved idea record.

1. **Read the approved idea and current pop-up.** Pulls the idea record and the live pop-up config (offer, fields, trigger, targeting). Skill: `life/popup-spec`. Output: current-state summary. Check: every current setting is quoted, not assumed.
2. **Pick the offer type before copy or design.** Chooses percent-off, flat amount, free gift, or a non-discount hook (early access, guide) from the brand's discount policy and margin. Skill: `life/popup-spec`. Output: chosen offer type with the reason. Check: offer type checked against discount-policy-check before it is designed further. Ask the founder if: no discount policy on file for this offer size.
3. **Set fields, trigger timing and targeting exclusions.** Sets the minimum fields to capture (email, phone for SMS/WhatsApp consent), the trigger (time, scroll, exit-intent) and who is excluded (existing subscribers, checkout page). Skill: `life/popup-spec`. Output: field/trigger/exclusion spec. Check: no hidden or hard-to-find close button; exclusions cover existing subscribers.
4. **Draft mobile and desktop variants.** Writes the headline, offer line and CTA at a skimmable reading level for both breakpoints. Skill: `life/popup-spec`. Output: two draft variants. Check: reading level under grade 10; claim-check pass on the offer line.
5. **Run claim-check on the draft copy.** Checks the offer line and any urgency language against the claims tiers and forbidden-words list. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail with cited rule. Check: fails closed on any unverifiable urgency claim.
6. **Propose the test order.** Sequences pop-up tests (offer type before copy before design), one variable at a time, with the current variant as the control. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: test queue with hypothesis per test. Check: each test isolates one variable.
7. **Assemble the pop-up spec card.** Builds the founder card: current state, proposed change, expected effect and basis, cost, risk. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: heading is an action; no acronym unexplained.
8. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and hands the card to the founder gate since this changes what every visitor sees. Skill: `none`. Output: gated card. Check: no external write made; artefact is a draft/spec.

Outputs: pop-up spec, test queue. QA: judged by full-site conversion and signed-up-customer value, not opt-in rate alone; no hidden close button; offer type cleared the discount-policy-check. Read point: read at 2 weeks per test on full-site conversion and list-value per visitor, not opt-in rate alone. Duration: 2 hours agent time; founder decision then 1-2 weeks live per test.

### LIFE-W03 Set consent, quiet hours and unsubscribe handling

Trigger: event: new market or new form. Human role: CRM manager / compliance owner for messaging. Capabilities: LIFE-C03. Needs: brand brain: markets sold into, current consent records in ESP/WhatsApp/SMS platforms, per-market law reference.

1. **List every market the brand sends into.** Reads shipping/billing markets from order data to know which consent laws apply (opt-in default in some markets, opt-out in others). Skill: `life/consent-governance`. Output: market list. Check: market list matches actual order geography, not assumed markets.
2. **Pull the consent rule per market.** Looks up the opt-in model, record-keeping requirement and quiet-hours window for each market's SMS/WhatsApp/email law. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: consent rule per market with source and date. Check: every rule carries a URL and date.
3. **Check current consent capture against the rule.** Reads the ESP/SMS/WhatsApp consent fields captured today and compares to what each market requires. Skill: `life/consent-governance`. Output: gap list per market. Check: gap list names the exact missing field or timestamp.
4. **Set the quiet-hours schedule per channel and market.** Sets the local-time send window for SMS, WhatsApp and push per market, since a message outside quiet hours can itself be a violation. Skill: `life/consent-governance`. Output: quiet-hours table. Check: table uses local time per market, not the brand's home timezone.
5. **Write the unsubscribe and preference-center rule.** Sets how fast an unsubscribe or STOP reply is honoured, and whether it applies per channel or brand-wide. Skill: `life/consent-governance`. Output: unsubscribe SLA and scope rule. Check: SLA meets or beats the strictest market's legal minimum.
6. **Draft the founder card for any gap needing a system change.** Where a gap needs a form field or platform setting change, drafts the card; where it is only a policy text update, drafts the text. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card or policy text. Check: no discount or claim invented; the ask is one setting or one field.
7. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; any consent-capture or unsubscribe change is founder_gate since it affects legal standing. Skill: `none`. Output: gated card. Check: every claim about a law cites its source and date.

Outputs: consent rule set per market, quiet-hours table, unsubscribe SLA. QA: every legal claim cites a URL and date; quiet hours in local time; unsubscribe honoured at or above the legal minimum. Read point: checked again at the next new market or a platform TCPA/DPDP-style rule change. Duration: 3 hours agent time, no waiting unless a form change is needed.

### LIFE-W04 Build a lifecycle flow, welcome through sunset

Trigger: on demand. Human role: lifecycle/retention marketing manager building or rebuilding one flow. Capabilities: LIFE-C04, LIFE-C05, LIFE-C06, LIFE-C07, LIFE-C08, LIFE-C09, LIFE-C10, LIFE-C11. Needs: approved idea record naming the flow type, brand brain: products, voice, claims tiers, offer/discount policy, order and event history for the trigger, registry: past builds of this flow.

1. **Read the idea and confirm the flow type.** Identifies which of the 8 flow types (welcome, browse abandon, cart/checkout abandon, post-purchase, win-back, sunset, replenishment/subscription, loyalty-VIP, back-in-stock) the approved idea or maintenance job calls for. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: flow type confirmed. Check: flow type matches one of the 8 named types, or the run stops to ask.
2. **Select the flow-type sub-skill.** Loads the matching life/build-flow/<type> sub-skill for the type confirmed in step 1. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: sub-skill loaded. Check: the sub-skill loaded matches the flow type exactly.
3. **Confirm the trigger event actually fires.** Checks the underlying event (signup, product view without add-to-cart, cart/checkout start, order placed, no purchase in N days, no engagement in N days, subscription charge date, restock webhook) exists and fires reliably in the store's data. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: confirmed trigger with a sample of recent fires. Check: trigger fired at least once in the last 30 days, or the build stops with the gap named. Ask the founder if: the trigger event does not exist in this store's data.
4. **Set entry, exit and re-entry filters.** Sets who enters, what removes them early (purchased, unsubscribed, already in a conflicting flow), and whether and when they can re-enter. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: filter set. Check: no two flows can claim the same person at the same lifecycle stage at once.
5. **Map the step-by-step sequence.** Lays out each message's delay, channel and purpose (welcome/education/social proof/urgency/discount), timed to the flow type's own logic (72 hours front-loaded for welcome, purchase-cadence for win-back, use-up cycle for replenishment). Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: step list with delay, channel, purpose. Check: delay and channel match the sub-skill's timing rule for this flow type.
6. **Set the discount eligibility rule, if any.** Decides whether and where a discount appears in the sequence and checks it against the brand's discount policy and any founder red line. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: pass/fail with the rule cited. Check: fails closed and asks the founder if a red line is unclear. Ask the founder if: discount policy is missing or ambiguous for this flow type.
7. **Draft the copy for every step.** Writes subject/preview/body or SMS/WhatsApp text for each step at a skimmable, honest, on-brand level. Skill: `life/message`. Output: draft copy per step, per channel. Check: claim-check pass on every step before it is placed in the brief.
8. **Run claim-check on the full draft set.** Checks every claim in every step's copy against the claims tiers and forbidden-words list. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail per step with the cited rule. Check: fails closed on any unverifiable claim.
9. **Set the success metric and read point.** Names the metric contract field (flow revenue per recipient, conversion rate, win-back rate) and the read window this flow will be judged on. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: metric and read window. Check: metric is a contract field, not invented.
10. **Assemble the flow brief.** Puts trigger, filters, step list, discount rule, copy references and success metric into the fixed flow-brief output-contract shape. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: flow brief JSON. Check: matches the output-contract shape exactly.
11. **Self-QA against the LIFE checklist.** Runs the full self-QA checklist before the QA Gate sees it. Skill: `none`. Output: QA-passed brief. Check: every item on the checklist passes or the run stops.
12. **Route to the founder gate.** Builds the plain-English card and sends the brief for the founder's decision (agent builds it live, sends to a developer/ESP admin, or not now). Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: the card names what is needed first if anything is missing.
13. **Log the registry entry.** Records the flow build against the idea id with its metric, read point and kill/scale rule. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: metric and read point match what step 9 set.

Outputs: flow brief (output-contract shape), founder card, registry entry. QA: every claim traces to a brand-record field or cited idea; no two flows share a customer stage; discount eligibility cleared discount-policy-check; trigger confirmed to actually fire before the build. Read point: flow-level revenue per recipient and the named conversion/win-back rate, read at the flow type's own natural cycle (72 hours for welcome, 30-60 days for win-back). Duration: 3 to 5 hours agent time per flow; founder decision then ESP build time.

### LIFE-W05 Build and maintain the segmentation model

Trigger: weekly. Human role: CRM manager building the segments every flow and campaign draws from. Capabilities: LIFE-C12. Needs: order/ESP event data, metric contract: RFM and engagement fields, current segment list and refresh log.

1. **Read the current segment list.** Pulls every live segment (RFM tiers, buyer stage, VIP, win-back-potential, engaged audience, location) and its last refresh time. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: segment inventory. Check: every segment in the standard set has a row.
2. **Pull order and engagement data for scoring.** Reads order recency/frequency/monetary and email/SMS engagement for the scoring window. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: scoring dataset. Check: window ends on the last full day.
3. **Compute RFM tiers and buyer stage in code.** Scores every customer into an RFM tier and a buyer-stage bucket (prospect, first-time, repeat, VIP, lapsing, lapsed) using the fixed scoring script, never estimated by the model. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: customer-to-tier/stage table. Check: arithmetic and thresholds run in code, not the model.
4. **Score win-back-potential and high-potential segments.** Flags customers showing decay against their own past cadence (win-back-potential) and customers matching high-LTV traits early (high-potential). Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: two additional scored lists. Check: decay is measured against the customer's own cadence, not a flat day count.
5. **Build the location segment.** Groups customers by shipping geography for market-specific sends (language, currency, festival calendar for India). Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: location segment. Check: geography read from shipping address, not billing.
6. **Check every segment against its using flows/campaigns.** Confirms each segment definition still matches what the flows and campaigns that reference it expect. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: used-by cross-check. Check: no segment is referenced by a flow/campaign that no longer exists, and vice versa.
7. **Write the segment definitions.** Documents each segment's logic (AND/OR conditions, cited fields), expected size and refresh cadence in the output-contract shape. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: segment definition set. Check: matches the fixed output-contract shape.
8. **Push refreshed segments to the ESP.** Where autonomy allows (agent_does), writes the refreshed segment membership to the ESP; a push to an ad platform for lookalikes is a hand-off to Media buyer, never done here. Skill: `life/segment-spec`. Output: refreshed segments live in the ESP. Check: no write to any ad account from this step.
9. **Report what changed.** Notes any segment whose size moved more than a set threshold since last week, for the founder to see without a gate. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: weekly segment change note. Check: size changes stated as numbers, not adjectives.

Outputs: segment definitions (output-contract shape), refreshed segment membership in the ESP. QA: every segment logic cites real fields; arithmetic run in code; used-by cross-check passes. Read point: weekly size and composition check; flows/campaigns reading the segment measure their own results separately. Duration: 1.5 hours agent time, automated after setup.

### LIFE-W06 Build the campaign calendar and send-ready draft

Trigger: weekly. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager planning and drafting the week's campaigns. Capabilities: LIFE-C13, LIFE-C14, LIFE-C15. Needs: metric contract: campaign send history and results, product/launch calendar, segmentation model, brand voice and claims tiers.

1. **Read last week's calendar and results.** Pulls what sent last week, to which segment, and its result from the metric contract. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: last week's send log with results. Check: results shown as unavailable if the window is incomplete, never 0.
2. **Pull the product and event calendar.** Reads upcoming launches, restocks, and India-relevant dates (festivals, sale events) that could anchor a send. Skill: `life/campaign-brief`. Output: event list for the coming weeks. Check: every festival/date claim is checked against this year's actual calendar, not assumed.
3. **Plan the two-layer calendar.** Plans the always-on baseline (value content, non-discount) and any event-based peak send, keeping content mostly non-discount and rotating format (story, UGC, education, offer) across the week. Skill: `life/campaign-brief`. Output: two-layer weekly plan. Check: no two sends in the week target the same segment with the same format.
4. **Pick the segment and content framework per send.** Assigns each planned send to a segment from the segmentation model and a content framework (problem-solution, social proof, behind-the-scenes, offer). Skill: `life/campaign-brief`. Output: send-to-segment-to-framework map. Check: segment picked exists and is fresh (refreshed this week).
5. **Decide discount or not, per send.** Checks each send against the discount policy before any discount is attached. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: pass/fail per send with the rule cited. Check: fails closed if the discount frequency cap is already used this month. Ask the founder if: monthly discount frequency cap already reached.
6. **Draft the copy per channel.** Writes subject/preview/body for email and short-form text for SMS/WhatsApp per send, at a skimmable reading level. Skill: `life/message`. Output: draft copy per send, per channel. Check: claim-check pass before the copy moves to email build.
7. **Run claim-check on all campaign drafts.** Checks every campaign draft's claims against the tiers before build. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail with cited rule. Check: fails closed on any unverifiable claim.
8. **Build and QA the email.** Turns approved copy into the 600px-wide, mobile-legible email build with live text over locked images, one decision per section, compressed and alt-tagged images. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: built email plus pre-send checklist. Check: images compressed and alt-tagged; only one clear decision per section.
9. **Assemble the campaign brief.** Puts send window, segment, framework, discount decision and copy references into the fixed campaign-brief output-contract shape. Skill: `life/campaign-brief`. Output: campaign brief set for the week. Check: matches the output-contract shape exactly.
10. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; the calendar and any discount send are founder_gate, non-discount value sends can be agent_does once the founder has approved the standing calendar pattern. Skill: `none`. Output: gated or approved briefs. Check: no external send happens from this workflow; ESP holds drafts only.

Outputs: weekly campaign calendar, send-ready campaign briefs and built emails. QA: content mostly non-discount; no two sends target the same segment with the same format in a week; every send-ready email passes the pre-send checklist. Read point: per-send revenue and click rate read the day after send; weekly total read against last week. Duration: 3 hours agent time per week; founder review then ESP scheduling.

### LIFE-W07 Run the deliverability and list-hygiene program

Trigger: weekly. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager / email deliverability owner. Capabilities: LIFE-C16. Needs: ESP sender reputation data, metric contract: open/bounce/spam-complaint rates, list growth and suppression history.

1. **Pull sender reputation and placement data.** Reads domain/IP reputation, inbox-vs-spam placement where the ESP reports it, and bounce/complaint rates for the week. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: reputation snapshot. Check: figures are the ESP's own reported numbers, not estimated.
2. **Check list hygiene.** Reads the share of the list that is unengaged (no open in 90-180 days) and whether a sunset flow is removing them. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: list hygiene figure. Check: unengaged share computed from the contract, never guessed.
3. **Compare against the brand's own baseline.** Checks this week's bounce, complaint and open figures against the brand's trailing 8-week baseline, not an industry number. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: baseline comparison. Check: comparison uses the brand's own history first.
4. **Diagnose degradation if any is found.** If a figure has degraded, checks content quality (subject lines, spam-trigger words, image-to-text ratio) as the first fix before touching authentication or sending infrastructure. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: diagnosis with the suspected cause ranked first. Check: content is checked before infrastructure, in that order.
5. **Run the repair ladder if needed.** Applies the repair steps in order: fix content, pause sending to the most unengaged segment, re-permission or suppress chronic non-openers, check authentication (SPF/DKIM/DMARC) only if the first steps do not explain it. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: repair plan with steps taken and steps pending. Check: authentication is checked last, not first, unless a clear signal points there.
6. **Suppress or resend to unengaged segments per policy.** Where agent_does, updates suppression per the standing hygiene policy; anything beyond the standing policy (a full re-permission campaign) goes to the founder. Skill: `life/deliverability-audit`. Output: updated suppression list. Check: no suppression removes a customer who purchased in the last 90 days without a specific reason.
7. **Write the health report.** Reports the reputation snapshot, the baseline comparison and any repair-ladder action taken. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: weekly deliverability report. Check: numbers from the contract only, with windows and freshness.

Outputs: deliverability health report, repair-ladder log, updated suppression list. QA: content checked before infrastructure; no recent purchaser suppressed without cause; figures compared to the brand's own baseline. Read point: weekly; a sharp drop triggers an off-cycle run the same day it is seen. Duration: 1 hour agent time weekly; more if the repair ladder runs.

### LIFE-W08 Set channel policy and design conversational messaging

Trigger: monthly. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager setting SMS/WhatsApp/push rules and two-way flows. Capabilities: LIFE-C17, LIFE-C18. Needs: channel cost data (per-SMS/WhatsApp-message cost), consent state per channel, current channel mix and results.

1. **Read current channel usage and cost.** Pulls send volume and per-message cost for SMS, WhatsApp and push over the last month. Skill: `life/channel-policy`. Output: cost-and-volume table. Check: cost figures come from the platform's own billing, not estimated.
2. **Check consent coverage per channel.** Confirms what share of the sendable list has valid consent for SMS, WhatsApp and push separately. Skill: `life/consent-governance`. Output: consent coverage by channel. Check: a channel below its market's legal consent threshold is flagged before any policy is set.
3. **Set content scope per channel.** Decides what content each channel carries (WhatsApp: order updates and high-value recovery; SMS: time-sensitive short offers; push: on-site nudges only) so channels do not duplicate the same message. Skill: `life/channel-policy`. Output: content-scope rule per channel. Check: no message type is duplicated across two channels to the same customer at the same time.
4. **Set cadence and cost-control caps.** Sets a per-customer weekly cap per channel and a monthly spend cap for paid channels (SMS, WhatsApp), sized to the cost data. Skill: `life/channel-policy`. Output: cadence and cap rule. Check: caps sized from real per-message cost, not an arbitrary number.
5. **Recommend WhatsApp as a channel swap where SMS is costly.** Where SMS cost per send is high relative to WhatsApp in the brand's market (common in India), proposes shifting eligible message types to WhatsApp. Skill: `life/channel-policy`. Output: channel-swap recommendation. Check: swap recommended only where WhatsApp consent and template approval already exist or are proposed together.
6. **Design the two-way conversation openers.** Writes the reply-inviting opening question for SMS/RCS/WhatsApp conversational flows (delivery check-in, product-fit question, feedback prompt). Skill: `life/message`. Output: opener set per use case. Check: every opener invites a real reply, not a yes/no dead end.
7. **Set the human hand-off rule.** Defines the point at which a real customer reply routes to a human agent rather than a bot continuing the script. Skill: `life/message`. Output: hand-off rule. Check: hand-off happens at the first real reply, never after a second bot message.
8. **Assemble the channel policy and conversation spec.** Puts content scope, cadence, cost caps, swap recommendation and conversation openers into one policy document. Skill: `life/channel-policy`. Output: channel policy document. Check: document covers every channel the brand actually uses.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; channel policy changes and any new conversational flow are founder_gate. Skill: `none`. Output: gated policy document. Check: no send action taken from this workflow.

Outputs: channel policy document, conversation opener and hand-off spec. QA: caps sized from real cost data; consent checked before any policy is set; hand-off happens at first real reply. Read point: monthly cost-per-outcome by channel, and reply/hand-off rate for conversational flows. Duration: 3 hours agent time monthly.

### LIFE-W09 Design the WhatsApp commerce program for India

Trigger: on demand. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager building the India WhatsApp channel. Capabilities: LIFE-C22. Needs: order/cart data for Indian buyers, courier NDR/RTO data, current COD share and RTO rate, WhatsApp Business API template status.

1. **Read the current India order pattern.** Pulls COD share, average RTO rate and cart abandonment for Indian orders specifically, not blended with other markets. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: India order/RTO snapshot. Check: figures scoped to Indian shipping addresses only.
2. **Check WhatsApp reach against SMS and email for this base.** Compares delivered/read rates where available, since WhatsApp typically reaches Indian buyers better than SMS or email. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: channel-reach comparison. Check: comparison uses the brand's own delivered/read data, not a general claim.
3. **Design the WhatsApp cart-recovery sequence.** Builds the cart/checkout abandonment sequence for WhatsApp specifically, using the life/build-flow/browse-cart-abandon logic with WhatsApp as the lead channel. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: WhatsApp cart-recovery flow steps. Check: sequence follows the same filter and timing rules as the cart-abandon flow build.
4. **Design order-update messages.** Sets the order-confirmed, shipped, out-for-delivery and delivered messages on WhatsApp, replacing or supplementing SMS for these transactional updates. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: order-update message set. Check: every update message maps to a real order-status event.
5. **Design the COD-to-prepaid nudge.** Writes the message offering a small, policy-checked incentive to switch a COD order to prepaid, sized to the RTO cost it avoids rather than an arbitrary number. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: COD-to-prepaid nudge with sizing shown. Check: incentive size traces to the measured RTO cost avoided.
6. **Design the NDR recovery sequence.** Fires an automatic WhatsApp sequence the moment a courier logs a non-delivery, offering reschedule or address fix. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: NDR recovery sequence. Check: sequence starts within the same day as the NDR event.
7. **Check template approval status.** Confirms every message type needing a WhatsApp Business template is approved before the flow can go live. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: template approval checklist. Check: no flow step ships on an unapproved template.
8. **Assemble the program spec.** Puts the cart-recovery, order-update, COD-to-prepaid and NDR flows into one WhatsApp commerce program document. Skill: `life/whatsapp-india-commerce`. Output: WhatsApp commerce program spec. Check: every flow in the spec traces to a real event and a checked template.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; every flow here is founder_gate before it goes live. Skill: `none`. Output: gated program spec. Check: no message is sent from this workflow.

Outputs: WhatsApp commerce program spec, template approval checklist. QA: incentive sizing traces to measured RTO cost; no unapproved template used; NDR sequence starts same-day. Read point: COD confirmation rate, RTO rate by pincode, and NDR recovery rate, read monthly against the pre-launch baseline. Duration: 1 day agent time; template approval wait time varies by Meta's review.

### LIFE-W10 Run the lifecycle testing and experimentation program

Trigger: on demand. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager running the test backlog. Capabilities: LIFE-C19. Needs: registry: running and past lifecycle tests, current flow/campaign/channel backlog, metric contract.

1. **Pull the current test backlog.** Reads every proposed flow, campaign, offer or channel test not yet run for this brand. Skill: `life/test-program-governance`. Output: backlog list. Check: backlog matches what Propose and the other LIFE workflows have queued.
2. **Check for test hygiene conflicts.** Confirms no two running tests overlap on the same segment and metric at the same time, which would confound the read. Skill: `life/test-program-governance`. Output: conflict check. Check: a conflict blocks a new test from starting until resolved.
3. **Prioritise the next tests to run.** Ranks the backlog by expected impact, sample size available and dependency order (structure before depth, as offers do). Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked test order. Check: ranking runs in code with score parts visible.
4. **Specify each test.** Writes the hypothesis, control and variant, sample split, minimum sample size and pre-registered read window and metric before launch. Skill: `life/test-program-governance`. Output: test specification per test. Check: metric and read window are pre-registered before launch, never chosen after seeing results.
5. **Set the holdout where relevant.** For a flow or campaign test measuring incrementality, defines the holdout group size and duration. Skill: `life/test-program-governance`. Output: holdout definition. Check: holdout is large enough to read cleanly, sized in code.
6. **Hand off the specs to the owning workflow.** Routes each test's build (flow, campaign, offer, channel) to its own workflow (W04, W06, W18, etc.) with the test spec attached. Skill: `none`. Output: routed test specs. Check: every spec reaches the workflow that will actually build the variant.
7. **Log each test to the registry.** Records id, hypothesis, metric, read point, kill/scale rule before the test goes live. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entries. Check: every entry has a metric and a read point that is a contract field.

Outputs: ranked test backlog, test specifications, registry entries. QA: no two running tests confound each other; metric and read window pre-registered before launch; ranking done in code. Read point: per-test, at the pre-registered read window. Duration: 2 hours agent time per prioritisation pass.

### LIFE-W11 Run the monthly lifecycle health review

Trigger: monthly. Human role: lifecycle marketing manager producing the monthly read-out. Capabilities: LIFE-C20. Needs: metric contract: full lifecycle metric chain, registry: this month's tests and their reads, flow/campaign send logs.

1. **Read the full metric chain.** Pulls list growth, opt-in rate, flow revenue share, campaign revenue share, deliverability, and retention/repeat-rate figures for the month. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: full metric chain with windows. Check: every field is a contract field; missing ones say unavailable.
2. **Separate traffic issues from flow issues.** Checks whether a flow's weak result is caused by low volume entering it (a traffic/segmentation problem) versus a weak flow itself, before diagnosing the flow. Skill: `life/health-review`. Output: traffic-vs-flow diagnosis per weak flow. Check: a volume problem is never mis-diagnosed as a content problem.
3. **Pull this month's test reads.** Reads every registry entry with a read point inside this month. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: test read list. Check: pending reads listed as pending, never as results.
4. **Diagnose the top 2-3 problems.** For the weakest points in the metric chain, states the specific cause with evidence, not a general observation. Skill: `life/health-review`. Output: diagnosis with evidence per problem. Check: every diagnosis names the exact setting, segment or content issue.
5. **Recommend the next 2-3 actions.** Names the specific next workflow to run (a flow rebuild, a segment fix, a deliverability repair) for each diagnosed problem. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: recommended next actions, ranked. Check: each recommendation names the workflow id that would execute it.
6. **Assemble the monthly report.** Puts the metric chain, diagnoses and recommendations into the report-section shape (what we did, what it did, what is next). Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: monthly lifecycle report. Check: matches the report-section output shape.
7. **Self-QA and post to the founder.** Runs the QA checklist; this is agent_does since it is a report, not a change. Skill: `none`. Output: final report. Check: no adjective substitutes for a number.

Outputs: monthly lifecycle health report. QA: traffic issues separated from flow issues before diagnosing; pending reads never shown as results; every recommendation names an executing workflow. Read point: monthly, compared to the prior month's same report. Duration: 2 hours agent time monthly.

### LIFE-W12 Score churn and lapse risk and route to the right save program

Trigger: weekly. Human role: retention marketing manager reading the order gap and routing saves. Capabilities: LIFE-C33, LIFE-G02. Needs: order history with per-customer purchase interval, subscription pause/failed-renewal events, unresolved support ticket data, engagement data from the segmentation model.

1. **Compute each customer's expected next-order gap.** Reads each customer's own historical purchase interval and flags anyone now past their expected next-order date by a set margin. Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: per-customer order-gap flag. Check: gap measured against the customer's own cadence, never a flat day count for everyone.
2. **Pull subscription pause and failed-renewal events.** Reads any subscription pause, skip or failed-payment event in the window. Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: subscription risk events. Check: failed renewals separated from voluntary cancellations.
3. **Set retry schedule and re-authorisation for failed renewals.** For a failed UPI AutoPay or e-mandate renewal, sets the retry cadence and the re-authorisation message per RBI e-mandate rules. Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: retry/re-auth schedule. Check: retry timing and re-auth wording match the current RBI e-mandate rule, cited with date.
4. **Pull unresolved support and engagement decay signals.** Reads open or recently-escalated tickets and any drop in email/SMS engagement for the same customers. Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: support/engagement signal set. Check: a signal only counts if it is dated within the scoring window.
5. **Compute the churn-risk score in code.** Combines order-gap, subscription risk, support and engagement signals into a risk tier (low/medium/high) per customer, all arithmetic in code. Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: risk-tiered customer list. Check: no risk score computed or adjusted by the model itself.
6. **Route each risk tier to its save program.** Routes low/medium engagement-decay risk to the ordinary win-back flow (W04), RTO-caused risk to the trust-rebuild win-back variant, and active-subscriber risk to the cancel-flow save offer (W20). Skill: `life/churn-risk-score`. Output: routing table by tier and cause. Check: an RTO-caused lapse never routes to a discount-led win-back first.
7. **Draft the routed message as a live draft.** For each routed customer or segment, produces the flow entry or one-off draft ready for the founder gate, citing the evidence behind the routing. Skill: `life/build-flow`. Output: routed live draft per tier. Check: every draft cites the specific signal that triggered it.
8. **Report involuntary churn apart from voluntary cancellations.** Reports the failed-renewal recovery rate separately from the voluntary cancel rate, since blending them hides which lever actually needs fixing. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: churn report with the two rates kept apart. Check: the two rates are never combined into one blended churn number.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; every routed save action is founder_gate before it sends. Skill: `none`. Output: gated drafts. Check: no message sent from this workflow.

Outputs: risk-tiered customer list, routed live drafts, involuntary-vs-voluntary churn report. QA: order gap measured against the customer's own cadence; arithmetic in code only; RTO-caused lapse routed to a trust-rebuild message, not a discount. Read point: weekly risk-tier movement; monthly involuntary-churn rate against the prior month. Duration: 2 hours agent time weekly.

### LIFE-W13 Design the post-purchase journey, delivery communication and unboxing experience

Trigger: on demand. Human role: CX lead mapping and designing the after-purchase experience. Capabilities: LIFE-C23, LIFE-C24, LIFE-C25. Needs: support ticket history, review text, metric contract: WISMO ticket rate, product/packaging cost data, brand's one-line promise.

1. **List the post-purchase touchpoints.** Lists every touchpoint from order confirmation to delivered-and-used (confirmation, shipping update, delivery, unboxing, first use, review request). Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: touchpoint list. Check: list covers every real touchpoint the store has, not a generic template.
2. **Classify each touchpoint by the Kano model.** Classifies each as basic (must-have, its absence angers), performance (more is better) or delight (unexpected, memorable). Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: Kano-classified touchpoints. Check: every touchpoint has a classification with the reasoning.
3. **Find the negative peak.** Reads support tickets and reviews to find the single worst-experienced point in the journey today, with quoted evidence. Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: negative peak with quotes. Check: the peak is evidenced by real quotes, not assumed.
4. **Design the order-status and delivery communication set.** Writes the confirmation, shipping and delivery-estimate messages and the branded tracking page content, aimed at cutting WISMO tickets under 5 percent of orders. Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: order-status message set and tracking-page copy. Check: delivery-estimate language never promises a date the courier data cannot support.
5. **Design the unboxing and packaging reveal.** Designs the layered reveal, insert tiering, and any gift or note, naming this as the journey's main peak to fund, capped to a set share of product cost. Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: unboxing/packaging brief with cost model. Check: spend capped at 2-5 percent of product cost; no falsely-claimed handwritten note.
6. **Reference competitor unboxing for mood only.** Pulls competitor unboxing references from the Reference Scout for layout and mood, never copying materials or wording. Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: reference set. Check: no competitor text, logo or product reproduced.
7. **Name the change each map requires.** States at least one concrete change per touchpoint map, not just a description of the current state. Skill: `life/journey-map`. Output: named changes list. Check: every map names at least one concrete change.
8. **Assemble the journey and communication brief.** Puts the touchpoint map, delivery communication set and packaging brief into one document. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: journey/communication/packaging brief. Check: brief covers order-to-delivered, not just one touchpoint.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; packaging spend and message wording changes are founder_gate. Skill: `none`. Output: gated brief. Check: no vendor or packaging order placed from this workflow.

Outputs: journey map with Kano classification, order-status/delivery message set, packaging and unboxing brief. QA: negative peak evidenced by quotes; delivery estimates match courier data; packaging spend capped to product cost share. Read point: WISMO ticket rate and unboxing-driven UGC volume, read monthly after launch. Duration: 1 day agent time; longer if new packaging is sourced.

### LIFE-W14 Design the returns, exchanges and refund experience

Trigger: on demand. Human role: returns coordinator / CX lead designing the returns flow and policy. Capabilities: LIFE-C26. Needs: current return/refund policy, RTO/fraud/chargeback data, per-market return law, support ticket volume tagged as returns.

1. **Read the current policy and return volume.** Pulls the live returns policy text and the last quarter's return rate, reason breakdown and refund-cycle time. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: current-state snapshot. Check: return rate and reasons come from order/support data, not estimated.
2. **Check per-market return law.** Confirms the minimum return window and any mandatory disclosure for each market the brand ships to. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: legal minimums per market with source and date. Check: every legal claim carries a URL and date.
3. **Set refund speed targets for both legs.** Sets a target for time-to-pickup and time-to-refund-after-receipt separately, since a returner cares about both. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: two speed targets. Check: targets are measured against both legs, not one combined number.
4. **Design the refund-vs-credit choice.** Designs the choice between cash refund and store credit, including any credit bonus, checked against the brand's margin. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: refund/credit choice design. Check: any credit bonus is sized against real margin, not an arbitrary number.
5. **Design the self-service portal rules.** Sets what a customer can do without contacting support (initiate, choose refund/exchange/credit, print a label) and what needs a human. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: self-service rule set. Check: fraud-flagged or high-value returns route to a human, never full self-service.
6. **Design RTO and fraud controls for COD markets.** Sets the pincode-level RTO risk check and the serial-returner flag for COD orders before a return or replacement ships. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: RTO/fraud control rule. Check: risk check runs on real pincode history, not a blanket COD restriction.
7. **Write the policy text.** Drafts the customer-facing returns and refund policy page text at a skimmable, honest reading level. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: policy draft text. Check: claim-check pass; policy visible pre-purchase, not only after checkout.
8. **Check the returners' repeat-rate gate.** Confirms any investment in making returns easier is justified by the observed repeat-purchase rate of past returners versus non-returners. Skill: `life/returns-policy-draft`. Output: repeat-rate comparison. Check: investment level matches the measured repeat-rate lift, not assumed goodwill.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; every policy or portal change is founder_gate and legal-market-checked. Skill: `none`. Output: gated policy and flow spec. Check: policy text checked against law per market before it ships.

Outputs: returns flow spec, policy draft text, RTO/fraud control rule. QA: policy checked against law per market; returns investment gated on returners' repeat rate; RTO check uses real pincode history. Read point: return rate, refund-cycle time and post-return repeat-purchase rate, read quarterly. Duration: 1 day agent time.

### LIFE-W15 Draft support reply templates, ticket-tagging rules and AI agent governance

Trigger: on demand. Human role: customer support lead / CX manager standing up or refreshing support automation. Capabilities: LIFE-C27, LIFE-C49. Needs: helpdesk ticket history by contact type, current return/refund/shipping policy, brand voice record.
