1. **Group tickets into contact types.** Reads a sample of recent tickets and groups them into the common contact types (WISMO, return/exchange, sizing/fit, damaged/defective, payment, cancel/change order, product question, complaint). Skill: `life/support-reply-draft`. Output: contact-type breakdown with volume. Check: every high-volume type in the sample has its own bucket, not an 'other' catch-all over 15 percent.
2. **Draft a reply template per type.** Writes a template per contact type in the brand's voice, citing current policy for any factual claim. Skill: `life/support-reply-draft`. Output: template library. Check: every factual claim in a template traces to the current policy.
3. **Decide automation scope per type.** Classifies each type as agent-can-close, agent-can-draft-only, or human-only, keeping human voice for recovery and VIP moments. Skill: `life/support-reply-draft`. Output: automation-scope classification. Check: recovery and VIP-flagged tickets are never agent-can-close.
4. **Set tagging and routing rules.** Writes the rule that tags and routes each incoming ticket to the right queue and priority. Skill: `life/support-reply-draft`. Output: tagging/routing rule set. Check: every contact type maps to exactly one primary tag.
5. **Write the AI agent's operating procedure.** For any contact type marked agent-can-close involving money or account changes, writes the exact rule (refund cap, who approves above it, which order states allow a refund) and the identity-verification step required first. Skill: `life/ai-agent-governance`. Output: operating-procedure document. Check: every money-moving rule states a hard cap and an approval path above it.
6. **Set escalation triggers.** Defines the signals that force escalation to a human: negative sentiment, repeated back-and-forth, an explicit request for a person, or ticket value above a threshold. Skill: `life/ai-agent-governance`. Output: escalation rule set. Check: escalation carries the full conversation history to the human, never a cold restart.
7. **Replay the agent against real past tickets.** Tests the drafted operating procedure and templates against a sample of last quarter's real tickets, scoring accuracy and tone before anything goes live. Skill: `life/ai-agent-governance`. Output: replay test report with scores. Check: replay uses real past tickets, not synthetic examples.
8. **Set the kill switch and rollback plan.** Defines how the AI agent is paused and what reverts if it starts producing wrong answers. Skill: `life/ai-agent-governance`. Output: kill-switch and rollback plan. Check: rollback restores the prior state in one step.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; the operating procedure and any agent-can-close scope are founder_gate before go-live and after every policy change. Skill: `none`. Output: gated governance package. Check: no AI agent goes live or stays live after a policy change without a fresh replay test.

Outputs: reply template library, tagging/routing rules, AI agent operating-procedure document, replay test report. QA: every claim traces to current policy; recovery/VIP tickets stay human; money-moving actions have a hard cap and approval path; replay tested before go-live and after every policy change. Read point: close rate, reopen rate and CSAT by contact type, read monthly; replay score re-checked after every policy change. Duration: 1 day agent time for initial setup; 2 hours for a policy-change re-test.

### LIFE-W16 Run the review collection, response and syndication program

Trigger: event: delivery confirmed / weekly. Human role: review platform manager / retention marketing manager. Capabilities: LIFE-C28, LIFE-C52. Needs: reviews platform data, delivery date data, brand voice, product feed, marketplace listing access if any.

1. **Set the review request timing.** Sets the request to fire about 3 days post-delivery, or hands timing to the reviews app if it already optimises this per product. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: timing rule. Check: timing based on delivery date, not order date.
2. **Draft honest, unfiltered request copy.** Writes the review request at a skimmable, non-pushy reading level, never implying a reward for a positive review on a destination that forbids incentives. Skill: `life/message`. Output: request copy per channel. Check: no incentive wording where the destination forbids it.
3. **Check incentive rules per destination.** Confirms which review destinations in this request may be incentivised with loyalty points (own site, usually yes) and which may not (Google, most marketplaces, no). Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: per-destination incentive rule. Check: a marketplace or Google-bound ask never mentions a reward.
4. **Draft responses to new reviews.** Writes an honest, unfiltered response to each new review, especially negative ones, never a templated denial. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: draft responses. Check: no manufactured or edited-sounding response.
5. **Flag defects back to the brand record.** Routes any review that reveals a real product or fulfilment defect to the brand brain, not just a canned reply. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: defect-flag list. Check: a defect flag names the specific product/fulfilment issue, not a general complaint.
6. **Rank reviews and UGC for syndication.** Ranks on-site reviews and photo/video UGC by usefulness (photo attached, specific detail, recency), not star rating alone. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: ranked review/UGC set. Check: ranking uses the stated criteria, not star rating alone.
7. **Select feed and ad-copy candidates.** Selects rating/review fields for the product feed and pulls top review quotes as ad copy candidates for the paid-creative specialist, noting the permission basis. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: feed fields and review-quote bank. Check: quotes are selected and cited, never rewritten.
8. **Push top reviews into marketplace listings where allowed.** Where a marketplace allows imported reviews, prepares the content for that listing. Skill: `life/review-program`. Output: marketplace-ready review content. Check: only pushed where the marketplace's own terms allow it.
9. **Self-QA and route.** Runs the QA checklist; response drafts and any listing/feed write are founder_gate until reviewed. Skill: `none`. Output: gated program outputs. Check: no review is edited, only selected and quoted.

Outputs: review request copy and timing rule, draft review responses, defect-flag list, feed-ready fields and review-quote bank. QA: no incentive wording where forbidden; no manufactured response; reviews selected and quoted, never rewritten. Read point: review volume, average rating trend and syndicated-quote usage in ads/feed, read quarterly. Duration: 3 hours agent time weekly; less once the timing rule is set.

### LIFE-W17 Run VoC surveys and score CX maturity

Trigger: quarterly. Human role: CX manager running the standing NPS/CSAT read and the maturity check. Capabilities: LIFE-C29, LIFE-C30. Needs: existing NPS/CSAT data, support ticket open-text, review text, staff input.

1. **Start from owned data before surveying.** Pools open-text answers from closed support tickets and reviews for the quarter before writing a single new survey question. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: pooled open-text set. Check: owned data reviewed before any new survey goes out.
2. **Cluster into recurring themes.** Clusters the pooled text into named themes with example quotes and a count per theme, flagging any theme new this quarter or growing. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: theme-clustered report with quotes and counts. Check: every theme has at least one quoted example.
3. **Run or refresh the standing NPS/CSAT survey.** Sends or checks the standing NPS/CSAT read for the quarter, keeping the same question wording as prior quarters for comparability. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: NPS/CSAT score with trend. Check: wording unchanged from the prior quarter unless a specific reason is logged.
4. **Add staff input.** Pulls a short structured input from support/CX staff on what they hear most that a survey or ticket text might miss. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: staff input summary. Check: staff input is dated and attributed to a role, not anonymous colour.
5. **Score CX maturity.** Scores the brand's CX approach as random, intentional or differentiated against a written standard, citing the evidence for the score. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: CX maturity score with evidence. Check: score cites specific evidence, not a general impression.
6. **Check new customer-facing tools against the standard.** Reviews any customer-facing tool launched this quarter (chatbot, portal, new flow) against the written experience standard. Skill: `life/voc-cx-governance`. Output: tool compliance check. Check: every new tool is checked, none launched without a check on record.
7. **Route confirmed themes to the owning specialist.** Sends a confirmed recurring theme to the relevant workflow (returns, support templates, product) rather than letting the report sit unread. Skill: `none`. Output: routed theme list. Check: every confirmed theme names the workflow it was routed to.
8. **Assemble and post the quarterly VoC/CX report.** Puts the theme report, NPS/CSAT trend, staff input and maturity score into one report. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: quarterly VoC/CX report. Check: matches the report-section output shape.

Outputs: theme-clustered VoC report, NPS/CSAT trend, CX maturity score, tool compliance check. QA: owned data reviewed before surveying; every theme has a quoted example; no new customer-facing tool launched unchecked. Read point: quarterly, compared against the prior quarter's same report. Duration: 1 day agent time quarterly.

### LIFE-W18 Design an offer package from margin and AOV

Trigger: on demand. Human role: offers/pricing owner designing a full offer package. Capabilities: LIFE-C36, LIFE-C39, LIFE-C40, LIFE-C41, LIFE-C42. Needs: landed cost/COGS per SKU, order-value distribution, the buyer's single biggest objection, discount policy and any founder red line, refund/return cost history.

1. **Read margin, AOV and the objection map.** Pulls landed cost per SKU, the real order-value distribution (not the average) and the buyer's biggest objection from the brand record. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: margin/AOV/objection snapshot. Check: AOV distribution used, not the single average figure.
2. **Write the offer's value proposition.** Builds the offer as a full package (price, terms, proof, guarantee) scored against the value equation, built around the single biggest objection. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: value-proposition draft. Check: the offer answers the stated biggest objection, not a generic benefit.
3. **Build the margin waterfall.** Computes the SKU margin waterfall and contribution per order in code for the proposed package. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: margin waterfall. Check: all arithmetic in code; currency conversion applied where relevant.
4. **Design the bundle and quantity-ladder tiers.** Maps the product's real use journey into bundle ideas, sizes tiers with a justified reason for each, and pre-selects a middle option, checking every tier clears the contribution floor. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: bundle/ladder spec. Check: at least one full-price entry tier kept; every tier clears the contribution floor.
5. **Design the gift-with-purchase, if used.** Chooses a gift that helps the buyer use the core product, sized and placed to lift the tier the brand wants to sell, never framed dishonestly. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: GWP spec. Check: gift is not more of the same product just bought.
6. **Design upsell and cross-sell placement.** Places upsell and cross-sell offers by friction level (cart, checkout, post-purchase), anchored high with a cheaper down-sell, reviewed for margin. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: upsell/cross-sell placement spec. Check: placement reviewed against margin and against compliance rules for the platform.
7. **Cost and write the guarantee.** Computes the guarantee's net contribution cost from real refund data and writes it in the if-not-X-in-Y-we-will-Z form, activated at delivery, not order date. Skill: `life/offer-package-design`. Output: guarantee wording and cost model. Check: refund root cause checked before relying on the guarantee to fix a high refund rate.
8. **Check the whole package against discount policy.** Runs the assembled package (price, bundle, gift, guarantee) against the discount policy and any founder red line. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: pass/fail with the rule cited. Check: fails closed and asks the founder if a red line is unclear. Ask the founder if: a founder red line is unclear or missing for this package.
9. **Set the test plan.** Names the metric and read window this offer package will be judged on before it launches. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: test plan. Check: metric is a contract field, pre-registered before launch.
10. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and sends the full package to the founder for the decision. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card with the full package. Check: no revenue-only or ROAS-only justification accepted.

Outputs: offer design (output-contract shape: package, objection answered, margin waterfall, contribution, discount-policy check, test plan). QA: contribution floor cleared on every tier; guarantee cost computed from real refund data; no discount-policy or red-line conflict; test plan pre-registered before launch. Read point: take-rate by tier, contribution per order, and refund rate after guarantee launch, read at the pre-registered window. Duration: 1 to 2 days agent time.

### LIFE-W19 Govern discount policy and price integrity

Trigger: on demand. Human role: offers/pricing owner setting and enforcing the discount rulebook. Capabilities: LIFE-C38. Needs: past discount history and frequency, founder red lines from the decisions log, current reference-price practice.

1. **Read discount frequency and depth history.** Pulls how often and how deep the brand has discounted in the last 6-12 months, by channel and segment. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: discount history snapshot. Check: figures come from order/ESP data, not memory.
2. **Set frequency caps.** Sets a cap on how often a segment can be offered a discount in a period, so discounting does not train buyers to wait. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: frequency cap rule. Check: cap is a specific number and period, not 'occasionally'.
3. **Set conditionality rules.** Sets what a discount must require (minimum order value, first-time only, loyalty tier) rather than a blanket site-wide cut. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: conditionality rule set. Check: every discount type has a stated condition.
4. **Set honest framing rules.** Sets the rule for reference prices and urgency claims: a reference price must be a real prior selling price, and urgency must be real. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: framing rule set. Check: no fake reference price or fake countdown allowed by the rule.
5. **Record founder red lines.** Reads and records any hard limit the founder has already set (a floor price, a channel never discounted, a product never discounted). Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: red-line list. Check: a red line not on record is treated as unclear, never assumed.
6. **Write the policy document.** Assembles frequency caps, conditionality, framing rules and red lines into one governing document every other LIFE workflow checks against. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: discount policy document. Check: document is specific enough that a pass/fail check needs no judgement call.
7. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and sends the policy for founder confirmation before it becomes the standing rule. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: fails closed - if a rule is ambiguous, the card asks rather than guesses.

Outputs: discount policy document with frequency, conditionality, framing rules and red lines. QA: every rule is specific enough for a pass/fail check; red lines recorded, not assumed; no fake reference price or urgency allowed. Read point: checked whenever an offer or campaign workflow calls discount-policy-check; policy itself reviewed twice a year. Duration: 3 hours agent time.

### LIFE-W20 Design the subscription offer, perks and cancel flow

Trigger: on demand. Human role: CRM manager / retention marketing manager designing the subscription program. Capabilities: LIFE-C43. Needs: subscription platform data (churn, pause events), COGS, order data, cancellation reasons.

1. **Read current subscription economics.** Pulls active subscriber count, churn rate, pause rate and COGS per subscription cycle. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: subscription economics snapshot. Check: figures scoped to subscription orders only, not blended with one-off orders.
2. **Size the value proposition against a plain discount.** Compares the subscription's total value (price, perks, flexibility) against the plain discount it replaces, to confirm the reason to subscribe is stronger than a flat percentage off. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: value-proposition comparison. Check: comparison shown in real numbers, not just a claim of 'better value'.
3. **Set cadence flexibility and perk gating.** Sets how easily a subscriber can change frequency, pause or swap product, and which perks unlock at which tenure or spend. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: cadence/perk rule set. Check: perk gating tied to real use, not an arbitrary tier line.
4. **Design the four-stage cancel flow.** Builds the cancel flow as a benefit reminder, an exit-reason survey, a reason-matched alternative (pause, swap, frequency change), then a conditional, costed save offer only if earlier stages fail. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: four-stage cancel-flow design. Check: cancel is as easy to complete as sign-up; the save offer is conditional, not automatic.
5. **Cost the save offer.** Computes the save offer's cost against the subscriber's own order count and value, not one flat offer for everyone. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: costed save-offer table. Check: save offer sized in code, matched to the subscriber's own value.
6. **Cap pause/skip/delay abuse.** Sets a limit on how many times a subscriber can pause or delay before the cadence auto-adjusts or a human reviews. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: abuse-cap rule. Check: cap prevents indefinite free pausing without breaking a genuine flexibility need.
7. **Design non-monetary retention mechanics.** Adds order streaks, a milestone surprise gift, or a mystery reward as an addition to, not a replacement for, the discount-based save offer. Skill: `life/subscription-flow-designer`. Output: non-monetary mechanic design. Check: non-monetary mechanic is tested against a discount-only save, not assumed better.
8. **Set per-stage save-rate tracking.** Sets the metric to track save rate at each of the four cancel-flow stages, so a weak stage can be rewritten instead of guessed at. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: per-stage tracking plan. Check: each stage has its own named metric.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; subscription and cancel-flow design changes are founder_gate. Skill: `none`. Output: gated subscription/cancel-flow spec. Check: no subscription platform config changed from this workflow.

Outputs: subscription offer spec, four-stage cancel-flow design, costed save-offer table. QA: cancel as easy as sign-up; save offer sized to the subscriber's own value; per-stage save rate tracked separately. Read point: per-stage save rate and overall subscriber churn rate, read monthly. Duration: 1 day agent time.

### LIFE-W21 Design referral, partner and payment-term offers

Trigger: on demand. Human role: loyalty/offers program manager designing acquisition-adjacent offers for existing customers. Capabilities: LIFE-C45, LIFE-C46. Needs: repeat-buyer base data, non-competing partner candidates, margin data for BNPL/trial economics.

1. **Design the give-X-get-X referral offer.** Sizes a give-X-get-X referral reward from the repeat-buyer base's real value, so the giver and the receiver both get a meaningful, margin-safe reward. Skill: `life/referral-payment-offer`. Output: referral offer spec. Check: reward sized against real margin, not copied from a competitor.
2. **Set the referral tracking mechanism.** Sets the unique code or link and the reward-payout trigger and timing. Skill: `life/referral-payment-offer`. Output: tracking mechanism. Check: reward only pays out on a confirmed, non-refunded order.
3. **Find non-competing partner perks.** Identifies partner brands serving the same customer without competing, that can offer a no-cost perk in exchange for cross-promotion. Skill: `life/referral-payment-offer`. Output: partner candidate list. Check: partner does not compete on the same product category.
4. **Design installment/BNPL terms.** Designs installment terms with a clear per-installment price shown to the buyer, checked against the payment provider's real fee. Skill: `life/referral-payment-offer`. Output: BNPL/installment term spec. Check: provider fee is real, not estimated, and shown against margin.
5. **Design nominal-price trial pricing.** Designs a nominal-price trial that converts to full price on a stated date, with delayed payment capture handled correctly for the trial period. Skill: `life/referral-payment-offer`. Output: trial pricing spec. Check: conversion date and full price are disclosed to the buyer before they start the trial.
6. **Check both offers against discount policy.** Runs the referral reward and the trial/BNPL terms against the discount policy and any founder red line. Skill: `life/discount-policy-check`. Output: pass/fail with the rule cited. Check: fails closed if a red line is unclear.
7. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and sends both offer designs for the founder's decision. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: no payment term shown to the buyer that is not accurate to the provider's real terms.

Outputs: referral offer spec, partner perk candidate list, BNPL/trial pricing spec. QA: referral reward sized to real margin; provider fees real, not estimated; trial conversion terms disclosed upfront. Read point: referral take-rate and BNPL/trial conversion-to-full-price rate, read monthly. Duration: 1 day agent time.

### LIFE-W22 Run the offer testing framework

Trigger: on demand. Human role: offers/pricing owner sequencing and reading offer tests. Capabilities: LIFE-C47. Needs: offer test backlog, registry: past offer tests, metric contract.

1. **Pull the offer test backlog.** Reads every proposed offer, bundle, GWP, upsell, guarantee or subscription test not yet run. Skill: `life/offer-testing-framework`. Output: backlog list. Check: backlog matches what W18, W20 and W21 have queued.
2. **Sequence structure before depth.** Orders tests so structure (does a bundle beat a single SKU at all) is tested before depth (which bundle size wins), one variable at a time. Skill: `life/offer-testing-framework`. Output: sequenced test order. Check: no test changes two variables at once.
3. **Check dependency order.** Confirms a test whose prerequisite (e.g. a working discount-policy check) is not yet done ranks below that prerequisite. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: dependency-checked order. Check: a dependent test never runs ahead of its prerequisite.
4. **Pre-register the metric and read window.** Sets the exact metric and read window for each test before it launches. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: pre-registered metric/window per test. Check: metric and window fixed before launch, never chosen after seeing results.
5. **Keep the backlog current.** Adds new candidates from W18/W20/W21 and removes tests already run, keeping one live backlog rather than scattered lists. Skill: `life/offer-testing-framework`. Output: updated backlog. Check: backlog has no duplicate test for the same offer question.
6. **Log each test to the registry.** Records id, hypothesis, metric, read point and kill/scale rule before launch. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entries. Check: every entry has a metric that is a contract field.

Outputs: sequenced, dependency-checked offer test backlog, registry entries. QA: one variable per test; metric and window pre-registered before launch; no duplicate test in the backlog. Read point: per-test, at its pre-registered read window. Duration: 1.5 hours agent time per pass.

### LIFE-W23 Design and manage the gift card program

Trigger: on demand. Human role: loyalty/CRM manager designing the gift card as a product. Capabilities: LIFE-C58. Needs: current checkout/gift card app if any, return/refund policy, cash-flow visibility.

1. **Read current gift card usage if any.** Pulls current denominations, redemption rate and outstanding balance if a gift card app already exists. Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: current-state snapshot. Check: figures come from the app's own data, not estimated.
2. **Choose denominations and any bonus tier.** Sets the sellable denominations and any bonus-value tier (buy X, get X-plus-bonus loaded). Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: denomination and bonus design. Check: bonus tier sized against real margin, not copied from a competitor.
3. **Set expiry policy per market law.** Sets the expiry rule checked against what is legal in each market the brand sells gift cards into. Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: expiry policy with source and date. Check: expiry rule never conflicts with a market's minimum legal validity period.
4. **Design the corporate/bulk-gifting offer.** Designs a bulk-purchase tier and process for B2B or occasion buying, distinct from the single-card retail flow. Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: bulk-gifting offer design. Check: bulk tier has its own pricing and delivery process, not the retail flow scaled up.
5. **Set the refund-credit interaction rule.** Decides how a refund-issued store credit interacts with a purchased gift card, so a refund credit is never expired the way a promotional one might be. Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: credit-interaction rule. Check: refund-issued credit follows the refund policy's own expiry, never the promotional one.
6. **Model the float liability.** Models the unredeemed-balance liability so the founder sees the cash-flow effect of the program before launch. Skill: `life/gift-card-program`. Output: float-liability model. Check: liability model computed in code from real sell-through assumptions stated up front.
7. **Write the reminder flow copy.** Writes the purchase-confirmation, balance-reminder-before-expiry and redemption-receipt messages. Skill: `life/message`. Output: reminder flow copy. Check: balance reminder timed before expiry, not after.
8. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and sends the full program design and float model to the founder. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: no gift card app or checkout change made from this workflow.

Outputs: gift card program design, float-liability model, reminder flow copy. QA: expiry policy checked per market law; float liability computed in code; refund credit never expired like a promotional one. Read point: sell-through rate and float balance, read quarterly after launch. Duration: 1 day agent time.

### LIFE-W24 Design proactive outreach: live chat, phone concierge and voice/IVR

Trigger: on demand. Human role: customer support lead / CX manager building proactive outreach channels. Capabilities: LIFE-C59, LIFE-C60, LIFE-C65. Needs: site analytics (visit/session data), VIP/segmentation list, high-AOV cart abandonment data, consent and quiet-hour rules.

1. **Define the intent signals for proactive chat.** Sets the visit-count, product-viewed and known-VIP signals that qualify a visitor for a proactive chat opener. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: intent-signal definition. Check: signals are specific and measurable, not 'seems interested'.
2. **Write the proactive chat openers.** Writes an opener per signal, never a generic 'need help?', and sets the cap on how often one visitor is approached. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: opener set with frequency cap. Check: every opener is specific to its triggering signal.
3. **Set the AOV threshold for phone follow-up.** Sets the order value or product category above which a phone call beats email/SMS for cart abandonment or a pre-purchase question. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: AOV threshold. Check: threshold set from the brand's own AOV distribution, not a round guess.
4. **Prepare call scripts per scenario.** Writes the call script for cart-abandon follow-up, pre-purchase questions and VIP check-ins. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: call scripts. Check: each script matches one named scenario, not a single generic pitch.
5. **Build the daily call queue.** Ranks today's high-AOV abandoners and inquiries by order value and recency for a human to call. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: ranked call queue. Check: queue ranked by value and recency, in code.
6. **Decide which use cases justify voice/IVR.** Chooses which events (COD pre-dispatch confirmation, delivery-failure follow-up, post-resolution satisfaction check) justify an automated IVR call, separate from SMS/WhatsApp. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: IVR use-case list. Check: IVR reserved for cases where a call genuinely outperforms text, not used by default.
7. **Write IVR scripts and set cadence.** Writes the IVR call flow for each automated use case and sets the call-attempt cadence and quiet hours, matching the SMS/WhatsApp consent rules already set. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: IVR scripts and cadence. Check: quiet hours match the channel policy already set in W08.
8. **Set outcome logging for all three channels.** Sets how chat, call and IVR outcomes log back into the order/customer record and the segmentation model. Skill: `life/concierge-outreach`. Output: outcome-logging rule. Check: every outcome (answered, confirmed, no answer, opted out) has a place to log to.
9. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; every proactive-outreach channel here is founder_gate before launch. Skill: `none`. Output: gated outreach package. Check: no call, chat message or IVR call is placed from this workflow; a human places every call.

Outputs: proactive-chat trigger and opener set, ranked call queue and scripts, IVR scripts and cadence. QA: signals specific and measurable; AOV threshold from real distribution; IVR quiet hours match channel policy. Read point: conversion and reply rate by signal, and cost per outcome by channel, read monthly. Duration: 1 day agent time.

### LIFE-W25 Run marketplace seller-support queues

Trigger: continuous. Human role: customer support agent handling Amazon/Flipkart buyer-seller queues. Capabilities: LIFE-C67. Needs: marketplace seller console access, order and delivery-proof data.

1. **Pull open buyer-seller messages and claims.** Reads open messages and A-to-Z-style guarantee claims from each marketplace's own seller console. Skill: `life/marketplace-support-queue`. Output: open queue snapshot. Check: queue read from each marketplace's own console, not the brand's own helpdesk.
2. **Check each item's response clock.** Notes the exact deadline each marketplace enforces (about 48 hours for a buyer message, about 72 hours for a claim response). Skill: `life/marketplace-support-queue`. Output: deadline-tagged queue. Check: every item shows its own clock, since missing the message clock itself can trigger a claim right.
3. **Draft the buyer-message response.** Drafts a response inside the marketplace's own message window, in the brand's voice. Skill: `life/support-reply-draft`. Output: drafted responses. Check: no draft skips the message-clock deadline.
4. **Assemble claim evidence.** Pulls delivery proof and the correspondence log the marketplace accepts for a guarantee claim. Skill: `life/marketplace-support-queue`. Output: evidence package per claim. Check: evidence matches what the specific marketplace's claim process accepts.
5. **Draft the claim response.** Drafts the claim response with the assembled evidence inside the marketplace's own response window. Skill: `life/marketplace-support-queue`. Output: drafted claim responses. Check: response drafted inside the claim's own clock, separate from the brand's helpdesk SLA.
6. **Track claim rate and reason.** Tracks claim rate and reason over time, flagging a rising rate as a signal for the returns/fulfilment specialist. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: claim-rate trend. Check: trend kept separate from the brand's own helpdesk reporting.
7. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; every drafted response posts only after founder or authorised review, until the marketplace-write surface is switched on. Skill: `none`. Output: gated drafts. Check: queue and SLA clocks kept separate from the brand's own helpdesk tagging.

Outputs: drafted buyer-message and claim responses within each marketplace's clock, claim-rate trend report. QA: every response drafted inside its own marketplace clock; evidence matches the specific marketplace's accepted format; queue kept separate from the brand's own helpdesk SLA. Read point: claim rate and response-time-to-deadline, read monthly. Duration: 30 minutes agent time daily.

### LIFE-W26 Run the mystery-shopper brand journey audit (CX leg)

Trigger: quarterly. Human role: CX lead auditing the brand's own post-purchase and support journey. Capabilities: BRAND-C67. Needs: site/checkout access for a real test order, support contact channel, brand's stated promises (delivery estimate, guarantee, response time).

1. **Place a real test order.** Walks the brand's own purchase journey as a customer would: browse, cart, checkout, order confirmation, using a real or test-gateway order per the brand's rules. Skill: `life/mystery-shopper-audit`. Output: order journey log with timestamps. Check: no live production charge unless the brand's own test-order process allows it.
2. **Log every stated promise against what happens.** Records the delivery estimate, confirmation message and any promise made at checkout, to compare against what actually happens. Skill: `life/mystery-shopper-audit`. Output: promise-vs-reality log. Check: every promise is quoted verbatim from the brand's own page or message.
3. **Send a real support query.** Sends a genuine support question through the brand's own contact channel, the way a real customer would. Skill: `life/mystery-shopper-audit`. Output: support interaction log. Check: the query is realistic, not a test designed to be easy.
4. **Time the response against the stated SLA.** Measures the actual response time against what the brand states or implies as its response time. Skill: `life/mystery-shopper-audit`. Output: SLA-vs-actual comparison. Check: comparison uses the brand's own stated SLA, not an assumed one.
5. **Catalogue every gap found.** Lists every place the real journey fell short of the stated promise, from checkout through support, each with evidence. Skill: `life/mystery-shopper-audit`. Output: gap catalogue with evidence. Check: every gap entry is evidenced by a timestamp, screenshot or quoted message.
6. **Route each gap to its owning workflow.** Sends a delivery-communication gap to W13, a support gap to W15, a checkout gap to the Site specialist. Skill: `none`. Output: routed gap list. Check: every gap names the workflow or agent it was routed to.
7. **Assemble the quarterly audit report.** Puts the journey log, promise-vs-reality comparison and routed gaps into one report. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: quarterly mystery-shopper report. Check: matches the report-section output shape.
8. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; this is founder_gate since the report may prompt a policy or page change. Skill: `none`. Output: gated report. Check: no change made to the site or support system from this workflow itself.

Outputs: quarterly mystery-shopper audit report with routed gaps. QA: every gap evidenced, not impressionistic; comparison uses the brand's own stated promise, not an assumed one; every gap routed to an owning workflow. Read point: quarterly, compared to the prior quarter's same audit. Duration: 3 hours agent time quarterly.

### LIFE-W27 Run the corporate gifting sales desk

Trigger: event: inbound corporate enquiry; 90 days before Diwali for outbound. Human role: B2B/corporate gifting sales desk operator. Capabilities: LIFE-G01. Needs: inbound enquiry or the outbound lead list, bulk pricing tiers, customisation (packaging/engraving) options, GST invoicing capability, split-delivery address list.

Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: qualified enquiry or lead list. Check: outbound timing starts at least 90 days before the target festival.
2. **Quote with bulk tiers.** Prepares a quote using the bulk pricing tiers checked against margin, not a discretionary discount. Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: bulk quote. Check: quote uses the standing bulk tiers, checked against discount policy for anything beyond them.
3. **Confirm custom packaging or engraving options.** Confirms what customisation is offered, its lead time and any minimum order quantity. Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: customisation confirmation. Check: lead time stated matches the actual production/engraving capacity.
4. **Prepare the GST invoice.** Prepares the GST-compliant invoice for the B2B order, checked against the current GST rate and the buyer's GSTIN if provided. Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: GST invoice draft. Check: invoice checked against the current GST rule, cited with date.
5. **Plan split delivery to multiple addresses.** Plans delivery to the many recipient addresses the corporate order requires, checking courier serviceability for each. Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: split-delivery plan. Check: every address checked for courier serviceability before the order is confirmed.
6. **Set the repeat-order reminder.** Sets a reminder to the same corporate buyer ahead of next season's likely order. Skill: `life/corporate-gifting-desk`. Output: repeat-order reminder. Check: reminder timed to the same lead-time rule as the original outbound.
7. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist; any quote beyond the standing bulk tiers or a new customisation option is founder_gate. Skill: `none`. Output: gated quote and order plan. Check: no invoice issued or order confirmed from this workflow without the founder's sign-off on anything beyond the standing tiers.

Outputs: bulk quote, GST invoice draft, split-delivery plan, repeat-order reminder. QA: quote uses standing bulk tiers or is gated if beyond them; GST invoice checked against current rule; every delivery address checked for serviceability. Read point: corporate order volume and repeat-order rate, read each gifting season. Duration: 2 hours agent time per enquiry; more for outbound list-building.

### LIFE-W28 Run the grievance officer queue and consumer notices

Trigger: event: grievance or notice received; weekly SLA check. Human role: grievance officer / CX manager handling statutory complaints. Capabilities: LIFE-G03. Needs: grievance/complaint inbox, consumer-forum (e-Daakhil) and legal notice tracker, India's E-Commerce Rules SLA (48-hour acknowledgement, 1-month resolution).

1. **Log every grievance on receipt.** Logs each grievance or consumer notice the moment it arrives, with the statutory clock started from that timestamp. Skill: `life/grievance-queue`. Output: logged grievance with clock started. Check: clock starts from actual receipt, not from when it is read.
2. **Acknowledge within 48 hours.** Sends the acknowledgement within the 48-hour statutory window required under India's E-Commerce Rules. Skill: `life/grievance-queue`. Output: acknowledgement sent with timestamp. Check: acknowledgement sent inside 48 hours or flagged as breached the moment it is missed.
3. **Investigate and prepare the resolution.** Investigates the complaint against the order/product/policy record and prepares a resolution within the 1-month statutory window. Skill: `life/grievance-queue`. Output: resolution draft. Check: resolution draft cites the specific policy or order fact it rests on.
4. **Track consumer-forum and legal notices separately.** Tracks any e-Daakhil consumer-forum case or legal notice on its own separate clock and status, distinct from an ordinary grievance. Skill: `life/grievance-queue`. Output: notice tracker update. Check: a legal notice is never merged into the ordinary grievance queue's SLA.
5. **Run the weekly SLA check.** Checks every open grievance and notice against its statutory clock, flagging anything approaching or past deadline. Skill: `life/grievance-queue`. Output: weekly SLA report. Check: every open item shows days remaining or days breached, in code.
6. **Route unresolved or escalated items to the founder.** Sends any item nearing breach, or any consumer-forum/legal notice, to the founder rather than letting the statutory clock lapse quietly. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: an item nearing breach is never left for the next weekly check alone.
7. **Log closure.** Records the resolution and closure date against the statutory deadline once resolved. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: closure record. Check: closure date compared explicitly to the statutory deadline.

Outputs: logged grievance queue with SLA clocks, acknowledgements sent, resolution drafts, notice tracker, weekly SLA report. QA: 48-hour acknowledgement and 1-month resolution windows tracked in code; legal notices tracked on their own separate clock; nothing nearing breach left unescalated. Read point: weekly SLA compliance rate; every notice tracked to closure. Duration: 30 minutes agent time daily; more per active grievance.

### LIFE-W29 Run warranty, repair and after-sales service

Trigger: event: service request. Human role: after-sales service coordinator. Capabilities: LIFE-G04. Needs: service request, warranty terms, repair partner network, courier pickup capability.

1. **Intake the service request.** Logs the request type (resizing, repair, re-plating, warranty claim, buyback/exchange) with the product and purchase date. Skill: `life/warranty-service-queue`. Output: logged service request. Check: purchase date checked against the warranty window before anything is promised.
2. **Check warranty coverage.** Confirms whether the request is covered under warranty, out-of-warranty paid service, or a buyback/exchange case. Skill: `life/warranty-service-queue`. Output: coverage determination. Check: determination cites the specific warranty clause, not a general assumption.
3. **Arrange courier pickup.** Books the pickup with the courier or repair-partner network for the item to travel to service. Skill: `life/warranty-service-queue`. Output: pickup booking. Check: pickup address and item description confirmed with the customer before booking.
4. **Route to the repair partner.** Sends the item and its coverage determination to the correct repair partner (resizing, repair, re-plating) with the expected turnaround. Skill: `life/warranty-service-queue`. Output: repair-partner routing. Check: turnaround quoted matches the partner's own stated capacity, not a guess.
5. **Send status updates.** Sends the customer a status update at intake, at drop-off to the partner, and at return dispatch. Skill: `life/message`. Output: status update messages. Check: every update reflects a real status change, not a scheduled filler message.
6. **Track cost per case.** Tracks the cost of each service case (courier both ways, partner fee, any replacement part) against the product's warranty reserve. Skill: `life/warranty-service-queue`. Output: cost-per-case figure. Check: cost computed in code from real courier and partner invoices, not estimated.
7. **Close the case and log it.** Confirms the item's return or the buyback/exchange completion and logs the case with its resolution and total cost. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: closed case record. Check: closure logged only once the customer confirms receipt or the exchange completes.

Outputs: logged and routed service requests, status update messages, cost-per-case tracking, closed case records. QA: warranty determination cites the specific clause; turnaround matches the partner's real capacity; cost tracked in code from real invoices. Read point: average turnaround time and cost per case, read monthly against the warranty reserve. Duration: 20 to 40 minutes agent time per case.

### LIFE-W30 Build and moderate a branded customer community

Trigger: founder decides to test a community; quarterly review after launch. Human role: community manager for customers. Capabilities: LIFE-C53. Needs: existing repeat-customer list, brand voice record.

1. **Check whether a community fits the brand.** Checks whether the brand has a base of repeat, high-engagement buyers large enough to sustain a community, since not every store benefits. Skill: `life/community-program`. Output: go/no-go recommendation with evidence. Check: recommendation cites the actual repeat-buyer count and engagement level, not a general enthusiasm.
2. **Choose the platform and structure.** Chooses Slack, Discord or Circle and designs the channel/space structure for the brand's actual use cases (product help, early access, feedback). Skill: `life/community-program`. Output: platform choice and channel structure. Check: structure matches named use cases, not a generic template.
3. **Write the moderation policy.** Writes what gets removed and how a complaint raised in a public channel routes to support instead of being handled in public. Skill: `life/community-program`. Output: moderation policy. Check: every complaint routing path leads to the actual support queue, not left in public.
4. **Build the engagement calendar.** Builds a light, always-on engagement calendar (prompts, early-access drops, AMAs) distinct from the weekly campaign calendar. Skill: `life/community-program`. Output: engagement calendar. Check: calendar is light enough to sustain without daily staff time.
5. **Set the founder/team presence rule.** Sets when a founder or team member should show up in person versus staying quiet, so presence feels intentional, not absent or overbearing. Skill: `life/community-program`. Output: presence rule. Check: rule names specific trigger moments, not a vague 'sometimes'.
6. **Route recurring themes back to VoC.** Sets the path for a recurring community theme to reach the brand brain and the VoC record (W17), not stay siloed in the community. Skill: `none`. Output: routing rule. Check: routing path names the exact workflow (W17) it feeds.
7. **Set health tracking.** Tracks membership, active-member share and the repeat-purchase rate of members versus non-members as the one proxy metric. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: health tracking plan. Check: repeat-purchase comparison computed in code, member vs non-member.
8. **Self-QA and route to the founder gate.** Runs the QA checklist and sends the full recommendation, structure and policy to the founder before build. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: no workspace created or invitation sent from this workflow.

Outputs: community structure and moderation policy, engagement calendar, monthly/quarterly community health note. QA: go/no-go grounded in real repeat-buyer evidence; complaints route to support, not left public; member vs non-member repeat rate tracked in code. Read point: quarterly: membership, active-member share, and repeat-purchase rate of members vs non-members. Duration: 1 day agent time to design; ongoing light moderation after launch.

## Organic and search (ORG), 30 workflows

### ORG-W01 Set organic account and platform strategy

Trigger: on demand. Human role: social media manager setting the platform plan for the quarter. Capabilities: ORG-C01. Needs: brand brain: avatars, category, region, voice, current account list and health, competitor teardown.

1. **Read the avatar and voice.** Pull avatar age band, region, language and where they already spend time online from the brand brain. Skill: `org/organic-channel-strategy`. Output: avatar summary for platform fit. Check: summary names a real age band and region, not a generic buyer. Ask the founder if: the avatar section is missing or older than 6 months.
2. **Pull the competitive teardown.** Get structure-only notes on which platforms 3-5 close competitors run, cadence and format mix. Skill: `org/format-design`. Output: competitor platform/cadence table. Check: table names structure only, no copied text.
3. **Score each candidate platform.** Score avatar fit, production cost, and set a 2-week trial success bar for anything new. Skill: `org/organic-channel-strategy`. Output: scored platform table with trial bars. Check: every new platform has a stated success bar and kill date.
4. **Set content pillars.** Set 3-5 pillars, each naming a SKU and the objection it answers. Skill: `org/organic-channel-strategy`. Output: pillar list. Check: every pillar names a SKU and an objection.
5. **Lock the visual world.** Set palette, type and caption style against the brand's approved assets so every account matches. Skill: `org/organic-channel-strategy`. Output: visual world spec. Check: spec matches approved brand assets, not a new look. Ask the founder if: no approved brand asset file exists yet.
6. **Write the account setup checklist.** Bio, link-in-bio, contact, category tag, WhatsApp Business link, per platform. Skill: `org/organic-channel-strategy`. Output: setup checklist per platform. Check: every field has a real value, no placeholder.
7. **Check platform-policy compliance.** Check disclosure and content-policy requirements per platform (synthetic voice, sponsored, minors in ads). Skill: `none`. Output: compliance note per platform. Check: every platform's current policy checked, not last year's.
8. **Package the founder card.** Turn the plan into a plain-English card for any new account, platform drop, or account-type change. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder decision card. Check: card has 3 sentences and clear buttons.
9. **Log the decision.** Record the founder's decision and reason in the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: reason stored verbatim.

Outputs: positioning brief, platform plan, visual world spec, account setup checklist. QA: Every pillar names a SKU and objection; No platform runs past its trial date without a decision; Visual spec matches approved assets. Read point: at the trial kill date for any new platform, else quarterly. Duration: agent: 2-3 hours; founder decision: same day to 1 week.

### ORG-W02 Build the monthly content calendar, scripts and shoot plan

Trigger: weekly (calendar maintenance), monthly (full rebuild). Human role: content strategist and social media manager building the month's postable plan. Capabilities: ORG-C02, ORG-C03, ORG-C04, ORG-C06. Needs: brand brain: pillars, SKUs, stock status, voice, claims tiers, founder's available shoot days, prior month's performance read.

1. **Mine buyer and avatar questions.** Pull the ranked question bank from support tickets, DMs, reviews and Search Console. Skill: `org/buyer-question-mapping`. Output: ranked question bank. Check: every question has a quoted source and date. Ask the founder if: no support inbox is connected.
2. **Set the posting floor and cadence.** Set a minimum weekly post count per platform the team can hold without burning out, based on last month's actual delivery rate. Skill: `org/production-planning`. Output: cadence target per platform. Check: floor is at or below what was actually delivered last month, not aspirational.
3. **Sequence SKUs and stock moments.** Slot SKUs against current and projected stock status so no post promotes an item going out of stock mid-run. Skill: `none`. Output: SKU-sequenced draft calendar. Check: no slot promotes a SKU with a stock-out inside the post's live window.
4. **Add the festive and regional layer.** Map Diwali, Holi, regional festivals and shipping cutoffs onto the calendar, tagging language variant per slot. Skill: `none`. Output: calendar with festive/regional tags. Check: no post promises delivery the warehouse cannot hit.
5. **Set the fair test window.** Give the calendar a stated run length before judging it, tied to the store's own baseline. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: test window and success bar. Check: window is stated in dates, not 'a while'.
6. **Plan founder camera time and batching.** Confirm the founder's available shoot days, set fixed batching days, and pick fast-turnaround formats for founder-only days. Skill: `org/production-planning`. Output: shoot schedule. Check: every founder-dependent slot has a confirmed date or is flagged. Ask the founder if: the founder has not confirmed available days for the month.
7. **Write scripts, hooks and captions.** For every slot, dispatch to the matching make-content child by format and platform. Skill: `org/make-content`. Output: draft per slot: hook, body, caption, CTA, claims used. Check: every draft names one SKU and one buyer, reading level under grade 10.
8. **Build shot lists.** For video and carousel slots, confirm the shot list or slide list is filmable/designable without a follow-up question. Skill: `none`. Output: shot/slide list per slot. Check: shot seconds sum to target length; slide count 5-9.
9. **Run the claims gate.** Check every draft's factual claims against the claims tiers. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail per draft. Check: zero untraced claims remain.
10. **Route destinations.** Confirm every slot's link or Story tag points to a live, matching page before it is scheduled. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: destination pass/hold per slot. Check: destination confirmed live at check time, not assumed.
11. **Assemble the final calendar.** Merge cadence, SKU sequencing, festive layer, scripts, shot lists and destination checks into one dated calendar. Skill: `none`. Output: dated calendar rows: platform, pillar, SKU, format, hook, funnel stage, status. Check: every row has a status and a funnel-stage tag.
12. **Log the plan.** Register the month's plan and its success bar in the registry for the next performance read to compare against. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: metric and read point are contract fields, not invented.

Outputs: dated content calendar, scripts and captions per slot, shot lists, shoot schedule. QA: Every slot has a named SKU, buyer, claims-checked draft and destination pass; Cadence floor matches actual delivered capacity; Festive dates checked against shipping cutoffs. Read point: end of the stated test window, against the store's own baseline. Duration: agent: 1-2 days; founder review and shoot days: 1-2 weeks.

### ORG-W03 Keep the idea bank fed and design repeatable formats

Trigger: weekly, or when the idea bank runs low, or after a post clearly outperforms. Human role: content strategist mining ideas and naming winning formats. Capabilities: ORG-C05, ORG-C07. Needs: comment/DM export, product facts sheet, founder story log, recent post performance, public competitor posts.

1. **Mine comments and DMs.** Pull recurring questions, jokes and requests from the last week's comments and DMs. Skill: `org/format-design`. Output: idea stubs from owned conversation. Check: each idea has a source quote.
2. **Mine product facts and founder stories.** Pull first-hand material from the product facts sheet and founder story log. Skill: `org/format-design`. Output: idea stubs from first-hand material. Check: each idea is something a competitor cannot simply restate.
3. **Tear down competitor and category posts.** Review 5-10 public competitor/category posts for pacing, hook type and shot count only. Skill: `org/format-design`. Output: teardown notes: structure and mood only. Check: no copied words, logos, products or faces.
4. **Check recent performance for a repeatable winner.** Compare recent posts to the account's own baseline for a clear outperformer. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: candidate winning post, if any. Check: comparison uses the account's own baseline, not an industry number.
5. **Name and template the format.** If a winner qualifies, name it and write a fixed template a team member can run without the founder. Skill: `org/format-design`. Output: named format with template. Check: template states what makes it runnable without the founder.
6. **Rank the new ideas.** Rank all new idea stubs for calendar priority. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked idea list. Check: no arithmetic done outside code.
7. **Add to the idea bank.** Log every new idea and format with source and date. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: idea bank entries. Check: every entry has a source and date.

Outputs: idea bank additions, teardown notes, a new named format (when one qualifies). QA: Every idea traces to a real source; No competitor material copied beyond structure/mood; New format states its without-founder runnability. Read point: next monthly performance read, watching whether the new format repeats its result. Duration: agent: half a day, weekly.

### ORG-W04 Draft daily community replies

Trigger: daily. Human role: community manager clearing the comment and DM queue. Capabilities: ORG-C09. Needs: comment/DM queue, brand voice file, policy/FAQ answers, current stock and shipping status.

1. **Pull and sort the queue.** Sort into product question, complaint, praise, spam/bot, sales-ready lead. Skill: `org/community-response-drafting`. Output: sorted queue. Check: every item has exactly one type.
2. **Draft product-question replies.** Write a specific answer with a page link where relevant. Skill: `org/community-response-drafting`. Output: draft replies. Check: no generic templated reply on a specific question.
3. **Draft complaint replies.** Acknowledge specifically and give a real next step. Skill: `org/community-response-drafting`. Output: draft replies. Check: every complaint reply names a concrete next step.
4. **Draft sales-ready lead replies.** Write the reply plus the DM hand-off line into any approved funnel. Skill: `org/community-response-drafting`. Output: draft replies. Check: hand-off line matches the approved funnel design.
5. **Check DM automation policy.** Check any DM automation in use against current platform rate-limit and disclosure rules. Skill: `org/community-response-drafting`. Output: policy check result. Check: checked against current policy, not an assumption.
6. **Flag founder items.** Flag PR risk, refund exceptions or legal threats for the founder instead of drafting a reply. Skill: `none`. Output: flagged list. Check: nothing risky is drafted and sent without a flag.
7. **Queue for human send.** Package all drafts for a human to review and send. Skill: `none`. Output: send-ready queue. Check: every drafted reply has a type and a next step.

Outputs: drafted replies, flagged items for the founder. QA: No generic reply on a specific question; DM automation checked against current policy; Every complaint reply names a next step. Read point: daily, response time and resolution tracked weekly. Duration: agent: 30-60 minutes; human send: same day.

### ORG-W05 Design the comment-to-DM and lead-magnet funnel

Trigger: on demand. Human role: social media manager designing how a post captures a stranger as a contact. Capabilities: ORG-C10. Needs: offer or lead-magnet candidate, pillar/content map, platform DM-automation policy, buyer-question map.

1. **Confirm the lead magnet matches a real want.** Check the offer against the buyer-question map for evidence buyers actually asked for it. Skill: `org/funnel-design`. Output: evidenced lead magnet. Check: traces to a real, quoted buyer want, not a generic discount.
2. **Design the DM flow.** Set the trigger keyword, auto-reply copy and human hand-off point. Skill: `org/funnel-design`. Output: flow spec. Check: hand-off point is named, not open-ended.
3. **Check platform policy.** Check the flow against current Meta/Instagram DM-automation policy. Skill: `org/funnel-design`. Output: policy check. Check: checked against current policy.
4. **Design the viral-spike overflow plan.** Set the comment velocity that needs a human on standby and the overflow message. Skill: `org/funnel-design`. Output: overflow plan. Check: a specific velocity threshold is named.
5. **Map the destination.** Decide where the captured contact lands (WhatsApp, email, CRM) and hand the after-capture sequence to Lifecycle. Skill: `org/funnel-design`. Output: destination handoff note. Check: Lifecycle is named as the owner of what happens after capture.
6. **Package the founder card.** Present cost, automation, and platform-policy risk. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder decision card. Check: card names cost and risk plainly.
7. **Log the decision.** Record the founder's decision. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: reason stored verbatim.

Outputs: funnel design spec, founder decision card. QA: Lead magnet is evidenced, not generic; Policy checked current; Overflow plan exists before launch. Read point: 4 weeks after launch: capture rate and DM response time. Duration: agent: half a day; founder decision: same day to 1 week.

### ORG-W06 Repurpose UGC and reviews into content

Trigger: weekly. Human role: content strategist turning proof into postable content. Capabilities: ORG-C11. Needs: verified review/photo export, creator seeding output, usage-rights confirmation, claims tiers.

1. **Pull new verified proof.** Pull new verified reviews, photos and unboxing videos since the last run. Skill: `org/ugc-repurposing`. Output: candidate proof list. Check: only items with real text or footage, not a bare star.
2. **Check usage rights.** Confirm each item's usage rights against the buyer or creator agreement. Skill: `org/ugc-repurposing`. Output: rights-cleared list, flagged list. Check: nothing unconfirmed proceeds without founder permission. Ask the founder if: usage rights are unconfirmed for a strong piece of content.
3. **Pick the strongest proof per SKU.** Select the most specific quotes and images per SKU. Skill: `org/ugc-repurposing`. Output: selected proof per SKU. Check: specific detail beats generic praise.
4. **Draft the content.** Dispatch to make-content for the matching format (quote card, stitched reel, before/after). Skill: `org/make-content`. Output: drafts. Check: source credit matches the agreement.
5. **Run the claims gate on restated claims.** Check any customer claim restated as a brand claim against the claims tiers. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail. Check: a customer opinion is never assumed to be an approved claim.
6. **Add to the calendar and library.** Tag by SKU and funnel stage, add to the asset library. Skill: `none`. Output: tagged drafts in the calendar/library. Check: every draft is tagged.

Outputs: postable UGC drafts, rights-flagged items. QA: No content used without confirmed rights; Restated claims tier-checked; Every draft tagged by SKU and funnel stage. Read point: next monthly performance read, UGC-tagged post performance vs baseline. Duration: agent: half a day, weekly.

### ORG-W07 Run pre-publish destination and funnel-stage QA

Trigger: event: post scheduled. Human role: social media manager's final check before anything goes live. Capabilities: ORG-C12. Needs: scheduled post, destination link or Story tag, live site status.

1. **Open the destination.** Open the post's link or Story product tag directly. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: page load result. Check: checked live, not assumed.
2. **Confirm the page matches the promise.** Confirm live, matching product/offer, honest stock or waitlist state. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: match verdict. Check: stock state is honest, not defaulted to in-stock.
3. **Confirm price and offer match exactly.** Compare the post's stated price/offer to the live page. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: price match verdict. Check: exact match required, not approximate.
4. **Tag the funnel stage.** Tag the post awareness, consideration, decision or retention. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: funnel-stage tag. Check: every post gets exactly one tag.
5. **Pass or hold.** Pass the post or hold it with the specific mismatch reason. Skill: `org/destination-qa`. Output: pass/hold decision. Check: a hold names the exact mismatch.
6. **Log a held post.** When a post is held, log the reason and notify the calendar owner so the slot is fixed or rescheduled, not silently dropped. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: held-post log entry. Check: every hold has a logged reason and an owner notified.

Outputs: pass/hold verdict per post, funnel-stage tag. QA: Destination checked live at the moment; Price/offer match is exact; Every passing post carries a funnel-stage tag. Read point: immediately, before publish. Duration: agent: under 5 minutes per post.

### ORG-W08 Run the content lifecycle: reuse and retire

Trigger: weekly. Human role: content strategist managing the asset library's shelf life. Capabilities: ORG-C13. Needs: content/asset library with tags and dates, cross-channel usage log.

1. **Pull the library.** Pull every asset with publish date, SKU tag and format. Skill: `org/content-lifecycle`. Output: asset list. Check: every asset has a date and tag.
2. **Apply shelf-life rules.** Apply the right rule per asset type (seasonal, price-specific, evergreen). Skill: `org/content-lifecycle`. Output: asset list with expiry status. Check: no default rule applied without checking the asset type.
3. **Find reuse slots.** Find unused reuse slots for still-valid assets: PDP, email, DM, post-purchase. Skill: `org/content-lifecycle`. Output: reuse candidates. Check: each candidate names a specific surface.
4. **Mark retirements.** Mark expired assets and list every surface they still appear on. Skill: `org/content-lifecycle`. Output: retire list with live surfaces. Check: retirement covers every surface, not just the origin post.
5. **Hand off reuse and retirement.** Send reuse candidates to the calendar and retirement list to the surface owners (Site for PDP, Life for email). Skill: `none`. Output: handoff notes. Check: each item has a named owner.
6. **Log the cycle.** Register how many assets were reused, retired and where, for the next monthly performance read. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: counts are real, not estimated.

Outputs: reuse candidate list, retirement list. QA: Every asset has a shelf-life rule applied; Retirement covers every surface; Price/stock content never stays live past accuracy. Read point: next monthly performance read, checking reused assets' new-surface performance. Duration: agent: 2-3 hours, weekly.

### ORG-W09 Run creator discovery and seeding

Trigger: continuous scan, weekly batch for approval; monthly seeding cycle. Human role: a creator/community manager running organic gifting and seeding. Capabilities: ORG-C14, ORG-C49. Needs: product inventory available for gifting, claims tiers, past creator performance, founder budget/scope for seeding.

1. **Scan for new creators.** Scan public Instagram, YouTube and Pinterest posts for creators discussing the category or named competitors without mentioning this brand. Skill: `org/creator-seeding-and-discovery`. Output: candidate list. Check: every candidate is a real public post, not inferred.
2. **Score candidates.** Score category fit, audience size, engagement rate, visual-world match. Skill: `org/creator-seeding-and-discovery`. Output: scored candidates. Check: scoring criteria applied consistently.
3. **Draft personalized outreach.** Draft a note referencing each candidate's actual content. Skill: `org/creator-seeding-and-discovery`. Output: outreach drafts. Check: no copy-paste template.
4. **Batch for founder approval.** Package the week's candidates and drafts for founder sign-off. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: approval batch. Check: nothing sends without approval. Ask the founder if: always, before any outreach sends.
5. **Build the seeding brief.** For approved creators, set SKU, quantity, what to show, deadline, rights ask, disclosure requirement. Skill: `org/creator-seeding-and-discovery`. Output: seeding briefs. Check: every brief has a rights ask on file before shipment.
6. **Track delivery.** Track shipment and delivered content per creator; flag missed deadlines. Skill: `org/creator-seeding-and-discovery`. Output: delivery status. Check: non-delivery is flagged, not dropped.
7. **Feed content to the UGC pipeline.** Hand delivered content with rights status to the UGC repurposing workflow. Skill: `org/ugc-repurposing`. Output: content queued for repurposing. Check: rights status travels with the content.
8. **Log the cycle.** Register cost, creators seeded and content yield. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: cost and yield are real numbers, not estimates.

Outputs: discovery candidates with outreach drafts, seeding briefs, delivered UGC content. QA: No outreach without founder approval; Rights asked for before shipment; Non-delivery tracked. Read point: monthly: cost per piece of usable content vs prior cycles. Duration: agent: continuous scan, 1-2 hours/week; seeding cycle: 2-4 weeks turnaround.

### ORG-W10 Read and report organic content performance

Trigger: monthly. Human role: social media manager reporting what actually worked. Capabilities: ORG-C16. Needs: platform analytics export, metric contract fields, tagged content calendar.

1. **Pull SKU/hook-level numbers.** Pull performance by SKU and hook from the metric contract. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: SKU/hook performance table. Check: missing fields show as unavailable.
2. **Compute the store's own baseline.** Compute the trailing-90-day median in code. Skill: `org/organic-performance-read`. Output: baseline value. Check: computed in code, not estimated.
3. **Flag stranger-blind content.** Flag high reach with no sessions/cart lift. Skill: `org/organic-performance-read`. Output: stranger-blind flags. Check: flag requires both high reach and no lift, not either alone.
4. **Flag broad low-value hits.** Flag high views outside the avatar's demographic where data allows. Skill: `org/organic-performance-read`. Output: low-value flags. Check: flagged only where demographic data actually exists.
5. **Rank top hooks.** Rank top 3 hooks/formats by conversion signal. Skill: `org/organic-performance-read`. Output: top hooks list. Check: ranked by conversion, not raw engagement.
6. **Write the report.** Write what we did, what it did, what is next. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: monthly report. Check: no vanity total leads the report.

Outputs: monthly organic performance report. QA: Comparisons use the store's own baseline; Missing data marked unavailable; No vanity total leads. Read point: monthly, fixed cadence. Duration: agent: half a day.

### ORG-W11 Run social listening for brand, competitor and category trends

Trigger: continuous monitoring, weekly digest. Human role: social media manager tracking category conversation. Capabilities: ORG-C44. Needs: brand and competitor name list, category keyword list, public sources.

1. **Monitor mentions.** Monitor brand, competitor and category mentions across public sources. Skill: `org/social-listening-digest`. Output: raw mention list. Check: sources are public and dated.
2. **Classify mentions.** Classify sentiment, topic, postable idea or risk. Skill: `org/social-listening-digest`. Output: classified mentions. Check: every mention gets a classification.
3. **Track week-over-week trends.** Compare this week's counts to last week's to catch a real shift. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: trend deltas. Check: trend claims require a week-over-week pattern.
4. **Draft the digest.** Write trends, sentiment shifts, 3-5 postable ideas. Skill: `org/social-listening-digest`. Output: weekly digest. Check: every idea traces to a quoted, dated mention.
5. **Route risk flags.** Send anything reading as PR/reputation risk to the founder same-day, apart from the digest. Skill: `org/social-listening-digest`. Output: risk flags. Check: routed same-day, not held for the weekly cycle. Ask the founder if: a risk flag is found.
6. **Log the digest.** Register the week's digest and any risk flags in the registry for the monthly rollup. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: digest and flags both logged with a date.

Outputs: weekly listening digest, risk flags. QA: Trend claims based on a real pattern; Every idea sourced; Risk flags routed same-day. Read point: weekly digest, monthly trend rollup. Duration: agent: 1-2 hours/week, continuous monitoring in background.

### ORG-W12 Run Pinterest search and board strategy

Trigger: on new content ready for Pinterest, plus a monthly board/trend review. Human role: SEO/content manager treating Pinterest as visual search. Capabilities: ORG-C45. Needs: approved image/video for the pin, keyword/question map, current board architecture.

3. **Pick or create the board.** Match to a board titled in buyer search terms. Skill: `org/make-content/pinterest-pin`. Output: board assignment. Check: board title uses buyer language, not internal jargon.

6. **Log the pin and review.** Add the pin to the content calendar and register the monthly board review. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: both the pin and the board review are logged.

Outputs: pin drafts, monthly board review. QA: Title/description carry the search phrase; Destination confirmed live; No board without a signal. Read point: monthly, impressions and saves per board. Duration: agent: 20-30 minutes per pin; monthly review: 1-2 hours.

### ORG-W17 Set the SEO and AEO work roadmap and priorities

Trigger: on demand. Human role: an SEO manager deciding whether and where to spend effort at all. Capabilities: ORG-C17. Needs: category demand signal, margin per SKU, technical floor status, idea bank filtered by bottleneck.

1. **Check category demand.** Check search volume trend and category growth for the target category. Skill: `org/seo-aeo-roadmap`. Output: demand signal. Check: demand signal is dated and sourced.
2. **Check margin.** Check per-SKU margin for the category before recommending spend. Skill: `org/seo-aeo-roadmap`. Output: margin check. Check: low-margin categories are not recommended for a push.
3. **Check the technical floor.** Check current indexing/crawlability status. Skill: `org/seo-aeo-roadmap`. Output: technical floor status. Check: content-cluster work is never sequenced ahead of a failing floor.
4. **Filter the idea bank.** Filter and rank ideas by bottleneck and store tags. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked idea candidates. Check: ranking done in code.
5. **Sequence the work.** Sequence technical floor, then money pages, then one cluster at a time, then AEO. Skill: `org/seo-aeo-roadmap`. Output: sequenced roadmap. Check: sequence follows the fixed order.
6. **Write the expectation memo.** State a realistic timeline in months. Skill: `org/seo-aeo-roadmap`. Output: expectation memo. Check: timeline is realistic, not inflated.
7. **Run a founder-question round.** Ask what the data cannot settle. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: question round. Check: no question the data could already answer.
8. **Package the founder card.** Present the roadmap for a decision. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder decision card. Check: card is under 4 sentences plus pills.

Outputs: sequenced SEO/AEO roadmap, expectation memo. QA: No cluster work before the floor passes; Every recommendation ties to a demand/margin number; Timeline is realistic. Read point: quarterly review of the roadmap against actual progress. Duration: agent: 1-2 days; founder decision: 1 week.

### ORG-W19 Run the technical SEO and AI-crawlability audit into developer briefs

Trigger: quarterly. Human role: an SEO manager running the technical floor audit. Capabilities: ORG-C20. Needs: live site URL, prior audit baseline, robots.txt/CDN settings, Site's speed baseline.

1. **Crawl the site.** Crawl with mobile and JS rendering for indexing, canonical, sitemap, robots, redirect and heading problems. Skill: `shared/surface-audit`. Output: raw crawl findings. Check: crawl covers the whole site, mobile and JS-rendered.
2. **Check AI-crawler access.** Check GPTBot, PerplexityBot, Google-Extended access against robots.txt and CDN rules. Skill: `org/technical-crawl-audit`. Output: AI-crawler access report. Check: checked explicitly, not assumed from classic crawl results.
3. **Log the speed baseline.** Log current speed numbers without diagnosing them. Skill: `org/technical-crawl-audit`. Output: speed baseline log. Check: logged only, not diagnosed here.
4. **Order findings by the technical floor.** Order findings with indexing/crawlability first. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ordered findings. Check: floor items rank first.
5. **Attach evidence.** Attach a URL, response code, screenshot or quoted setting to every finding. Skill: `org/technical-crawl-audit`. Output: evidenced findings. Check: every finding is independently verifiable.
6. **Split fixable-here vs Site hand-off.** Split findings this agent can brief directly from speed items for Site. Skill: `org/technical-crawl-audit`. Output: split lists. Check: speed items are only handed off, never fixed here.
7. **Write developer briefs.** Write a developer brief per fixable finding. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: developer briefs. Check: every brief has a rollback step.
8. **Write the Site hand-off note.** Package speed findings for the Site specialist. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: hand-off note. Check: note is addressed to Site, not left ambiguous.

Outputs: prioritized fix list, AI-crawler access report, developer briefs, Site hand-off note. QA: Every finding has verifiable evidence; AI-crawler access checked explicitly; Speed items only handed off. Read point: next quarterly audit, re-checking each prior finding's fix status. Duration: agent: 1-2 days; sub-agent crawl: several hours in background.

1. **Pull the numbers.** Pull sessions, clicks, impressions, CTR, position, organic revenue from the contract. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: numbers table. Check: missing fields shown as unavailable.
2. **Lead with revenue.** Structure the report to lead with organic revenue, not traffic. Skill: `org/seo-reporting-and-diagnosis`. Output: revenue-led draft. Check: revenue is the first number shown.
