3. **Diagnose any drop.** Check CTR, device mismatch, engagement, position in that order for any drop. Skill: `org/seo-reporting-and-diagnosis`. Output: diagnosis. Check: diagnosis names a specific mechanism with evidence.
4. **Compare to the prior month.** Format as what we did, what it did, what is next. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: formatted report. Check: format matches the shared shape exactly.
5. **Package and send.** Finalize the monthly report. Skill: `none`. Output: monthly SEO report. Check: reading level under grade 10.
6. **Log the report.** Register the report and its numbers for next month's comparison. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: numbers stored match the contract exactly.

Outputs: monthly SEO report with diagnosis. QA: Report leads with revenue; Diagnosis names a specific mechanism; Missing data marked unavailable. Read point: monthly, fixed cadence. Duration: agent: half a day.

### ORG-W28 Vet SEO, link and AEO vendors before hiring

Trigger: on demand. Human role: an SEO manager screening a vendor pitch before the founder signs. Capabilities: ORG-C33. Needs: vendor pitch/proposal, vendor's claimed case studies, proposed tactics.

1. **List every vendor claim.** Extract every claim the vendor makes. Skill: `org/vendor-vetting`. Output: claim list. Check: no claim skipped.
2. **Check for red flags.** Check each claim against known red flags (guarantees, PBNs, link farms). Skill: `org/vendor-vetting`. Output: red flags found. Check: checked against a real red-flag list, not a gut feel.
3. **Verify case studies.** Verify at least 2 claimed case studies independently. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: verification results. Check: verified where possible, treated as a snapshot.
4. **Check tactics against platform policy.** Check proposed tactics against current spam policies. Skill: `org/vendor-vetting`. Output: policy risk check. Check: checked against current policy.
5. **Check AEO-specific vendors.** Check whether an AEO/GEO vendor runs a real repeatable audit or a one-off scan. Skill: `org/vendor-vetting`. Output: AEO method check. Check: distinguishes a repeatable method from a one-off resold as ongoing.
6. **Score and recommend.** Score the vendor with a specific reason. Skill: `org/vendor-vetting`. Output: scorecard. Check: reason is specific, not a gut feel.
7. **Package the founder card.** Present the scorecard before any signature. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder decision card. Check: card presented before any contract step.

Outputs: vendor scorecard. QA: Every red flag checked; Case studies verified where possible; Recommendation reason is specific. Read point: if hired: 90-day check against the vendor's own stated claims. Duration: agent: 1 day.

### ORG-W29 Run the answer-engine probe, fix the facts page, and correct wrong answers

Trigger: monthly (probe and facts page), event: wrong fact found (correction). Human role: an AI-visibility owner keeping AI answers about the brand accurate. Capabilities: ORG-C34, ORG-C35, ORG-C36, ORG-C37. Needs: frozen prompt set, entity and competitor list, brand facts (approved tier), prior month's citation log.

1. **Run the frozen prompt set.** Run each prompt on ChatGPT, Gemini, Perplexity, Google AI by hand. Skill: `org/ai-visibility-probe`. Output: raw per-prompt results. Check: every prompt run on every prioritized platform.
2. **Classify each result.** Classify named/cited-with-link/cited-without-link/absent, and retrieval vs memory. Skill: `org/ai-visibility-probe`. Output: classified results. Check: classification never collapses to a single rank number.
3. **Check persistence.** Recheck last month's citations to confirm they held. Skill: `org/ai-visibility-probe`. Output: persistence check. Check: actually re-run, not assumed.
4. **Classify gaps.** Classify each gap: missing, wrong, outdated, no coverage. Skill: `org/ai-visibility-probe`. Output: gap list. Check: every gap has supporting evidence.
5. **Build or refresh the facts page.** Draft missing fact blocks answering the gaps, atomic and tier-checked. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: facts page blocks. Check: each block is atomic and sourced.
6. **Check cross-surface consistency.** Check the same fact across site, schema, feed, directories, reviews. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: consistency check. Check: the same fact reads identically everywhere.
7. **Fix a wrong fact at the source.** On a wrong fact found, fix the site first. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: site fix. Check: site fixed before any outreach starts.
8. **Draft publisher correction outreach.** Identify the likely publisher source and draft outreach. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: correction outreach draft. Check: targets the actual likely source, not a guess.
9. **Rewrite a page for citation, if needed.** For an existing high-value page an engine should cite but cannot, rewrite it answer-first. Skill: `org/aeo-page-rewrite`. Output: rewritten page with readiness score. Check: page still reads naturally, not fragmented.
10. **Schedule a recheck.** Schedule re-running the engines after the fix. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: recheck date. Check: a real future date is set, not left open-ended.
11. **Write the monthly report.** Write share-of-voice trend and top 3 gaps with recommended fixes. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: AI visibility report. Check: never reduces to a single rank number.

Outputs: AI visibility report, facts page draft/refresh, correction plan, rewritten page (as needed). QA: Report never reduces to a single rank number; Every citation verified directly; Correction always follows site-then-publisher-then-web order. Read point: the scheduled recheck date, and the next monthly cycle. Duration: agent: 1-2 days; sub-agent probing: several hours across platforms.

### ORG-W30 Audit AI shopping agents against the claims tiers

Trigger: weekly. Human role: an AI-visibility owner checking what shopping agents actually tell a buyer. Capabilities: LOOP-C38. Needs: Agentic Storefront query access, claims tiers, live product feed, frozen agentic prompt set.

1. **Query the agentic surfaces.** Query ChatGPT, Copilot, Google AI Mode, Gemini shopping with the frozen prompt set. Skill: `org/agentic-shopping-audit`. Output: raw agentic responses. Check: every prompt run on every listed surface.
2. **Record stated facts.** Record price, stock, shipping and product claims stated by each agent. Skill: `org/agentic-shopping-audit`. Output: stated fact log. Check: recorded verbatim, not paraphrased.
3. **Check against the live feed and claims tiers.** Compare stated facts to the live feed and claims tiers. Skill: `org/agentic-shopping-audit`. Output: mismatch list. Check: checked against the live feed, not a cached value.
4. **Route mismatches.** Route price/stock mismatches to the feed fix path, claim mismatches to fact-correction. Skill: `org/fact-consistency-management`. Output: routed fixes. Check: each mismatch routed to the correct owner.
5. **Log for trend tracking.** Log the week's results against prior weeks. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: trend log entry. Check: logged weekly, not as a one-off.
6. **Escalate a repeat mismatch.** If the same mismatch appears 2 weeks running, escalate to the founder since the routed fix did not take. Skill: `none`. Output: escalation note. Check: escalation triggers only on a real repeat, not a single week. Ask the founder if: the same mismatch repeats for 2 consecutive weeks.

Outputs: agentic audit log, routed fixes. QA: Checked against the live feed, not cached; Mismatches routed correctly; Logged weekly for trend. Read point: weekly, plus a monthly trend rollup with the AI-visibility report. Duration: agent: 2-3 hours/week.

## Product and supply (PROD), 30 workflows

### PROD-W01 Screen and validate a new product candidate

Trigger: on demand | scheduled weekly (bank sweep). Human role: Product researcher doing first-pass triage before any real research time is spent. Capabilities: PROD-C01, PROD-C02, PROD-C03, PROD-C04, PROD-C05. Needs: brand brain: store type and category tags, idea bank filtered to 01-PRD, candidate name or category, a stated time-box per candidate.

1. **Log the candidate and start the clock.** Open a research-log entry for the candidate with a hard time-box (default 2 hours). Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: research log entry with a deadline. Check: the entry has a stop time before any research starts.
2. **Run the fast screen and read trend against saturation.** Check category size, pain evidence, scarcity and store-model fit against the fixed checklist; pull the multi-year trend line and count existing sellers in the same pass. Skill: `prod/screen-candidate`. Output: pass/caution/reject with reasons, plus a trend-and-saturation read. Check: every check has a stated reason and the trend line covers at least 2 years.
3. **Validate demand on video-commerce and social-native platforms.** If the candidate is trend-flagged, check its demand signal on TikTok-style shop data and native ad feeds so one viral clip is not mistaken for proof. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: demand validation note. Check: the signal is seen on 2 or more independent sources, not one clip.
4. **Scout ad libraries for sustained competitor spend.** Search public ad libraries and spy tools for products with real, sustained spend in the category, and read what gap the incumbents leave. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: competitor scouting sheet (words and numbers only). Check: no competitor asset, text, logo or product image is copied, only signal notes.
5. **Mine reviews on the closest listings for the price buyers actually pay.** Read reviews on the nearest DTC and Amazon listings for price complaints and willingness-to-pay signals. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: price-signal note. Check: every price signal quotes its source review.
6. **Delegate repetitive search steps and close the time box.** Hand repeatable search legwork to a sub-agent pass so the time box is not spent typing searches by hand; stop at the deadline regardless of state. Skill: `none`. Output: closed time-box log. Check: the stop time from step 1 was respected.
7. **Score the candidate and log the verdict.** Combine the screen, demand validation, scouting sheet and price signal into one verdict: pass, caution or reject, with the reason for each part. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: scored candidate record. Check: the verdict traces to at least one concrete piece of evidence per part.
8. **Hand off pass and caution candidates.** Send a pass or caution candidate to the differentiation workflow (PROD-W02) and the economics gate (PROD-W03); log a one-line reason for any reject. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff note to PROD-W02 and PROD-W03. Check: every candidate has exactly one next step named.

Outputs: candidate screen record, demand validation note, competitor scouting sheet, price-signal note. QA: every check has a stated reason; no competitor asset copied, signal only; the time box was respected and logged; no candidate reaches sourcing spend without this screen. Read point: at hand-off: whether the candidate proceeds to differentiation and economics within the time-box window. Duration: agent time under 2 hours per candidate; no external wait.

### PROD-W02 Extend the line and sharpen the differentiation angle

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Product manager planning the next SKUs and the cash plan behind them. Capabilities: PROD-C06, PROD-C07. Needs: brand brain: hero product performance and SKU list, differentiation angle gap from PROD-W01, sales history by SKU, cash position from Finance.

1. **Pull the hero product's performance and the category default.** Read the hero SKU's sales, margin and reviews, and note what the category's default product looks like. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: performance snapshot. Check: numbers come from the metric contract, not memory.
2. **Name the one differentiating thing.** State the single feature or angle that differs from the category default, backed by the gap already scouted in PROD-W01. Skill: `prod/differentiation-angle`. Output: one-line differentiation statement. Check: the angle traces to a real, cited competitor gap, not an assumption.
3. **Draft 2 to 3 line-extension options.** Propose SKU options that build on the differentiation angle rather than copying the hero product. Skill: `prod/differentiation-angle`. Output: line-extension options. Check: each option states what it adds over the hero SKU.
4. **Tag the demand pattern for each option.** Classify each option evergreen, trending or seasonal using sales history and category norms. Skill: `prod/differentiation-angle`. Output: demand-pattern tag per option. Check: the tag has a stated evidence source.
5. **Map seasonal and gifting spikes.** Plot any seasonal or gifting spike against the calendar for options tagged seasonal. Skill: `none`. Output: spike map. Check: spikes are read from at least one full prior cycle where data exists.
6. **Check whether a subscription is earned.** Look for real repeat-purchase evidence before proposing a subscription model; otherwise flag the SKU a one-time novelty. Skill: `prod/differentiation-angle`. Output: subscription-fit verdict. Check: the verdict is based on repeat_rate_90d/365d, not founder preference.
7. **Size the cash and stock plan the pattern implies.** Compute, in code, the cash and stock commitment implied by the demand pattern (e.g., pre-buy for a seasonal spike). Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: cash/stock implication note. Check: every number is computed, not estimated.
8. **Log and hand off.** Log the differentiation brief and demand-pattern tags to the registry and hand the SKU options to the economics gate. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: differentiation brief. Check: the brief is complete enough for PROD-W03 to run without asking a question.

Outputs: differentiation brief, demand-pattern tags, cash/stock implication note. QA: angle traces to a cited gap, not an invented claim; demand tag has evidence; cash numbers computed in code. Read point: when the SKU reaches the economics gate: whether the angle holds at the compliance and margin check. Duration: agent time under 1 hour; no external wait.

### PROD-W03 Model economics, run the compliance gate and decide go/no-go

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Product manager running the funding decision with the founder. Capabilities: PROD-C08, PROD-C10, PROD-C11. Needs: candidate or differentiation brief, cost basis (unit cost, freight, duty estimate), target price, the brand's own margin floor, claims and compliance category, expansion market data if relevant.

1. **Pull cost basis, target price and the brand's margin floor.** Read the real cost inputs and the brand's own floor, never a course number or industry rule of thumb. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: cost and floor snapshot. Check: the floor is the brand's own, cited to where it was set.
2. **Run the margin and compliance gate in code.** Compute price, shipping, shelf-life and regulatory checks together as one pass/fail gate. Skill: `prod/margin-compliance-gate`. Output: gate result with the full number breakdown. Check: a missing input blocks the gate rather than defaulting to an assumption.
3. **Score expansion markets if relevant.** If the candidate targets more than the home market, score each market's gate result separately. Skill: `prod/margin-compliance-gate`. Output: per-market gate result. Check: each market is gated on its own numbers, not the home market's.
4. **Apply the remaining go-live gates.** Check provable claims, an owned point of difference, the brand's own thresholds, contribution margin and ad-cost headroom together. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: go-live gate result. Check: every claim used in the gate is in the approved or site-backed tier.
5. **Rank surviving candidates.** If more than one candidate cleared the gates, rank them on the fixed scorecard. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: ranked shortlist with score parts visible. Check: no arithmetic done in the model; source carries no extra weight.
6. **Write the one-page go/no-go memo.** State the candidate, the evidence, the cost, what it could move on the metric contract, and the gate results. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: go/no-go memo. Check: reading level under grade 10; every number in the memo is in the record.
7. **Set the pre-set review date.** For anything recommended for approval, set the date the result gets checked. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: review date on the registry entry. Check: the review date is a real calendar date or a count, not "soon".
8. **Send to the founder gate and log the decision.** Send the card with the 3 buttons; log whatever the founder decides, with their reason verbatim. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged decision. Check: the founder's reason is stored verbatim, not paraphrased.

Outputs: margin-gate result, compliance flag list, scored shortlist, go/no-go memo. QA: every input has a source; missing inputs blocked the gate; claims traced to tiers; a review date is set for anything approved. Read point: the pre-set review date logged on approval. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours; founder decision adds 1 to 3 days wait.

### PROD-W04 Run field research and hand the validated angle to creative

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Product researcher grounding the angle in real shopper behaviour before Creative builds on it. Capabilities: PROD-C12. Needs: approved or near-approved candidate, avatar draft from Brand strategist, a stated field-research time budget.

1. **Define the field-research question.** State exactly what shopper behaviour would confirm or kill the current angle. Skill: `none`. Output: one research question. Check: the question is answerable within the time budget.
2. **Run the field pass within its time limit.** Read interviews, forums, reviews and in-market signals for the question, stopping at the time limit. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: raw field notes. Check: the time limit was not exceeded.
3. **Extract the validated detail.** Pull out the avatar detail, hook language and any onboarding idea that actually surfaced in real shopper words. Skill: `none`. Output: validated avatar/hook/onboarding notes. Check: every note quotes or closely paraphrases a real source.
4. **Check against the differentiation brief.** Flag any contradiction between the field finding and the differentiation brief from PROD-W02. Skill: `none`. Output: contradiction flag or clear pass. Check: a contradiction is never silently dropped.
5. **Package the handoff note.** Write the validated angle, hook and onboarding idea as a short handoff, with the source evidence attached. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: handoff note. Check: reading level under grade 10; every claim in the note has a source.
6. **Send the handoff and log it.** Send the note to Paid creative and copy, and to Site for any onboarding idea; log the handoff. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged handoff. Check: the receiving agent and date are both recorded.

Outputs: validated angle handoff note. QA: every finding traces to a real shopper source; the time limit was respected. Read point: when Creative reports whether the angle tested; the read is logged by CREA and referenced here. Duration: agent time 1 to 3 hours depending on field access.

### PROD-W05 Build the tech pack, BOM, packaging spec and durability test

Trigger: event: new SKU or spec change | event: new packaging design. Human role: Product/QA manager writing the exact spec a factory builds to. Capabilities: PROD-C13, PROD-C71. Needs: approved candidate or SKU, reference sample if one exists, target unit cost, packaging requirements (drop height, transit mode).

1. **Write measurements with tolerance bands.** Specify every physical dimension with a tolerance, never a bare number. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: dimensioned spec with tolerances. Check: every measurement has a tolerance band.
2. **Build the component-level BOM.** List every component with its own spec and target cost. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: bill of materials. Check: every component has a spec and a target cost.
3. **Price packaging as its own cost line.** Cost packaging separately from the product, never folded into a single unit cost. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: packaging cost line. Check: packaging appears as its own line, not blended into unit cost.
4. **Version the tech pack.** Assign a version number and log it against the SKU record. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: versioned tech pack. Check: the version number is unique and traceable to the SKU.
5. **Book the packaging durability test.** Book the drop-test, tamper-evidence and transit-durability test the design needs before production. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: booked test. Check: the test is booked before any production run, not after.
6. **Track the test result.** Record pass, fail or marginal against the stated pass criteria. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: test result. Check: the result states which specific criterion failed, if any.
7. **Update and re-book on failure.** If the packaging fails, revise the spec, bump the version, and re-book the test. Skill: `prod/build-tech-pack`. Output: revised, re-versioned spec. Check: no unversioned change is made to a failed spec.
8. **Hand off to sourcing.** Send the versioned, tested tech pack to sourcing for RFQ. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: handoff to PROD-W07. Check: the tech pack handed off has a passed durability result or an explicit waiver reason.

Outputs: versioned tech pack, BOM, packaging spec with cost line, packaging test result. QA: every measurement has a tolerance; packaging is its own cost line; the durability test is booked before production, not after. Read point: when the packaging test result returns: pass, hold or redesign. Duration: agent time 2 to 4 hours; packaging lab turnaround 3 to 10 days.

### PROD-W06 Discover, vet, watch and score suppliers, and monitor commodity costs

Trigger: on demand (new supplier) | weekly background scan | monthly (scorecard, commodity watch). Human role: Sourcing manager running the full supplier relationship from first search to the ongoing scorecard. Capabilities: PROD-C14, PROD-C68, PROD-C69, PROD-C18. Needs: tech pack or open spec, MOQ needs, supplier region preference (India or China), existing supplier list if any.

1. **Pick the region and run the discovery funnel.** Run the region-specific discovery procedure: broad platform search against the open spec. Skill: `prod/source`. Output: long list of candidate suppliers. Check: the funnel used the region's own primary platform, not a generic search.
2. **Message a wide shortlist on the same spec.** Contact the shortlist with identical spec and terms so responses are comparable. Skill: `prod/source`. Output: response tracker. Check: every message sent used the same spec text.
3. **Verify with a video call and certification check.** Verify the top responders with a live video call and a certification document check. Skill: `prod/source`. Output: verified shortlist. Check: no supplier is added to the verified list on claims alone.
4. **Vet any sourcing agent the same way.** Apply the same verification standard to a sourcing agent as to a factory. Skill: `prod/source`. Output: agent vetting result. Check: an agent is vetted, never assumed reliable because a factory recommended them.
5. **Keep the spec running in the background.** Leave the open spec live against IndiaMART, Alibaba and Global Sources for a better or new supplier. Skill: `prod/source`. Output: background watch alert. Check: the watch only alerts; it never contacts a supplier on its own.
6. **Score active suppliers monthly.** Score every active supplier on on-time delivery, defect rate, responsiveness and price stability. Skill: `prod/supplier-relationship-monitor`. Output: supplier scorecard. Check: the scorecard uses real delivery and defect data, not opinion.
7. **Track commodity input costs.** Track the priced-in raw materials behind each SKU on a set cadence. Skill: `prod/supplier-relationship-monitor`. Output: commodity price-move flag. Check: the flag names the exact input and the price move.
8. **Flag when a cost-down is not worth the risk.** Compare a possible cost-down against the relationship-risk cost of pushing too hard. Skill: `prod/supplier-relationship-monitor`. Output: risk-weighted recommendation. Check: the recommendation states the risk it is weighing, not just the saving.
9. **Log everything.** Log the shortlist, scorecard, commodity flag and any risk recommendation to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged supplier record. Check: every entry links to the prior entry for the same supplier.

Outputs: verified supplier shortlist, supplier scorecard, commodity price-move flag, new-supplier alert. QA: every supplier verified by video call and certification, not claims alone; scorecard uses real data; the background watch never contacts a supplier automatically. Read point: monthly scorecard review; the alert's read point is when the founder decides to open a conversation. Duration: agent time 2 to 6 hours for a discovery pass; the weekly scan runs unattended; human calls add days.

### PROD-W07 Draft the RFQ, compare quotes and confirm landed cost

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Sourcing manager turning a spec into a purchasing decision. Capabilities: PROD-C15, MONEY-C03. Needs: versioned tech pack, supplier shortlist, volume tiers to quote, freight and duty assumptions.

1. **Translate the spec into a structured RFQ.** Turn the tech pack into an RFQ with tolerance bands stated plainly. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: RFQ document. Check: every measurement in the RFQ matches the tech pack's tolerance.
2. **Send the RFQ on identical terms.** Send to the shortlist with the same terms and the same deadline. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: sent RFQ log. Check: every supplier received the same terms and deadline.
3. **Collect quotes across volume tiers.** Gather quotes for each stated volume tier from every responding supplier. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: raw quotes table. Check: every quote covers every requested volume tier or states why not.
4. **Build landed cost per SKU in code.** Compute product, freight, duty and packaging cost with a backup test for every cost line. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: landed cost model per volume tier. Check: every cost line has a source and a backup-tested check.
5. **Compare cost and cash impact across tiers.** Compare landed cost per unit and the cash impact of each volume tier. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: tier comparison sheet. Check: the comparison shows cash impact, not just unit cost.
6. **Flag incomplete quotes.** Flag any quote missing a required certification or term. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: quote flag list. Check: a flagged quote is excluded from the recommendation until resolved.
7. **Recommend a supplier and tier.** Recommend a supplier and volume tier with the full numbers shown. Skill: `prod/rfq-quote-compare`. Output: sourcing recommendation. Check: the recommendation names the numbers it is based on.
8. **Hand landed cost to Money and Ops.** Hand the landed cost model to Money and Ops to confirm and own for the brand's P&L. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: landed cost handed to MONEY-C03. Check: Money confirms receipt before the model is treated as final.

Outputs: RFQ document, quote comparison sheet, landed cost model per volume tier. QA: every cost line has a backup-tested source; quotes compared on identical terms; landed cost computed in code, never estimated. Read point: when Money confirms the landed cost model for the P&L. Duration: agent time 2 to 3 hours; supplier quote turnaround 3 to 10 days.

### PROD-W08 Run the sample gate, negotiate the price and book the production slot

Trigger: event: sample received. Human role: Sourcing/QA manager gating the sample and locking the deal. Capabilities: PROD-C16, PROD-C17, PROD-C70. Needs: approved reference sample (in-house and at factory), tech pack, the new sample, backup factory quote if negotiating, competitor teardown if available.

1. **Confirm the reference sample exists.** Check the approved reference sample is in-house before any comparison starts. Skill: `prod/sample-qc-gate`. Output: confirmed reference. Check: no comparison starts without a confirmed in-house reference.
2. **Run the 3-stage gate.** Compare the new sample against the reference side by side across the 3 stages. Skill: `prod/sample-qc-gate`. Output: stage-by-stage comparison. Check: every stage records a pass, hold or reject.
3. **Score communication and reliability.** Score the factory's communication and reliability shown during the sample round. Skill: `prod/sample-qc-gate`. Output: reliability score. Check: the score is based on response time and accuracy, not tone.
4. **Record the deviation.** Record the exact deviation for any hold or reject, in measurable terms. Skill: `prod/sample-qc-gate`. Output: deviation record. Check: the deviation is stated as a measurement, not a feeling.
5. **Build negotiating leverage.** If the sample passed, build real leverage: a vetted alternate factory, a competitor teardown, the category's normal factory margin. Skill: `prod/negotiate-cost-down`. Output: leverage brief. Check: the leverage is real (a vetted alternate exists), not a bluff.
6. **Ask for the cost-down.** Present the ask with the leverage stated plainly, not as a bare request. Skill: `prod/negotiate-cost-down`. Output: negotiation outcome. Check: the ask names the specific leverage used.
7. **Confirm price and book the slot.** Confirm the final price and terms, then book the production slot at the exact factory. Skill: `prod/negotiate-cost-down`. Output: booked production slot. Check: the slot is booked at the exact factory that passed the sample, not a sister factory.
8. **Track the slot weekly.** Check the booked slot is still on schedule until production starts. Skill: `none`. Output: slot status. Check: a slip is flagged the week it becomes known, not after.
9. **Log the decision.** Log the sample decision, negotiated price and slot booking to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged sourcing decision. Check: no creative shoot, price commitment or launch date is set before this log exists.

Outputs: stage status and deviation notes, negotiation brief, confirmed price and terms, booked production slot. QA: reference sample existed before comparison; no downstream commitment before the sample clears; leverage is real, not a bluff. Read point: when the booked slot's production start date arrives; a slip is flagged the day it is known. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours per stage; sample shipping and factory response add 1 to 3 weeks.

### PROD-W09 Structure payment terms and hand PO governance to Finance

Trigger: event: deal close. Human role: Sourcing manager setting the deal's cash terms and governance. Capabilities: PROD-C19, MONEY-C37. Needs: confirmed price and terms, supplier relationship history, brand cash position from Finance.

1. **Choose the payment route.** Pick wire, LC, trade assurance or escrow based on the supplier relationship's history and risk. Skill: `prod/payment-terms-structure`. Output: chosen payment route. Check: the route matches the relationship's actual risk level, not a default.
2. **Structure the split and MOQ.** Set the advance, balance and retention split, and confirm the MOQ. Skill: `prod/payment-terms-structure`. Output: payment split and MOQ. Check: the split is stated as percentages tied to milestones.
3. **Compute float, cash-locked figure and financing cost.** Compute, in code, the float days, the cash locked up, and any financing cost. Skill: `prod/payment-terms-structure`. Output: cash/MOQ packet. Check: every figure is computed, not estimated.
4. **Hand the numbers to Finance.** Send the full cash/MOQ packet to Finance at deal close. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: packet delivered to Finance. Check: Finance confirms receipt before the deal proceeds.
5. **Gate the PO on the tech pack version.** Confirm the PO cites the exact, current tech pack version, never a verbal spec. Skill: `none`. Output: version-gated PO. Check: the PO references a specific tech pack version number.
6. **Assign one owner and one date per step.** Confirm one named owner and one date for every step on the production critical path. Skill: `none`. Output: owned critical path. Check: every step has exactly one owner and one date.
7. **Route communication through one channel.** Route all supplier communication for this PO through one logged channel. Skill: `none`. Output: logged channel. Check: no side-channel communication about this PO exists unlogged.
8. **Log the governed PO.** Log the PO and its governance record to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: governed PO record. Check: the record links the PO, the tech pack version and the owner list.

Outputs: payment terms recommendation, cash/MOQ packet for Finance, governed PO record. QA: every float day and financing cost computed, not estimated; the PO cites the exact tech pack version; one owner and one date per production step. Read point: at Finance's cash review, and at each production-step date. Duration: agent time under 1 hour; Finance review adds 1 to 2 days.

### PROD-W10 Run pre-shipment inspection and choose freight and 3PL

Trigger: event: shipment ready. Human role: Sourcing/logistics manager clearing a shipment for departure. Capabilities: PROD-C20, PROD-C21. Needs: production run details, defect history for this SKU, shipment weight and volume, launch date, cash cover for freight mode.

1. **Book third-party inspection.** Book third-party inspection before the container leaves the factory. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: booked inspection. Check: inspection is booked before the shipment date, not after.
2. **Size the defect buffer from real history.** Size the acceptable defect buffer from this SKU's own defect history, not a flat industry percentage. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: defect buffer. Check: the buffer cites the SKU's own historical defect rate.
3. **Record disposition decisions.** Record what happens to every defective unit found: rework, discount, discard. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: disposition log. Check: every defective unit has a recorded decision.
4. **Pick the freight mode.** Choose air, sea or a hybrid by weight, launch date and cash cover. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: freight mode decision. Check: the decision states the weight/date/cash trade-off it made.
5. **Select the 3PL on service history.** Choose the 3PL on service history, not the lowest quote. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: 3PL selection. Check: the selection cites the 3PL's on-time and damage-rate history.
6. **Check cross-border thresholds.** Check duty thresholds and paperwork requirements before committing. Skill: `prod/shipment-readiness`. Output: threshold check result. Check: the check is done before the shipment is booked, not after.
7. **Clear the shipment to leave.** Confirm the shipment is cleared, with the inspection result attached. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: clearance confirmation. Check: clearance is recorded with the inspection result attached, not separately.
8. **Log the choices.** Log the freight/3PL choice and inspection result to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged shipment record. Check: the record links to the PO it ships against.

Outputs: inspection result and disposition log, freight/3PL recommendation. QA: defect buffer sized from real history; 3PL chosen on service history, not price alone; cross-border threshold checked before commit. Read point: on arrival: whether the defect rate matched the inspection's estimate. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours; inspection turnaround 1 to 3 days.

### PROD-W11 Audit the sourcing base: onboarding, billing anomalies and defect CAPA

Trigger: event: onboarding or new client | per billing cycle | on defect complaint above a threshold. Human role: Sourcing/ops manager running standing governance over the whole supplier and courier base. Capabilities: PROD-C24, PROD-C67, MONEY-C70, PROD-C73. Needs: full supplier and SKU list, contracted rates for suppliers, couriers and 3PLs, current billing statements, tech pack version history, defect complaint records.

1. **Audit the whole sourcing base once.** On onboarding, audit every active supplier, term and price in one pass. Skill: `prod/sourcing-audit`. Output: onboarding audit report. Check: every active supplier appears in the audit, none skipped.
2. **Set the PO cadence to the SKU count.** At high SKU count, move purchase orders from a weekly to a daily cadence. Skill: `prod/sourcing-audit`. Output: PO cadence setting. Check: the cadence change is logged with the SKU-count threshold that triggered it.
3. **Watch bills against contracted rates.** Watch every recurring supplier and courier bill against its contracted rate, in code. Skill: `prod/sourcing-audit`. Output: billing check result. Check: the check compares against the contracted rate, not last month's bill.
4. **Flag anomalies the month they appear.** Flag a quiet rate increase, a duplicate charge or a new surcharge the same billing cycle it appears. Skill: `prod/sourcing-audit`. Output: billing anomaly flag. Check: the flag is raised within the same billing cycle.
5. **Track 3PL and warehouse SLA daily.** Track 3PL and warehouse SLA performance daily against the written SLA. Skill: `prod/sourcing-audit`. Output: SLA tracking log. Check: a breach is flagged the day it happens, not at month end.
6. **Trace a defect to its batch.** When a defect complaint crosses the threshold, trace it to the exact production batch or lot using the tech pack's versioning. Skill: `prod/defect-capa-trace`. Output: batch trace. Check: the trace names the exact batch or lot, not just the SKU.
7. **Run the CAPA loop.** Run the corrective-action loop with the supplier: root cause, fix, verification. Skill: `prod/defect-capa-trace`. Output: CAPA record. Check: the CAPA has a verified fix, not just a supplier promise.
8. **Log everything.** Log every anomaly, SLA breach and CAPA outcome to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged governance record. Check: every entry links back to the specific supplier, courier or 3PL.

Outputs: onboarding audit report, billing anomaly flag, SLA breach flag, batch trace and CAPA record. QA: every anomaly computed against the contracted rate, not a feeling; batch trace uses the tech pack's own versioning; CAPA has a verified fix. Read point: the CAPA's verification date; the next billing cycle for anomaly recurrence. Duration: agent time 2 to 4 hours for onboarding audit; billing/SLA checks run unattended weekly.

### PROD-W12 Compute reorder points and manage DTC and Amazon FBA replenishment

Trigger: weekly. Human role: Inventory planner sizing every SKU's reorder point and channel-specific replenishment. Capabilities: PROD-C22, PROD-C39. Needs: SKU velocity by channel, lead time per supplier, safety-stock policy, channel push plan (promotions, launches), Amazon IPI and storage limits if applicable.

1. **Pull real velocity and lead time.** Pull real velocity and lead time per SKU per channel; a subscription or bundle SKU uses committed cadence, not trailing sales. Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: velocity/lead-time table. Check: velocity and lead time are real numbers, not defaults.
2. **Compute reorder point and safety stock.** Compute reorder point and safety stock in code, sized against India-specific spike risk (festive demand, COD RTO). Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: reorder point and safety stock per SKU. Check: the spike-risk adjustment is stated, not silently folded in.
3. **Compare current stock to the reorder point.** Compare current stock against the computed point and flag stockout risk. Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: stockout-risk flag. Check: every SKU below its reorder point is flagged.
4. **Size non-routine orders.** Size any non-routine order from the committed channel push plan (a promotion or launch). Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: non-routine order size. Check: the size traces to a committed plan, not a guess.
5. **Size Amazon FBA replenishment.** Size FBA replenishment shipments from a real cover target, treating a stockout as the failure mode to avoid. Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: FBA replenishment size. Check: the cover target is the brand's own, not a platform default.
6. **Watch IPI and storage limits.** Watch Amazon IPI score and storage limits live so a restock is never blocked. Skill: `prod/reorder-point-calc`. Output: IPI/storage flag. Check: the flag is raised before the limit blocks a restock, not after.
7. **Draft the PO.** Draft a PO for every SKU below its reorder point. Skill: `none`. Output: PO draft. Check: the draft covers every flagged SKU.
8. **Log the plan.** Log the reorder plan and any PO draft to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged reorder plan. Check: the plan is dated and links to the SKU record.

Outputs: reorder point and safety stock per SKU, PO draft where triggered, IPI/storage limit flag. QA: velocity and lead time are real numbers, not defaults; IPI limit checked before a restock is planned; arithmetic computed in code. Read point: weekly, against actual stockouts and IPI score. Duration: agent time under 1 hour, runs unattended weekly.

### PROD-W13 Flag concentration risk, refresh landed cost and flag dead stock

Trigger: monthly | weekly (dead-stock sweep). Human role: Sourcing/inventory manager watching structural risk and ageing stock together. Capabilities: PROD-C23, PROD-C27. Needs: supplier and country ledger, current FX, freight and duty rates, stock age and velocity by channel, long-term storage/dead-stock thresholds.

1. **Compute concentration shares.** Compute supplier and country concentration shares across the sourcing base. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: concentration share by supplier and country. Check: shares are computed in code from the ledger, not estimated.
2. **Confirm a mapped backup.** Confirm a mapped backup supplier exists in a second country for any concentrated share. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: backup-supplier confirmation. Check: the backup is a real, vetted supplier, not assumed.
3. **Recompute landed cost on any change.** Recompute landed cost the moment price, freight, FX or duty changes. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: refreshed landed cost. Check: the recompute is triggered by an actual rate change, not on a fixed calendar only.
4. **Watch inventory age against velocity.** Watch stock age against velocity across DTC and marketplace stock. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: age-vs-velocity table. Check: the table covers every channel the SKU sells on.
5. **Flag before it becomes dead weight.** Flag a SKU against the long-term storage or dead-stock threshold before it becomes dead weight. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: dead-stock alert. Check: the alert fires before the threshold is crossed, not after.
6. **Draft the clearance options.** Draft a markdown, bundle or discontinue plan with the cash impact of each option. Skill: `prod/inventory-risk-flag`. Output: clearance plan with cash impact per option. Check: every option's cash impact is computed, not estimated.
7. **Send to the founder gate.** Send the concentration-risk and dead-stock flags to the founder gate. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: the card names what it costs to act and what it costs to wait.
8. **Log the refresh.** Log the refreshed landed cost and clearance decision to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged risk/cost record. Check: the log links to the prior month's entry for trend.

Outputs: concentration risk flag, refreshed landed cost, dead-stock alert and clearance plan. QA: backup supplier is mapped, not assumed; landed cost recomputed on every relevant price move; clearance cash numbers computed, not estimated. Read point: founder decision on the clearance plan; next month's concentration recompute. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours monthly; dead-stock sweep runs unattended weekly.

### PROD-W14 Sync multi-channel inventory, route orders and plan peak-season capacity

Trigger: continuous; event: new channel added | annual, ahead of festive season. Human role: Inventory/ops planner keeping one stock pool safe across channels and ready for peak season. Capabilities: PROD-G05, PROD-C72. Needs: stock pool across site, marketplaces, quick commerce and stores, per-channel buffer policy, order-routing and split-shipment rules, festive calendar, warehouse staffing and WMS/OMS capacity.

1. **Keep one synced stock pool.** Keep one stock pool synced across every channel with per-channel buffers to stop overselling. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: synced stock pool. Check: no channel oversells against its buffer.
2. **Set routing and split-shipment rules.** Set order routing and split-shipment rules by channel and warehouse. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: routing ruleset. Check: every channel has a stated routing rule, none default by accident.
3. **Reconcile the pool daily.** Reconcile the stock pool daily and flag any channel drifting out of sync. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: reconciliation flag. Check: drift is flagged the day it is detected.
4. **Project peak-season safety stock.** Ahead of the festive season, project the safety stock needed across the season. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: peak safety-stock projection. Check: the projection uses at least one prior festive cycle's data where it exists.
5. **Check staffing and system load.** Check warehouse staffing and WMS/OMS load against the projected peak volume. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: capacity check. Check: the check states a percentage of capacity used, not a yes/no.
6. **Build the readiness plan.** Build the festive readiness plan rolling stock, staffing and system load into one plan. Skill: `prod/inventory-network-plan`. Output: peak-season readiness plan. Check: the plan covers stock, staffing and system load together, not just stock.
7. **Send to the founder gate.** Send the readiness plan to the founder gate ahead of the season. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: the card is sent with enough lead time to act on any gap.
8. **Log the rules and plan.** Log the sync rules and readiness plan to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged network plan. Check: the log is dated and versioned for the season.

Outputs: multi-channel sync and routing rules, peak-season readiness plan. QA: no channel oversells against the buffer policy; readiness plan covers stock, staffing and system load together. Read point: peak season's actual stockout and fulfilment-delay rate against the plan. Duration: agent time 2 to 3 hours annually for the readiness plan; sync runs continuously.

### PROD-W15 Select a fulfilment 3PL/WMS and build warehouse and returns workflows

Trigger: on demand (3PL/WMS selection) | weekly (warehouse ops) | ongoing, reviewed weekly (returns). Human role: Fulfilment manager standing up or resetting domestic fulfilment operations. Capabilities: PROD-C52, PROD-C53, PROD-C54. Needs: order volume and SKU count, SLA requirements, candidate 3PL list, warehouse layout if in-house, return/RTO volume and reasons.

1. **Run the RFP and SLA negotiation.** Run the RFP and SLA negotiation for a domestic fulfilment 3PL, separate from import-side freight. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: negotiated SLA. Check: the SLA states numeric targets, not general commitments.
2. **Run the integration test.** Run the integration test for the 3PL's WMS before go-live. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: integration test result. Check: go-live does not happen before the integration test passes.
3. **Set slotting rules.** Set warehouse slotting rules by SKU velocity. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: slotting rules. Check: fast-moving SKUs are slotted for the shortest pick path.
4. **Set pick-pack-QC and staffing.** Set the pick-pack-QC method and staffing level against real order volume. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: pick-pack-QC and staffing plan. Check: staffing is sized to real volume, not a guess.
5. **Design the returns grading rule.** Design the fixed rule for a returned unit: RTO or customer return, restock, refurbish or write off. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: grading ruleset. Check: the rule is fixed, decided in advance, not case-by-case.
6. **Apply the rule on schedule.** Apply the grading rule on a fixed schedule as returns arrive. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: graded-unit log. Check: every returned unit gets a graded outcome within the schedule.
7. **Review weekly.** Review staffing levels and grading outcomes weekly. Skill: `prod/fulfilment-ops-setup`. Output: weekly review note. Check: the review compares actual outcomes to the plan.
8. **Log the decisions.** Log the 3PL/WMS decision and the grading rule to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged fulfilment setup. Check: the log links the SLA, the integration test result and the grading rule.

Outputs: 3PL/WMS selection record, warehouse ops ruleset, returns grading ruleset. QA: 3PL chosen on SLA and service history, not lowest quote alone; grading rule is fixed, not case-by-case judgment; integration tested before go-live. Read point: weekly SLA and grading-outcome review. Duration: agent time 1 day for RFP/selection; ongoing weekly review under 1 hour.

### PROD-W16 Run daily courier rate-shop and NDR resolution

Trigger: per order, real time | daily, same-day on NDR. Human role: Shipping/ops executive running the daily courier decision and same-day NDR outreach. Capabilities: PROD-C55, PROD-C56. Needs: live courier rate feed, pincode serviceability data, SLA history per courier per zone, NDR queue, customer contact channel.

1. **Pick the courier in code.** Pick the courier per shipment in code from live rate, pincode serviceability and SLA. Skill: `prod/daily-shipping-ops`. Output: per-order courier choice. Check: the choice is computed from live data, not a fixed default carrier.
2. **Fail over on the spot.** Fail over to a backup courier the moment the primary fails serviceability or SLA. Skill: `prod/daily-shipping-ops`. Output: failover event log. Check: failover happens automatically, not after a manual check.
3. **Pull today's NDRs.** Pull every non-delivery report raised today. Skill: `prod/daily-shipping-ops`. Output: today's NDR list. Check: the list is pulled at least once before the courier's attempt window closes.
4. **Confirm or reschedule.** Confirm the address or reschedule with the customer before the courier's attempt window closes. Skill: `prod/daily-shipping-ops`. Output: outreach result. Check: outreach happens before the attempt window closes, not after.
5. **Log the outcome.** Log the outreach outcome and any RTO decision. Skill: `prod/daily-shipping-ops`. Output: NDR outcome log. Check: every NDR has a recorded outcome, none left open past the window.
6. **Flag rising zones.** Flag any zone with a rising NDR or RTO rate for the monthly scorecard. Skill: `none`. Output: zone risk flag. Check: the flag states the zone and the trend, not a single day's spike.
7. **Log the day.** Log the day's routing and NDR outcomes to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged daily shipping record. Check: the log is complete for every order shipped that day.

Outputs: per-order courier routing log, NDR resolution log. QA: routing decision computed from live data, not a fixed default carrier; every NDR worked the day it was raised. Read point: same day: NDR resolved before the attempt window closes; monthly: zone RTO trend. Duration: agent time runs continuously per order; NDR outreach adds same-day human contact time.

### PROD-W17 Score couriers monthly and draft a renegotiation

Trigger: monthly | event: shipment cost per order drifts, or a contract renewal nears. Human role: Ops manager reallocating volume and pushing back on rising shipping cost. Capabilities: PROD-C59, LOOP-C34. Needs: courier scorecard history, RTO, first-attempt delivery and TAT data per courier per zone, public rate benchmarks for comparable India couriers.

1. **Update the running scorecard.** Update the scorecard per courier per zone on RTO rate, first-attempt delivery rate and delivery TAT. Skill: `prod/courier-scorecard`. Output: updated scorecard. Check: the scorecard covers every courier and zone in use.
2. **Compare performance.** Compare each courier's zone performance against the others on a set cadence. Skill: `prod/courier-scorecard`. Output: comparison ranking. Check: the comparison uses the same window for every courier.
3. **Shift volume where the gap is real.** Shift volume to the better performer where the gap is real and sustained, not a single good week. Skill: `prod/courier-scorecard`. Output: volume reallocation. Check: the shift cites a sustained gap over more than one period.
4. **Watch cost against public benchmarks.** Watch the brand's own shipping cost per order against public rate benchmarks for comparable India couriers. Skill: `prod/courier-scorecard`. Output: cost-vs-benchmark read. Check: the benchmark is a real, dated public rate card.
5. **Draft the renegotiation ask.** When cost drifts above benchmark or a renewal nears, draft the renegotiation ask with the benchmark evidence attached. Skill: `prod/courier-scorecard`. Output: renegotiation draft. Check: the draft cites the specific benchmark it is asking to match.
6. **Send to the founder gate.** Send the volume-shift and renegotiation recommendations to the founder gate. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: the card states the expected saving and the risk of asking.
7. **Log the outcome.** Log the scorecard update and any renegotiation outcome to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged scorecard/renegotiation record. Check: the log links to the prior month's scorecard.

Outputs: courier scorecard, volume reallocation recommendation, renegotiation draft. QA: volume shift based on a real and sustained gap, not one bad week; renegotiation ask cites public benchmarks, not a guess. Read point: next month's scorecard, to see if the shift or renegotiation moved the numbers. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours monthly.

### PROD-W18 File courier and marketplace claims

Trigger: event: shipment marked lost or damaged, or RTO received damaged; weekly sweep | weekly. Human role: Ops/claims coordinator recovering money owed for lost, damaged or wrongly billed inventory. Capabilities: PROD-G01, PROD-G02. Needs: shipment/RTO records, proof of loss or damage (photos, weight, seal), marketplace seller console access, fee statements.

1. **Identify this week's incidents.** Identify every shipment marked lost, damaged or wrongly marked RTO this week. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: incident list. Check: the list covers the full week, not a sample.
2. **Assemble proof.** Assemble proof for each incident: photos, weight discrepancy, seal condition. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: proof packet per incident. Check: every claim has a proof packet before it is filed.
3. **File the courier claim.** File the claim with the courier or aggregator, with proof attached. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: filed courier claim. Check: the claim is filed within the courier's own filing window.
4. **Find marketplace reimbursement candidates.** Find lost or damaged marketplace inventory and wrong fees inside each seller console. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: reimbursement candidate list. Check: the list is pulled from the console, not from memory.
5. **File the marketplace claim.** File the marketplace reimbursement claim (FBA reimbursement, Flipkart SPF, Myntra claim). Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: filed marketplace claim. Check: the claim type matches the marketplace's own reimbursement program.
6. **Track payout.** Track payout against every open claim. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: payout tracker. Check: a claim is marked paid only when the payout is confirmed, not assumed.
7. **Escalate overdue claims.** Escalate any claim past its normal resolution window. Skill: `prod/claims-file`. Output: escalation log. Check: escalation happens the day the window is exceeded.
8. **Log and hand to Money.** Log every claim, its status and payout to the registry, handed to Money for reconciliation. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged claims record. Check: PROD-C35's FBA paperwork is referenced, not duplicated here.

Outputs: courier claim record with proof, marketplace reimbursement claim record, payout tracker. QA: every claim has proof attached; payout tracked against the claim, not assumed paid. Read point: when payout lands or the claim window closes unpaid. Duration: agent time 2 to 3 hours weekly; courier/marketplace resolution adds 1 to 4 weeks wait.

### PROD-W19 Run a non-standard operations request: export, offline retail, recall or made-to-order

Trigger: event: first international order or new export market | event: pop-up, exhibition or stockist deal approved | event: safety-related defect or regulator notice | event: personalised or made-to-order SKU sold. Human role: Ops manager handling an order or channel type outside the standard playbook. Capabilities: PROD-G03, PROD-G04, PROD-G06, PROD-G07. Needs: the triggering event's details, brand brain: policies and certifications, the matching sub-skill for the type.

1. **Classify the exception type.** Classify the request as export, offline retail, recall or made-to-order. Skill: `none`. Output: classified request. Check: exactly one type is chosen; a request matching two types is split.
2. **Run the export procedure if export.** Set up IEC and LUT, HS codes, commercial invoice and export paperwork; choose DDP vs DDU. Skill: `prod/non-standard-ops`. Output: export compliance packet. Check: the DDP/DDU choice is stated with the buyer's expectation it protects.
3. **Run the offline-retail procedure if offline.** Set up POS, stock transfer and return, consignment settlement, staff roster and daily reconciliation. Skill: `prod/non-standard-ops`. Output: offline retail ops record. Check: daily sales reconciliation happens every day of the event.
4. **Run the recall procedure if a safety issue.** Find affected batches, stop sale on every channel, contact buyers, arrange returns or replacements, log regulator notices. Skill: `prod/non-standard-ops`. Output: recall log. Check: sale is stopped on every channel before any other recall step proceeds. Ask the founder if: always: a recall never proceeds without the founder gate first.
5. **Run the made-to-order procedure if custom.** Capture the spec at checkout, get customer approval on a proof, track production, state non-returnable terms. Skill: `prod/non-standard-ops`. Output: made-to-order tracking record. Check: the non-returnable term is shown to the customer before payment, not after.
6. **Run the type-specific compliance check.** Check export duty rules, retail licensing, regulator requirements, or custom-order terms, matching the type. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: compliance check result. Check: the compliance check matches the classified type exactly.
7. **Document the handling.** Document the full handling and any customer communication. Skill: `none`. Output: documentation record. Check: the documentation is complete enough for an audit.
8. **Log the outcome.** Log the outcome to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged exception record. Check: the log states the type and the final status.

Outputs: export compliance packet, pop-up/stockist ops record, recall log, made-to-order tracking record. QA: recall always reaches the founder gate before any customer contact; export paperwork matches the chosen DDP/DDU term; made-to-order terms state non-returnable clearly. Read point: type-specific: export shipment cleared; pop-up settlement done; recall closed; made-to-order delivered. Duration: varies by type: export 1 to 2 days; recall is immediate and founder-gated; made-to-order tracks production lead time.

### PROD-W20 Screen Amazon candidates and mine competitor reviews and keywords

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Amazon category manager doing Amazon-specific candidate diligence. Capabilities: PROD-C30, PROD-C31. Needs: candidate from PROD-W01/PROD-W03, Amazon browse/search data (public), competing listing reviews, reverse-ASIN keyword tool access.

1. **Apply the elimination screens.** Apply price floor, page-1 price spread, keyword variability and brand-dominance screens. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: elimination screen result. Check: every screen states a reason, not just a pass/fail.
2. **Run the fad filter.** Check the candidate's search trend for a real fad pattern, not a single spike. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: fad-filter verdict. Check: the verdict uses more than one data point in time.
3. **Read low-star reviews for fixable complaints.** Read 1 to 3 star reviews on competing listings for complaints the candidate could fix. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: fixable-complaint list. Check: no review text is copied verbatim into the brief, only the gap it points to.
4. **Confirm the demand-and-gap signal.** Confirm mid-rated, high-volume sellers exist as a demand-and-gap signal. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: demand-gap confirmation. Check: the confirmation cites review count and rating together.
5. **Mine keywords.** Mine keywords from reverse-ASIN tools for the target category. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: keyword list. Check: the keyword list is ranked by search volume where available.
6. **Score the candidate.** Score the candidate against the elimination screens with the reasons shown. Skill: `prod/amazon-opportunity-screen`. Output: Amazon screen result. Check: every elimination reason is stated.
7. **Log and hand off.** Log the screen result, review gaps and keyword list to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged Amazon screen. Check: the log hands off to PROD-W21 for the economics model.

Outputs: Amazon screen result, product-gap brief from reviews, keyword list. QA: every elimination reason stated; fad filter uses real trend data, not a single spike; no competitor review text copied verbatim. Read point: when the candidate reaches the Amazon economics model (PROD-W21). Duration: agent time 2 to 3 hours.

### PROD-W21 Model Amazon channel economics

Trigger: monthly. Human role: Amazon manager gating a SKU's real net margin on the channel. Capabilities: PROD-C32. Needs: FBA/FBM fee stack, storage cost schedule, PPC budget assumption, DTC price for parity check, landed cost from PROD-W07.

1. **Pull the current fee stack.** Pull the current Amazon referral, FBA and storage fee stack for the category. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: current fee stack. Check: the fee stack is dated to the current Amazon publication.
2. **Model fees against landed cost.** Model the full fee stack against the landed cost, in code. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: fee-stack model. Check: the model is computed in code, not estimated.
3. **Add PPC and storage cost.** Add a PPC budget assumption and storage cost to the model. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: extended fee-stack model. Check: the PPC assumption is stated as an assumption, not a fact.
4. **Check price parity.** Check the Amazon price against the DTC price-parity rule. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: parity check result. Check: the check compares against the live DTC price, not a stale one.
5. **Compute net margin.** Compute net margin after every fee, in code. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: net margin per SKU. Check: the margin is computed in code, never estimated in text.
6. **Flag margin failures.** Flag any SKU whose net margin fails the brand's floor. Skill: `prod/amazon-fee-model`. Output: margin-floor flag. Check: the floor used is the brand's own, cited to where it was set.
7. **Log the model.** Log the fee-stack model and margin result to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged Amazon economics. Check: the log is dated and versioned to the current fee schedule.

Outputs: Amazon fee-stack model, net margin result per SKU. QA: every fee line sourced from Amazon's current published rate; margin computed in code; price-parity checked against live DTC price. Read point: monthly, or before any Amazon launch decision. Duration: agent time 1 hour monthly.

### PROD-W22 Build and launch a marketplace listing

Trigger: event: listing build or refresh | event: new listing or certification renewal. Human role: Marketplace/catalogue manager building a compliant, optimised listing. Capabilities: PROD-C34, PROD-C44, PROD-C45. Needs: product copy, approved claims, keyword list, category node, image slots and specs per platform, BIS/hallmarking/labelling status if regulated category, target marketplace.

1. **Confirm the target marketplace.** Confirm which marketplace this listing is for and select the matching listing procedure. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: selected marketplace procedure. Check: the marketplace named matches one of Amazon, Flipkart, Myntra, Nykaa, Blinkit or Zepto.
2. **Run the compliance check first.** Run the BIS, hallmarking and labelling compliance check for the category before building anything. Skill: `prod/marketplace-compliance-check`. Output: compliance result. Check: no listing is built for a regulated category before this check clears.
3. **Place the top keyword.** Place the target keyword in the title and the first bullet or the platform's equivalent field. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: keyword-placed title/bullets. Check: the top keyword appears in the title and the first content field.
4. **Write benefit-led, claim-checked copy.** Write benefit-led bullets or description copy with one true, tiered claim each. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: claim-checked copy. Check: every claim is in the approved or site-backed tier.
5. **Brief every image slot.** Brief every image slot to the platform's own size, crop and content rule. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: image slot briefs. Check: every image slot has a brief, none left generic.
6. **Fill every attribute field.** Fill every attribute field and set backend keywords where the platform supports them. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: filled attribute set. Check: no required field is left blank.
7. **Check the category node.** Confirm the category node or listing category is correct for the platform. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: confirmed category node. Check: the node matches the platform's current taxonomy, not a guessed one.
8. **Run the full-draft claim check.** Run the claim check on the complete draft before handoff. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: final claim-check result. Check: the check covers the whole draft, not just the bullets.
9. **Package the draft.** Package the listing draft ready to paste into the seller console; never post it directly. Skill: `none`. Output: packaged listing draft. Check: nothing is posted directly to a live listing.
10. **Hand off with compliance attached.** Hand the draft to the named human who applies it, with the compliance result attached. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: handoff record. Check: the compliance result travels with the draft, not separately.

Outputs: marketplace listing draft, compliance checklist result. QA: every claim is in the approved or site-backed tier; no field left blank; compliance checked before the listing is drafted, not after; nothing posted directly to a live listing. Read point: after the listing goes live: search rank on the target keyword, and review rate over the following weeks. Duration: agent time 2 to 4 hours per listing; human posting adds under 1 day.

### PROD-W23 Prepare and ship an FBA inbound shipment

Trigger: event: FBA shipment. Human role: Amazon operations executive preparing a compliant FBA shipment. Capabilities: PROD-C35. Needs: measured case dimensions, FBA's current hard limits, saved shipment template if one exists, carton labels.

1. **Measure against FBA's limits.** Measure case dimensions against FBA's current hard limits. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: dimension check. Check: the limits used are Amazon's current published limits, not a cached figure.
2. **Reuse a saved template.** Reuse a saved shipment template where the SKU matches one already shipped. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: reused or new template. Check: a template is reused whenever the SKU and case size match a prior shipment.
3. **Build the shipment plan.** Build the shipment plan: box count, weight per box, carrier. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: shipment plan. Check: box weight stays under the carrier's per-box limit.
4. **Check carton labels.** Generate and check carton labels against FBA's label spec. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: checked carton labels. Check: every carton has a label matching FBA's current spec.
5. **Keep the paper trail.** Keep the BOL, carrier receipt and carton count so a lost-inventory claim can be filed later. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: paper trail. Check: the paper trail is complete before the shipment leaves.
6. **Confirm receipt.** Confirm the shipment against Amazon's inbound dashboard once received. Skill: `prod/fba-shipment-prep`. Output: receipt confirmation. Check: any discrepancy between sent and received is flagged.
7. **Log the shipment.** Log the shipment plan and paper trail to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged FBA shipment. Check: a receipt discrepancy is linked to a claim in PROD-W18.

Outputs: FBA shipment plan, paper trail record. QA: dimensions checked against FBA's current limits, not an old cached limit; paper trail complete before the shipment leaves. Read point: on Amazon's confirmed receipt; any discrepancy feeds a claim (PROD-W18). Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours; inbound transit 3 to 10 days.

### PROD-W24 Plan and protect an Amazon product launch, account setup and Brand Registry

Trigger: event: considering Amazon launch | event: listing live | event: pre-launch. Human role: Amazon manager taking a brand from no seller account to a protected, launched product. Capabilities: PROD-C28, PROD-C29, PROD-C36. Needs: business entity and GST details, bank/payout details, identity verification documents, trademark status, stock and PPC plan for launch.

1. **Get the account right the first time.** Get the Amazon account, entity, payout and identity verification right the first time. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: verified account. Check: no field is submitted before its supporting document is confirmed correct. Ask the founder if: before any account or spend commitment.
2. **Sequence the trademark filing.** File the trademark before listing creation, in the correct sequence. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: trademark filing status. Check: the filing happens before, not after, the listing goes live.
3. **Create the listing.** Create the listing only after the sequencing checklist clears, using PROD-W22 for the listing build. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: listing created. Check: the listing build itself follows PROD-W22, not a shortcut here.
4. **Enrol Brand Registry.** Enrol Brand Registry once the trademark and listing are both live. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: Brand Registry enrolment. Check: enrolment is not attempted before both prerequisites are live.
5. **Turn on Storefront and Subscribe & Save.** Turn on Storefront and Subscribe & Save benefits Brand Registry unlocks. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: enabled benefits. Check: both benefits are checked live, not assumed enabled.
6. **Align stock and PPC for launch week.** Concentrate real demand into the first weeks by aligning stock and PPC to go live together. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: aligned launch plan. Check: stock and PPC go live on the same date, not staggered.
7. **Build a pre-launch audience.** Build an audience before launch day where the platform allows it. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: pre-launch audience plan. Check: the audience-building method is one the platform's terms allow.
8. **Protect against copycats.** Protect a novel product against copycats from day one: watermark images, file for design protection where relevant. Skill: `prod/amazon-launch-sequence`. Output: protection checklist. Check: protection steps are taken before launch day, not after a copy appears.
9. **Send to the founder gate.** Send the account-opening and launch plan to the founder gate before any spend commitment. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: no spend commitment happens before the founder approves.
10. **Log the sequence.** Log every sequencing step and its completion date to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged launch sequence. Check: every step has a completion date, none left open-ended.

Outputs: verified Amazon account, sequenced Brand Registry checklist, launch plan. QA: account verification done right the first time; nothing filed before its prerequisite; stock and PPC confirmed live together before launch day; founder approved before any account or spend commitment. Read point: launch week's sales and PPC read; Brand Registry enrolment confirmation. Duration: agent time spread over the sequence; Amazon's own verification adds 1 to 3 weeks.

### PROD-W25 Monitor marketplace health: reviews, account health and Buy Box

Trigger: weekly. Human role: Marketplace operations manager watching reputation and account risk weekly. Capabilities: PROD-C38, PROD-C46. Needs: Account Health dashboard data, review history, Buy Box status per listing, reseller pricing across marketplaces.

1. **Request reviews through permitted paths.** Request reviews only through the platform's own permitted request paths. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: review requests sent. Check: no review request uses an unpermitted method (no incentivised or off-platform ask).
2. **Contest misplaced feedback.** Contest any misplaced or policy-violating feedback through the platform's own process. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: contested feedback log. Check: every contest cites the specific policy it violates.
3. **Watch Account Health weekly.** Watch Account Health weekly so a policy notice never becomes a surprise suspension. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: account health status. Check: the dashboard is checked every week without exception.
4. **Watch Buy Box ownership.** Watch who holds the Buy Box across marketplaces for the brand's listings. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: Buy Box ownership report. Check: the report covers every marketplace the brand sells on.
5. **Watch reseller pricing.** Watch unauthorised reseller pricing against MAP across marketplaces. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: reseller pricing report. Check: MAP is the brand's own stated policy, not assumed.
6. **Flag problems for the founder.** Flag a MAP or price-integrity problem for the founder rather than changing price unilaterally. Skill: `prod/marketplace-health-monitor`. Output: MAP violation flag. Check: no price is changed by this agent to respond to a MAP issue.
7. **Log the week.** Log the week's account-health, review and Buy Box status to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged marketplace health record. Check: the log is dated and comparable week over week.

Outputs: account health report, review/feedback log, Buy Box/reseller report. QA: reviews requested only through permitted paths; price never changed without the founder gate; policy notice acted on the week it appears. Read point: weekly, and immediately on any account-health notice. Duration: agent time under 1 hour weekly.

### PROD-W26 Diagnose Amazon performance and plan channel growth

Trigger: on demand. Human role: Amazon manager explaining a sales change and planning the channel's next move. Capabilities: PROD-C40, PROD-C41, PROD-C43. Needs: Business Reports, attribution data, seasonality baseline, current SKU portfolio, candidate growth levers.

1. **Split traffic from conversion.** Split a sales change into traffic versus conversion before acting on it. Skill: `prod/amazon-performance-review`. Output: traffic/conversion split. Check: the split is computed from the reports, not inferred from a feeling.
2. **Check attribution and seasonality.** Check attribution and seasonality before calling a product a failure. Skill: `prod/amazon-performance-review`. Output: attribution/seasonality check. Check: the check uses the same window as the sales change being explained.
3. **Use only compliant promotion tools.** Respond using only Amazon's own compliant promotion tools, never an outside workaround. Skill: `prod/amazon-performance-review`. Output: promotion response plan. Check: every tool used is one Amazon's own terms permit.
4. **Decide on a second SKU.** Decide when to add a second SKU, based on the diagnosis and portfolio fit. Skill: `prod/amazon-performance-review`. Output: second-SKU recommendation. Check: the recommendation traces to the diagnosis, not a hunch.
5. **Test growth levers with a defined read.** Test smaller, situational growth levers with a defined read: FBM to FBA, Small and Light, a design-vendor bake-off, Amazon Live. Skill: `prod/amazon-performance-review`. Output: growth-lever test plan. Check: every test has a defined read before it starts.
6. **Delegate non-revenue admin.** Delegate non-revenue admin tasks so growth decisions get the attention. Skill: `none`. Output: delegated task list. Check: delegation does not include a founder_gate decision.
7. **Write the channel memo.** Write the one-page memo behind any decision to add or expand the Amazon channel. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: channel expansion memo. Check: the memo states the evidence, the cost and the read date.
8. **Log everything.** Log the diagnosis, experiment reads and channel decision to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged performance review. Check: every experiment's read date is logged, not left informal.

Outputs: sales diagnosis note, growth experiment read, channel expansion memo. QA: traffic vs conversion split before any conclusion; only Amazon-compliant promotion tools used; every experiment has a defined read before it starts. Read point: the experiment's defined read date; the memo's review date if approved. Duration: agent time 1 to 3 hours per diagnosis; experiment reads take their own defined window.

### PROD-W27 Build, hand off and audit the marketplace catalogue feed
