Trigger: daily | weekly (quality audit) | on new asset or new channel (format check). Human role: Catalogue/PIM manager keeping one clean, compliant feed across channels. Capabilities: PROD-C47, PROD-C63, PROD-C64. Needs: live price and stock records, product records (titles, images, GTIN), each channel's image/video spec, cross-channel price policy.

1. **Keep one clean feed.** Keep one clean product feed: titles, images, prices, availability, GTIN. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: master product feed. Check: the feed has no duplicate SKU and no missing GTIN.
2. **Validate against each channel's schema.** Validate the feed against each channel's own schema before handoff. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: validation report. Check: validation runs against the channel's current schema, not a cached one.
3. **Hand off to Media buyer and Organic.** Hand the validated feed to Media buyer and Organic to plug into shopping ads and marketplace channels. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: handoff record. Check: the receiving agent confirms the feed loaded without error.
4. **Run the standing quality audit.** Run a standing audit across every channel's live listings for broken images, missing attributes, duplicate SKUs. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: quality audit report. Check: the audit covers every live channel, none skipped.
5. **Check cross-channel price.** Check the brand's own price mismatch between its site and each marketplace. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: price mismatch report. Check: a mismatch is flagged, never silently corrected.
6. **Check image/video specs.** Check every new product image and video against each channel's own size, crop and content rule before it is pushed. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: asset spec check. Check: the check happens before push, not after a channel rejection.
7. **Flag failures precisely.** Flag every failure with the exact channel and field it occurred on. Skill: `prod/catalogue-feed-audit`. Output: failure flag list. Check: every flag names a channel and a specific field.
8. **Log the results.** Log the feed validation and audit results to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged feed/audit record. Check: the log is comparable day over day and week over week.

Outputs: master product feed, feed validation report, catalogue quality audit report. QA: feed validated against each channel's own schema, not a generic one; price mismatch flagged, never silently corrected; image/video checked before push, not after rejection. Read point: weekly audit result; each channel's feed-acceptance rate. Duration: agent time under 1 hour daily; audit under 2 hours weekly.

### PROD-W28 Onboard the brand on quick commerce

Trigger: on demand (new quick commerce launch). Human role: Quick commerce manager taking a brand from documents to live on Blinkit, Zepto or Instamart. Capabilities: PROD-C48. Needs: GST, FSSAI, barcode and packaging documents, product catalogue for the platform, target platform(s).

1. **Assemble the documents.** Assemble the GST, FSSAI, barcode and packaging documents the platform requires. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-onboard`. Output: document packet. Check: every document the platform lists is present before submission.
2. **Submit through the platform's channel.** Submit the onboarding application through the platform's own channel. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-onboard`. Output: submitted application. Check: the application is submitted through the platform's own onboarding process, not a workaround.
3. **Track review and respond.** Track document review status and respond to any query the platform raises. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-onboard`. Output: review status log. Check: every platform query gets a response within its stated window.
4. **Prepare the initial listing.** Prepare the initial catalogue listing per the platform's format, using the marketplace listing skill for that platform. Skill: `prod/list-on-marketplace`. Output: initial listing draft. Check: the listing uses the matching Blinkit or Zepto sub-skill, not a generic template.
5. **Confirm the validation window.** Confirm the platform's unit-economics validation window (commonly 90 days) and what it requires. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-onboard`. Output: validation window note. Check: the window and its requirement are both stated, not assumed.
6. **Go live and confirm the first order.** Get the brand live and confirm the first order flows correctly end to end. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-onboard`. Output: go-live confirmation. Check: the first order is confirmed to flow without error before the launch is called done.
7. **Log the milestones.** Log the onboarding milestones and go-live date to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged onboarding record. Check: every milestone has a date.

Outputs: onboarding document packet, go-live confirmation. QA: every required document assembled before submission; unit-economics validation window logged with its requirement. Read point: go-live date; the 90-day unit-economics validation window's outcome. Duration: agent time 3 to 5 hours across the process; platform review adds 1 to 4 weeks.

### PROD-W29 Manage quick commerce ad credits, promo calendar and dark-store fill rate

Trigger: weekly, and ahead of each festive window | daily (fill rate). Human role: Quick commerce manager running the ongoing account. Capabilities: PROD-C50, PROD-C51. Needs: ad credit balance per platform, organic search performance, festive promotional calendar, dark-store stock levels.

1. **Spend ad credits deliberately.** Spend each platform's ad tools deliberately instead of leaving credits unused. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: ad spend plan. Check: no credit balance sits unused past the platform's expiry.
2. **Pull back once organic picks up.** Pull back ad spend for a SKU once its organic search ranking improves. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: adjusted ad spend. Check: the pullback is triggered by a real organic-rank change, not a schedule.
3. **Recompute ROAS from transaction value.** Recompute ROAS from transaction value, not the platform's MRP-based default, before judging spend. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: recomputed ROAS. Check: the recompute uses transaction value, never the platform's MRP default.
4. **Negotiate festive windows.** Negotiate festive promotional windows ahead of the calendar date. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: negotiated promo terms. Check: negotiation happens ahead of the date, not during the window.
5. **Watch dark-store stock daily.** Watch stock levels across the dark-store network daily, the way a merchandiser watches shelf stock. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: dark-store stock report. Check: the report covers every dark store the brand is live in.
6. **Flag stockouts before the penalty.** Flag a dark-store stockout before it triggers an algorithmic ranking penalty. Skill: `prod/quick-commerce-ops`. Output: stockout flag. Check: the flag fires before the platform's own ranking-penalty window closes.
7. **Log the week.** Log ad spend, promo commitments and fill-rate flags to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged quick commerce ops record. Check: the log is comparable week over week.

Outputs: ad credit spend log, promo calendar commitments, dark-store fill-rate flag. QA: ROAS recomputed from transaction value, not MRP; fill-rate flag raised before the ranking penalty window closes. Read point: weekly ad spend efficiency; daily fill-rate status. Duration: agent time under 1 hour daily for fill rate; 1 to 2 hours weekly for ad/promo.

### PROD-W30 Track regulatory registers: Legal Metrology, FSSAI and EPR

Trigger: monthly audit, and on any MRP/GST change | quarterly, annual filing deadline. Human role: Compliance coordinator keeping every registration and filing current. Capabilities: PROD-C61, PROD-C62. Needs: SKU list with category, current Legal Metrology and FSSAI licence status, EPR plastic-packaging registration status, relabelling requirements.

1. **Keep the licence register.** Keep one register of which SKUs need Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) registration or an FSSAI licence. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: licence register. Check: every SKU in a regulated category appears in the register.
2. **Track validity windows.** Track each licence's current validity window. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: validity window tracker. Check: every licence's expiry date is current, not assumed.
3. **Flag relabelling on MRP/GST change.** Flag the relabelling needed on any MRP or GST change. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: relabelling flag. Check: a flag is raised on every MRP or GST change, not only at renewal.
4. **Register EPR packaging.** Register the brand's plastic packaging under India's EPR rules. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: EPR registration status. Check: the registration covers every plastic packaging type the brand uses.
5. **Track the annual EPR filing.** Track the annual EPR filing and recycling-target obligation. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: EPR filing tracker. Check: the filing deadline is tracked with enough lead time to act.
6. **Flag a shortfall.** Flag when a shortfall means buying EPR credits. Skill: `prod/regulatory-register-track`. Output: EPR shortfall flag. Check: the flag states the shortfall amount, not just that one exists.
7. **Send expiring licences to the founder.** Send any licence expiring within 60 days to the founder gate. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: founder card. Check: the card is sent with at least 60 days of lead time.
8. **Log every filing.** Log every registration, filing and renewal to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: logged compliance record. Check: the log is the single source of truth for every SKU's compliance status.

Outputs: compliance register, relabelling flag, EPR filing status. QA: every licence's validity window tracked, not assumed current; relabelling flagged on every MRP/GST change, not just renewal. Read point: each licence's renewal date; the annual EPR filing deadline. Duration: agent time 1 to 2 hours monthly; quarterly EPR filing adds a few hours.

## Money and ops (MONEY), 24 workflows

### MONEY-W01 Month-end close: settlement reconciliation and management accounts

Trigger: monthly, on the close calendar date | event: a settlement cycle ends. Human role: the bookkeeper closing the month. Capabilities: MONEY-C01, MONEY-C15, MONEY-C51, MONEY-C52, MONEY-C56, MONEY-C60, MONEY-G01, MONEY-G05. Needs: brand brain: chart of accounts and cost classification tree, accounting export for the month, gateway settlement files, marketplace settlement files, COD courier remittance files, open dispute list, fixed asset register, last month's P&L ladder.

1. **Pull the month's raw exports.** Pull Shopify and marketplace orders, gateway settlement files, courier COD remittance files and the accounting export for the close month. Skill: `none`. Output: raw export set with row counts per source. Check: every source file is present and dated inside the close month. Ask the founder if: a source connector has no read access yet.
2. **Reconcile the gateway settlement.** Unbundle each gateway payout into gross sale, fee, refund, GST on the fee and net, then match it to the bank credit. Skill: `money/reconcile/gateway`. Output: gateway reconciliation table with a named cause per gap. Check: every payout ties to a bank credit within 2 days or is flagged aged.
3. **Reconcile marketplace settlements.** Unbundle commission, fixed fee, shipping fee, TCS under Section 52, GST on fees and refunds for each marketplace payout. Skill: `money/reconcile/marketplace`. Output: marketplace reconciliation table, one row per payout. Check: TCS and GST on fees are broken out on their own lines, never netted into one number.
4. **Reconcile COD cash and courier remittance.** Age cash on delivery collected by each courier against what has actually reached the bank, by courier. Skill: `money/reconcile/cod-courier`. Output: COD aging table by courier. Check: every remittance stuck past the courier's own SLA cycle is flagged with an escalation note and a named contact.
5. **Reconcile ad platform invoices to logged spend.** Match the Meta and Google invoice amounts for the month to the spend the dashboards report. Skill: `money/reconcile/ad-platform-invoices`. Output: ad spend reconciliation line with variance. Check: any gap over 1 percent has a named cause, never left as rounding.
6. **Build the evidence pack for each open dispute.** Assemble the order record, delivery proof, OTP log and support chat for each open gateway chargeback or UPI dispute, and check its filing deadline. Skill: `none`. Output: one evidence pack per open dispute, with a filing deadline. Check: no dispute is missing its filing deadline. Ask the founder if: a dispute needs a person to submit it through the gateway's own portal.
7. **Update the fixed asset register.** Log any new capital purchase this month (equipment, fixtures, website build cost, packaging dies) and run the depreciation schedule. Skill: `money/fixed-asset-register`. Output: updated register with this month's depreciation line. Check: every asset carries purchase date, useful life and a running balance.
8. **Run the cost classification and P&L ladder.** Apply the fixed decision tree for COGS, cost of delivery, marketing cost and OPEX, and build the ladder from gross revenue to net profit. Skill: `money/pnl-close-checklist`. Output: P&L ladder for the month. Check: every line traces to a source; no line is double-counted between COGS and OPEX.
9. **Draft the settlement journal entries.** Draft one journal entry per settlement from the unbundled lines, instead of one lump bank deposit. Skill: `money/reconcile/gateway`. Output: draft journal entries for the accountant to post. Check: each entry's lines sum exactly to its bank credit.
10. **Run the close checklist.** Run bank reconciliations, accrual review, inventory valuation and deferred revenue checks. Skill: `money/pnl-close-checklist`. Output: close checklist, pass or fail per item. Check: no open item is left without an owner and a target date.
11. **Diff against last month.** Compare the ladder and every reconciliation line to the prior month and name the single biggest change. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: one paragraph naming the biggest change and its cause. Check: the paragraph names a cause, not only a number.
12. **Publish the management accounts.** Package the ladder, the reconciliation bridge and dispute status into the monthly management accounts report. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: management accounts report. Check: every number carries a source and a freshness stamp.
13. **Route unresolved items.** Send anything needing a signature, a gateway portal action or a judgment call outside the cost tree to the founder or bookkeeper. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: routed action list with owners. Check: every unresolved item has a named owner and a date. Ask the founder if: an accrual judgment call is not covered by the current cost classification tree.

Outputs: P&L ladder, reconciliation bridge (gateway, marketplace, COD, ad invoices), dispute evidence packs, fixed asset register update, management accounts report, close checklist. QA: every number sourced and freshness stamped; no missing value shown as zero; no ad account write attempted; GST and TCS never netted into one line. Read point: on publish, against last month's ladder and the annual budget. Duration: agent: 2 to 3 hours across the steps; waiting: up to 2 days for settlement files and accountant sign-off.

### MONEY-W02 Weekly contribution margin, SKU and customer profitability

Trigger: weekly (margin, SKU) | quarterly (customer profitability, or before a loyalty tier redesign). Human role: the finance analyst running the margin report. Capabilities: MONEY-C02, MONEY-C17, LIFE-C62. Needs: brand brain: SKU cost lines, product/SKU list, order export with channel and basket detail, support ticket cost per contact, return and refund log, discount ledger, prior period's margin table.

1. **Pull orders, costs and support cost.** Pull the week's orders with SKU, basket and channel detail, landed cost per SKU, support ticket cost, return cost and discount cost. Skill: `none`. Output: joined order-cost dataset. Check: every order has a channel tag and every SKU has a landed cost.
2. **Compute CM1, CM2 and CM3.** Compute CM1 (after COGS), CM2 (after delivery cost) and CM3 (after marketing cost), each labeled by scope. Skill: `money/margin`. Output: CM1/CM2/CM3 by basket, SKU and channel. Check: no margin number is shown without its scope label.
3. **Classify each SKU.** Classify every SKU as champion, hidden gem, growth driver or underperformer on contribution and cash tied up. Skill: `money/margin`. Output: ranked SKU contribution table with a classification tag. Check: every SKU has exactly one classification.
4. **Flag rationalization candidates.** Flag SKUs that are both cash-heavy and low-contribution for rationalization review. Skill: `money/margin`. Output: rationalization candidate list. Check: each candidate lists its contribution, cash tied up and last-90-day sell-through.
5. **Add support, return and discount cost per customer.** Add support-ticket cost, return and refund cost, shipping cost and discount cost against each customer's revenue, in code. Skill: `money/margin/customer-profitability`. Output: true customer profitability table. Check: no cost bucket is estimated; a missing bucket shows as unavailable.
6. **Feed loyalty tiering.** Rank customers by true profitability, not revenue, and flag mismatches against the current loyalty tier. Skill: `money/margin/customer-profitability`. Output: tier-mismatch list. Check: every flagged customer shows the gap between tier and true profitability. Ask the founder if: a tier redesign would move a named VIP customer down.
7. **Diff against the prior period.** Compare this period's CM, SKU classifications and customer profitability to the last run. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: change list since last run. Check: every named change states the driver, not just the delta.
8. **Publish the standing report.** Publish the CM, SKU and customer profitability report so nobody guesses what a margin number includes. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: contribution margin and profitability report. Check: every figure states CM1, CM2 or CM3 explicitly.

Outputs: CM1/CM2/CM3 report, SKU contribution and classification table, customer profitability table, rationalization candidate list. QA: margin scope always labeled; no revenue shown where profit was asked for; missing cost bucket shown as unavailable, not zero. Read point: weekly for CM and SKU; quarterly for customer profitability, against the prior run. Duration: agent: 45 to 90 minutes; no material waiting time.

### MONEY-W03 Price and promo economics gate

Trigger: event: new SKU or cost change | event: a promo is proposed. Human role: the founder's pricing and promotions check. Capabilities: MONEY-C04, MONEY-C05. Needs: brand brain: net-profit target, landed cost, proposed price or promo terms, 90-day sales history for the SKU, metric contract: current CM by SKU.

1. **Take the net-profit target.** Read the brand's net-profit target for the SKU or line, never a keystone rule of thumb. Skill: `none`. Output: target net-profit percent. Check: the target came from the brand brain, not an assumed default.
2. **Solve the price waterfall.** Solve bottom-up from landed cost through fees, shipping, returns and marketing cost to the shelf price that hits the target. Skill: `money/pricing-waterfall`. Output: proposed shelf price with full waterfall. Check: the waterfall shows every line between cost and price, none skipped.
3. **Or model the promo economics.** For a proposed promo (percent-off, BOGO, gift-with-purchase, flash sale), model its real cost shape through the same waterfall. Skill: `money/pricing-waterfall`. Output: promo cost model. Check: the model uses the promo's real mechanic, not a flat percent-off assumption.
4. **Run the break-even check.** Run a break-even go/no-go check on the promo, or a margin stress-test on the new price. Skill: `money/pricing-waterfall`. Output: go/no-go recommendation with the break-even volume. Check: the recommendation states the exact volume or margin needed to break even.
5. **Stress-test 60 to 90 days out.** For a new price, stress-test it against 60 to 90 days of actual sell-through once live. Skill: `money/pricing-waterfall`. Output: stress-test note. Check: the stress-test uses real sell-through, not the launch-day forecast.
6. **Write the founder brief.** Write the recommendation, the numbers behind it and the risk, with an explicit approve, reject or park line. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: founder brief with approve/reject/park line. Check: the brief states the number the founder is approving, not just a description.
7. **Stop for approval.** Stop before the price or promo goes public. Do not draft the site or ad copy change until approved. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: approval record. Check: no downstream artefact is drafted before this step returns approved. Ask the founder if: always: this capability is founder_gate.

Outputs: price waterfall, promo break-even model, founder brief with approve/reject/park line. QA: profit target never replaced by keystone; TCS, GST and fee lines all present in the waterfall; no live change without recorded approval. Read point: 60 to 90 days after a price goes live, or the day after a promo ends. Duration: agent: 30 to 60 minutes; waiting: founder decision turnaround.

### MONEY-W04 Cash forecast, inventory planning and financing evaluation

Trigger: weekly (forecast, inventory) | on demand (financing evaluation). Human role: the person planning cash for the next quarter. Capabilities: MONEY-C06, MONEY-C07, MONEY-C08, PROD-C42. Needs: brand brain: bank balance, payroll calendar, supplier lead times, ad spend plan, inventory on order, current financing terms if any.

1. **Pull cash inputs.** Pull the bank balance, payroll calendar, supplier lead times, inventory on order and the ad spend plan. Skill: `none`. Output: cash input set. Check: all five inputs present or named as unavailable.
2. **Build the 13-week forecast.** Build a rolling 13-week cash forecast from the inputs. Skill: `money/cash-forecast-13wk`. Output: 13-week cash table. Check: the forecast never lets inventory cash go negative.
3. **Reconcile profit to free cash flow.** Reconcile this month's profit to free cash flow and name the gap. Skill: `money/cash-forecast-13wk`. Output: profit-to-cash bridge. Check: the bridge names each of the 4 hidden cash traps checked.
4. **Flag the lowest cash point.** Flag the lowest projected cash week before it happens, with a sensitivity range. Skill: `money/cash-forecast-13wk`. Output: lowest-cash-week flag with sensitivity note. Check: the flag fires at least 3 weeks before the low point.
5. **Roll inventory cash forward.** Roll inventory forward with lead time to plan cash, targeting days-of-inventory to supplier lead time. Skill: `money/cash-forecast-13wk`. Output: inventory cash plan. Check: days-of-inventory is compared to lead time explicitly, not left implicit.
6. **Manage the cash conversion cycle.** Track days inventory, days payable and days receivable together as the cash conversion cycle. Skill: `money/cash-forecast-13wk`. Output: cash conversion cycle number with trend. Check: the cycle is shown as one number with its 3 parts, not only the parts.
7. **Compare financing options.** When cash runs low or a hard-to-source launch needs funding, compare real borrowing options (line of credit, inventory financing, revenue-based) and, for a hard-to-fund launch, crowdfunding, private capital or manufacturer referrals, against reference cost-of-capital rates. Skill: `money/financing-comparison`. Output: financing option comparison. Check: every option shows its real cost of capital, not the advertised rate.
8. **Stress-test repayments.** Stress-test any financing option's repayment schedule against a slower-sales scenario. Skill: `money/financing-comparison`. Output: repayment stress-test. Check: the stress case uses the brand's own slow-month history, not a generic haircut.
9. **Write the founder brief and stop.** Write the recommendation and stop for approval before any financing commitment or launch spend is signed. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: founder brief with approve/reject/park line. Check: no financing or sourcing commitment is treated as decided by this agent. Ask the founder if: always: financing evaluation and sourcing decisions are human_assist.

Outputs: 13-week cash forecast, inventory cash plan, financing option comparison, founder brief. QA: inventory never modeled negative; profit and cash reconciled monthly; financing cost shown at its real rate. Read point: weekly against the rolling forecast; on demand when financing is evaluated. Duration: agent: 1 to 2 hours; waiting: lender or manufacturer response time for financing evaluation.

### MONEY-W05 Capital raising, investor and lender reporting, and insurance

Trigger: on demand (raise or loan process) | monthly (reporting) | quarterly (board deck) | annually (insurance renewal) | event: insured loss. Human role: the person assembling the data room and the cover. Capabilities: MONEY-C58, MONEY-C59, MONEY-G02. Needs: brand brain: cap table, prior raises, insurance policies, P&L ladder, cash forecast, CAC/LTV report, loss or claim event details.

1. **Assemble the data room.** Assemble the recurring documents a lender or investor asks for: financials, cohort economics, cap table, contracts. Skill: `money/capital-and-investor-reporting`. Output: current data room. Check: every document in the room has a version date.
2. **Produce the recurring reporting pack.** Produce the monthly update, covenant compliance check and quarterly board deck financials from existing outputs. Skill: `money/capital-and-investor-reporting`. Output: reporting pack. Check: covenant compliance is stated as met or breached, never implied.
3. **Identify required insurance coverage.** Identify the coverage an e-commerce brand needs: product liability, cyber, business owner's policy, and D&O once there are outside investors. Skill: `money/insurance-cover-and-claims`. Output: coverage checklist by type. Check: each coverage type states why it applies to this brand.
4. **Compare quotes.** Compare insurance quotes against the coverage checklist and the brand's own risk profile. Skill: `money/insurance-cover-and-claims`. Output: quote comparison table. Check: premium, deductible and exclusions are shown side by side, not just premium.
5. **Track the renewal date.** Track the renewal date for every active policy and flag it before the window closes. Skill: `money/insurance-cover-and-claims`. Output: renewal calendar. Check: no policy renews without a flag at least 30 days ahead.
6. **Prepare a claim on a loss event.** When a loss occurs, prepare the claim with the evidence the insurer asks for (transit, stock, cyber or product liability) and track it to resolution. Skill: `money/insurance-cover-and-claims`. Output: claim file with tracked status. Check: the claim cites the exact policy clause it is filed under. Ask the founder if: the loss involves a customer injury or a data breach needing legal review.
7. **Write the founder brief and stop.** Write the recommendation on financing, coverage or a claim outcome and stop for approval before any commitment. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: founder brief with approve/reject/park line. Check: no financing, coverage or claim decision is treated as final by this agent. Ask the founder if: always: these are founder_gate or human_assist capabilities.

Outputs: data room, investor/lender reporting pack, insurance coverage checklist, claim file. QA: every document version-dated; covenant status stated plainly; claim evidence matches the exact policy clause. Read point: monthly for reporting; annually for renewal; on the claim's resolution date. Duration: agent: 1 to 2 hours per cycle; waiting: lender, investor or insurer turnaround.

### MONEY-W06 Report unit economics and plan the growth budget

Trigger: weekly (CAC, LTV, revenue forecast) | monthly (OPEX budget, MMM refresh). Human role: the growth finance lead. Capabilities: MONEY-C09, MONEY-C10, MONEY-C11, MONEY-C18, MONEY-C63. Needs: metric contract: CAC bands, orders with cohort and channel tags, ad spend by channel, marketing calendar, subscription plan terms if any, prior period's forecast.

1. **Screen orders for fraud and bots.** Screen the order set for fraud and bot-driven orders before computing any cohort number. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: clean order set. Check: flagged orders are excluded and counted, not silently dropped.
2. **Compute the 4 labeled CACs.** Compute blended, media-only, paid and fully loaded CAC from spend and orders. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: 4 labeled CAC figures. Check: no CAC is shown without its label.
3. **Build cohorts and LTGP:CAC.** Build first-order cohorts, compute CAC payback and LTGP:CAC against the metric contract's fixed bands. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: cohort table with payback and LTGP:CAC. Check: LTV is gross profit, never revenue.
4. **Gate acquisition scale-up.** Gate any acquisition scale-up recommendation on first-order economics, not blended averages. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: scale/hold recommendation. Check: the recommendation names the cohort it is based on.
5. **Forecast revenue from separate inputs.** Forecast new-customer and returning revenue separately from the spend-efficiency curve, cohort repeat rate and the dated marketing calendar. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: revenue forecast with error bars. Check: short histories are regressed to the mean, never extrapolated straight-line.
6. **Build subscription unit economics.** If the brand has subscriptions, build churn against the correct prior-period base, conservative lifetime estimates, and prepaid-plan economics. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: subscription unit economics report. Check: churn denominator is the correct prior-period base, not total customers.
7. **Build the annual budget.** Build the annual budget separate from the rolling forecast, sizing OPEX and payroll to the brand's own profit target. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: annual budget. Check: OPEX is sized to the profit target, not last year's spend plus a percent.
8. **Answer the marginal spend question.** Answer marginal spend questions (the next 10,000 dollars) by ranking channels on CM3 and CAC payback. Skill: `money/growth-economics-stack`. Output: ranked marginal spend answer. Check: the ranking is computed in code, never asserted in prose.
9. **Cross-check with an MMM read where relevant.** Where blended MER and platform ROAS keep disagreeing, cross-check the scenario ranking against a marketing mix read. Skill: `money/mmm-stack-evaluate`. Output: MMM cross-check note. Check: the note states agreement or the size of the gap, never silently picks one number.
10. **Publish the weekly and monthly outputs.** Publish CAC/LTV and revenue forecast weekly, and the OPEX budget and subscription economics monthly. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: unit economics and budget report. Check: every figure states its window and freshness.

Outputs: CAC/LTV dashboard, revenue forecast, annual OPEX budget, subscription unit economics report, marginal spend ranking. QA: LTV always gross profit; no channel-level CAC presented as a finance number; scale gate based on first-order cohort, not blended. Read point: weekly for CAC/LTV/forecast; monthly for budget, against the annual target. Duration: agent: 1.5 to 2.5 hours per full run.

### MONEY-W07 GST, HSN and tax compliance tracking

Trigger: monthly, before GST return filing | event: new SKU or B2B dispatch. Human role: the person tracking GST and tax compliance. Capabilities: MONEY-C12, MONEY-G04, LIFE-C55, PROD-C60. Needs: brand brain: selling markets, SKU list, GSTR-2B, return and refund log, credit note register, stock transfer and B2B dispatch log, gift card and loyalty point ledger.

1. **Assign and validate HSN or SAC codes.** Build and keep the SKU-to-HSN/SAC map correct for every SKU, since a wrong or too-short code is a common cause of e-invoice portal rejections. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: SKU-to-HSN/SAC map. Check: no SKU ships without a validated code.
2. **Track GST nexus and registration thresholds.** Track GST or sales-tax nexus and registration thresholds per market the brand sells into. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: nexus and threshold status by market. Check: a market crossing a threshold is flagged before the filing deadline, not after.
3. **Prepare GSTR-1 and 3B inputs.** Prepare the sales and input-credit data GSTR-1 and 3B need, and match input credit against GSTR-2B. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: GSTR-1/3B input pack. Check: every mismatch between claimed and GSTR-2B credit is listed, not netted away.
4. **Claim marketplace TCS and TDS credit.** Reconcile TCS collected by marketplaces under Section 52 and TDS deducted against the credit actually available. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: TCS/TDS credit reconciliation. Check: credit claimed never exceeds what the marketplace's own statement supports.
5. **Raise e-way bills.** Raise e-way bills for B2B dispatch and stock transfer above the threshold value. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: e-way bill log. Check: every dispatch above threshold has a matching e-way bill number.
6. **Reconcile GST credit notes against returns.** Check that every completed return or refund has a matching GST credit note issued and filed on time. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: credit note reconciliation. Check: a refund without a matching credit note is flagged before the return period closes.
7. **Book liabilities correctly.** Book gift cards and loyalty points as liabilities recognized on redemption, and track FX exposure separately from GST. Skill: `money/gst-tax-compliance`. Output: liability ledger entries. Check: gift cards are never recognized as revenue at sale.
8. **Hand off filing to the accountant.** Package GSTR-1, 3B inputs, credit reconciliation and e-way bill log for the brand's accountant to file. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: filing handoff package. Check: the package states what this agent tracked versus what filing itself still needs a person. Ask the founder if: always: actual GST return filing stays with the brand's accountant.

Outputs: SKU-to-HSN/SAC map, GST nexus status, GSTR-1/3B input pack, e-way bill log, credit note reconciliation. QA: no SKU without a validated HSN/SAC code; TCS and GST on fees never netted; filing itself stays human, tracking is agent_does. Read point: monthly, ahead of the filing deadline. Duration: agent: 1 to 1.5 hours; waiting: accountant filing turnaround.

### MONEY-W08 Run the cash operations cycle: payables, receivables, payroll and expense

Trigger: on bill receipt and weekly aging (AP) | on invoice due date and weekly aging (AR) | monthly payroll cycle | monthly card cycle (expense). Human role: the accounts operations person. Capabilities: MONEY-C53, MONEY-C54, MONEY-C55, MONEY-C57. Needs: brand brain: approval chain by amount and vendor, purchase orders and receipts, B2B and wholesale invoice terms, payroll roster and statutory rates, travel-and-expense policy, corporate card statement.

1. **Capture every vendor bill.** Capture every incoming vendor bill and match it to its purchase order and goods receipt. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: matched bill set. Check: every bill has a matching PO and receipt or is flagged unmatched.
2. **Route AP approval.** Route each matched bill through the approval chain by amount and vendor, and schedule payment on the agreed terms. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: AP approval queue and payment schedule. Check: no bill is scheduled for payment without its approval step complete.
3. **Run the AP aging review.** Run the weekly aging review on unpaid bills and flag anything approaching a late-payment penalty. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: AP aging report. Check: every bill nearing its due date is flagged at least 3 days ahead.
4. **Invoice wholesale and B2B buyers.** Invoice wholesale and B2B buyers on their agreed terms. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: issued invoices. Check: every invoice states its due date from the agreed terms, never a default.
5. **Track AR to collection.** Track every invoice from issue to collection and run a structured dunning sequence on anything overdue. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: AR aging and dunning log. Check: the dunning sequence follows a fixed schedule, not an ad hoc reminder.
6. **Process payroll.** Process payroll for the cycle from the roster and hours or salary data. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: payroll run. Check: every employee's gross-to-net calculation is shown, not just the net figure.
7. **Prepare statutory filings.** Prepare the statutory filings that go with payroll: PF, ESI, professional tax and TDS, plus Form 16 at year end. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: statutory filing pack. Check: every filing amount ties back to the payroll run it came from. Ask the founder if: a filing needs a person's digital signature to submit.
8. **Collect and apply the expense policy.** Collect receipts for every reimbursable expense and corporate card charge and apply the travel-and-expense policy. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: policy-checked expense list. Check: every line is marked compliant or flagged against the written policy.
9. **Reconcile the card statement.** Reconcile the corporate card statement to the ledger. Skill: `money/cash-ops-cycle`. Output: card-to-ledger reconciliation. Check: every statement line matches a ledger entry or is flagged.
10. **Publish the cash operations summary.** Publish one summary of AP aging, AR aging, payroll cost and expense compliance for the cycle. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: cash operations summary. Check: every number states its cycle and freshness.

Outputs: AP approval queue and aging, issued B2B invoices and AR aging, payroll run and statutory filing pack, expense and card reconciliation. QA: approval chain enforced by amount and vendor; payroll gross-to-net always shown, not only net; expense policy applied line by line. Read point: weekly for AP/AR aging; monthly for payroll and card cycle. Duration: agent: 1.5 to 2 hours per cycle; waiting: bank payment runs and card statement date.

### MONEY-W09 Set and reconcile partner, affiliate and creator commercial terms

Trigger: each payout cycle | event: a commission tier change is proposed | event: creator deal agreed | weekly check of live posts. Human role: the partnerships finance lead. Capabilities: MEDIA-C51, MONEY-G03. Needs: brand brain: partner tiers, product category margins, customer status rules, affiliate network payout statement, creator agreement template, live creator and affiliate post list.

1. **Set commission rates.** Set commission rates by partner type, product category and customer status (new versus returning), so margin is protected on every payout. Skill: `money/partner-commercial-terms`. Output: commission rate table. Check: no commission rate is set without checking the category's own CM.
2. **Reconcile the affiliate network statement.** Reconcile what the affiliate network says is owed against actual delivered, non-returned orders. Skill: `money/partner-commercial-terms`. Output: affiliate payout reconciliation. Check: a returned or cancelled order never carries a paid commission.
3. **Approve the payout.** Approve the reconciled payout amount before it is sent. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: approved payout amount. Check: the payout amount matches the reconciliation exactly. Ask the founder if: always: commission tiers and payouts are founder_gate.
4. **Keep the creator agreement template current.** Keep one creator agreement template current: deliverables, usage rights, exclusivity, payment terms and the disclosure clause. Skill: `money/partner-commercial-terms`. Output: current agreement template. Check: the disclosure clause matches the current ASCI guideline.
5. **Check live posts for disclosure.** Check live creator and affiliate posts weekly for the ASCI and platform disclosure tags the agreement requires. Skill: `money/partner-commercial-terms`. Output: disclosure compliance check. Check: every non-compliant post is listed with the platform and the missing tag.
6. **Flag non-compliance.** Flag any creator or affiliate post missing disclosure to the founder for a takedown or correction request. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: flagged post list. Check: each flag names the specific rule the post breaks. Ask the founder if: a repeat offender needs the agreement itself reviewed.

Outputs: commission rate table, affiliate payout reconciliation, creator agreement template, disclosure compliance check. QA: commission never paid on a returned order; disclosure checked against the current ASCI rule; payout never sent without approval. Read point: each payout cycle, against the reconciliation; weekly for disclosure checks. Duration: agent: 45 minutes to 1.5 hours per cycle.

### MONEY-W10 Audit tracking across a surface

Trigger: monthly, rotating by surface | event: new account, quarterly recheck, or after a major site or app change. Human role: the analytics engineer running the tracking audit. Capabilities: MONEY-C20, MONEY-C21, MEDIA-C04, MONEY-C65. Needs: metric contract: current key events, GA4 property/stream config, GTM container export, Meta pixel/CAPI config, Google Ads conversion action list, consent management config.

1. **Pick the surface.** Pick the surface to audit this run: GA4, Meta pixel and CAPI, Google Ads, or server events, by trigger or by the standing rotation. Skill: `money/audit-tracking`. Output: chosen surface. Check: the surface picked matches the trigger, not a guess.
2. **Pull the surface's live config.** Pull the live configuration for the chosen surface: GA4 property/stream settings, or the Meta pixel/CAPI setup, or Google Ads conversion actions, or the server-side tag container. Skill: `none`. Output: live config export. Check: the export is dated and matches the currently published version.
3. **Run the surface-specific audit.** Run the fixed checklist for that surface: GA4 property/stream structure, timezone, retention, consent and key events; or Meta dedup, event match quality and consent gating; or Google Ads primary conversion action and enhanced conversions; or server-side container structure and duplicate tags. Skill: `money/audit-tracking`. Output: surface audit result. Check: every checklist item is marked pass, fail or unavailable, none skipped.
4. **Verify event-to-standard-event mapping.** Verify every store event maps to the surface's own standard event or key event, with value passed through. Skill: `money/audit-tracking`. Output: event mapping table. Check: every store event has a mapped standard event or is flagged unmapped.
5. **Test in the surface's own debug tool.** Test the live setup in DebugView, Meta's Test Events tool, or the Google Ads diagnostics tab as the surface requires. Skill: `money/audit-tracking`. Output: debug test result. Check: the test uses a real test transaction, not only a config read.
6. **Build the ranked fix list.** Build a ranked fix list: the exact current setting, the exact proposed setting, and whether it needs founder approval or a person to apply it. Skill: `shared/surface-audit`. Output: ranked fix list. Check: every fix row names the surface it touches.
7. **Compare to the last audit.** Compare this audit to the last one run on the same surface and flag anything that regressed. Skill: `shared/metric-read`. Output: regression flag list. Check: a regression is flagged even if the current state still passes.
8. **Hand off the fix list.** Hand the fix list to a developer or the founder, since this agent never applies a tracking change itself. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: fix list handoff. Check: no tracking configuration was changed by this agent. Ask the founder if: a fix needs a person with admin access to the ad account or the tag manager.

Outputs: surface audit report, event mapping table, ranked fix list. QA: one property or pixel per journey confirmed; internal traffic filtered; no page_view marked as a key event. Read point: monthly per surface; immediately after any major site or app change. Duration: agent: 45 to 75 minutes per surface.

### MONEY-W11 Enforce UTM discipline and reconcile attribution models

Trigger: monthly | trigger: platform ROAS and blended MER keep disagreeing, or a budget review. Human role: the analytics engineer keeping attribution honest. Capabilities: MONEY-C22, MONEY-C23, MONEY-C24, MEDIA-C56. Needs: one written UTM convention, every existing paid and owned link, GA4, Meta, Shopify and marketplace order counts, last-click and data-driven attribution reports.

1. **Write or confirm the UTM convention.** Write one UTM convention (source, medium, campaign, content, term) or confirm the existing one is still followed. Skill: `money/utm-link-hygiene`. Output: UTM convention document. Check: every parameter has a fixed, documented value set.
2. **Audit every existing link.** Audit every existing paid and owned link against the convention. Skill: `money/utm-link-hygiene`. Output: link audit table. Check: every non-conforming link is listed with its exact fix.
3. **Clean fake organic and direct rows.** Clean fake organic or direct rows out of campaign tables caused by missing or broken UTMs before anyone reads them. Skill: `money/utm-link-hygiene`. Output: cleaned channel table. Check: no channel table is published with a known UTM leak still in it.
4. **Cross-check order counts.** Cross-check GA4, Meta, Shopify and marketplace order counts against each other and against store orders for the period. Skill: `money/attribution-model-reconcile`. Output: order count cross-check table. Check: every gap over 2 percent has a named cause.
5. **Flag bot-driven spikes.** Flag any spike in one platform's order or session count that does not match store orders, as a possible bot signal. Skill: `money/attribution-model-reconcile`. Output: bot spike flag list. Check: a flagged spike is handed to the security scan before anyone reacts to it.
6. **Report the attribution model gap.** Report the gap between attribution models (last-click versus data-driven, platform versus GA4) per channel. Skill: `money/attribution-model-reconcile`. Output: attribution gap report. Check: the gap is shown per channel, never as one blended number.
7. **Run or schedule a holdout test.** Before a big reallocation call, run or schedule a holdout test rather than trust the attribution gap alone. Skill: `money/attribution-model-reconcile`. Output: holdout test plan or read. Check: the test result, once available, is cited by its own confidence interval.
8. **Evaluate a third-party attribution stack if warranted.** When platform ROAS and blended MER keep disagreeing after reconciliation, decide whether a paid attribution or MMM tool is worth its cost against the brand's own blended-MER and lift-test approach. Skill: `money/mmm-stack-evaluate`. Output: MMM stack evaluation with a recommendation. Check: the recommendation compares tool cost to the brand's own current accuracy, not vendor claims alone. Ask the founder if: the decision commits to a recurring paid subscription.
9. **Document the attribution convention.** Document which model the brand uses for which decision, so nobody reads a number without knowing its source model. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: documented attribution convention. Check: the convention states which model governs budget decisions.

Outputs: UTM convention and link audit, order count cross-check, attribution gap report, MMM stack evaluation. QA: no channel table published with a known UTM leak; bot spikes flagged before anyone reacts; MMM tool judged against the brand's own lift tests. Read point: monthly for UTM and cross-check; on the trigger for MMM evaluation. Duration: agent: 1 to 1.5 hours; waiting: holdout test read window if one is run.

### MONEY-W12 Build the post-purchase attribution survey

Trigger: event: building or refreshing the attribution model | event: a new high-spend channel needs proof it works. Human role: the analytics engineer adding a zero-party data source. Capabilities: LIFE-C50. Needs: brand brain: product lines, customer types, current attribution gap report, thank-you page template.

1. **Draft the one question.** Draft the one-question, thank-you-page survey: how did you hear about us. Skill: `money/attribution-survey-build`. Output: draft survey question. Check: the question is one sentence, plain language.
2. **Branch the answer options.** Branch the answer options by product or customer type where the channel mix differs. Skill: `money/attribution-survey-build`. Output: branched answer set. Check: every branch maps to a channel the brand actually runs.
3. **Place it on the thank-you page.** Prepare the placement spec for the thank-you page as a developer brief, since this agent does not write site code. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: placement spec. Check: the spec names the exact page and does not touch checkout.
4. **Set the sample and response window.** Set the sample size and response window needed for the answers to be usable. Skill: `money/attribution-survey-build`. Output: sample and window plan. Check: the plan states the minimum responses before a channel share number is trusted.
5. **Turn answers into a channel-share number.** Turn the collected answers into a channel-share number, in code. Skill: `money/attribution-survey-build`. Output: channel-share table. Check: the table states its sample size next to every percentage.
6. **Compare to platform attribution.** Compare the survey's channel share to the platform and GA4 attribution reports. Skill: `money/attribution-model-reconcile`. Output: comparison note. Check: the comparison states agreement or the size of the gap, never picks one silently.
7. **Publish and refresh on a cadence.** Publish the result and set a refresh cadence tied to when a new high-spend channel is added. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: published survey read with refresh date. Check: the refresh date is tied to an event, not left open-ended.

Outputs: survey question and branching spec, channel-share table, comparison to platform attribution. QA: sample size always shown next to a percentage; survey never conflated with platform-reported attribution without a labeled comparison. Read point: at the refresh cadence, or when a new channel needs proof. Duration: agent: 30 to 45 minutes to build; waiting: weeks to collect enough responses.

### MONEY-W13 Publish and maintain reporting dashboards

Trigger: daily (finance scoreboard) | monthly (dashboard maintenance). Human role: the person keeping the numbers everyone reads current. Capabilities: MONEY-C14, MONEY-C30. Needs: metric contract, P&L ladder, CM report, GA4 saved reports, Shopify home cards, budget scenario views.

1. **Pull the day's or period's numbers.** Pull revenue, profit and cash as three separate reads for the dashboard. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: raw dashboard inputs. Check: revenue, profit and cash are never blended into one figure.
2. **Compute the daily contribution-margin scoreboard.** Compute the daily CM scoreboard. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: daily scoreboard. Check: the scoreboard states its window ends on the last full day.
3. **Compute the weekly profit memo inputs.** Compute CM, new versus returning split, variance and inventory risk for the weekly profit memo. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: weekly profit memo inputs. Check: new versus returning is split from cohort data, not guessed.
4. **Audit existing dashboard cards.** Audit the brand's standard dashboards (Shopify home cards, GA4 saved reports, budget scenario views) for cards that no longer read correctly. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: dashboard audit list. Check: every unreliable card is named with the reason it is unreliable.
5. **Remove unreliable cards.** Remove or flag cards found unreliable in the audit. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: cleaned dashboard set. Check: no removed card leaves a silent gap; a replacement is proposed.
6. **Add cards that answer a real question.** Add cards that answer a real, currently asked question, never a vanity metric. Skill: `money/dashboard-build`. Output: updated dashboard set. Check: every new card traces to a question someone actually asked.
7. **Publish the scoreboard and memo.** Publish the daily scoreboard and the weekly profit memo. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: published scoreboard and memo. Check: the memo reads as plain, decision-focused writing a founder will actually read.

Outputs: daily CM scoreboard, weekly profit memo, maintained dashboard set. QA: revenue, profit and cash kept as separate reads; every card traces to a real question; windows end on the last full day. Read point: daily for the scoreboard; monthly for dashboard maintenance, against what changed. Duration: agent: 20 to 30 minutes daily; 1 hour monthly for maintenance.

### MONEY-W14 Org design and structured hiring, scorecard to offer

Trigger: quarterly (org review) | event: a hire trigger fires. Human role: the person running hiring like a hiring manager, not a favor. Capabilities: MONEY-C31, MONEY-C32. Needs: brand brain: org chart, revenue stage, current roster, silo revenue and workload data, role library and comp bands, candidate applications or referrals.

1. **Name one owner per silo.** Name one owner for each core store silo (site, creative, media, lifecycle, ops, finance) so nothing has two owners or none. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: silo ownership map. Check: every silo has exactly one named owner.
2. **Set the next-hire trigger as a number.** Write the next-hire trigger as a number (revenue per employee, ticket backlog, hours over capacity), never a feeling. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: hire trigger definitions. Check: each trigger is a stated number and threshold, not a description.
3. **Run the quarterly org-chart-vs-revenue review.** Run the quarterly review of org chart against revenue stage, flagging key-person risk where one person is the only owner of a silo. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: org review with key-person flags. Check: every flag names the silo and the single point of failure.
4. **Check whether a trigger has fired.** Check the current numbers against the hire trigger thresholds. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: trigger status. Check: a fired trigger states the exact number that crossed the threshold.
5. **Build the job description and scorecard.** Build a structured job description naming the silo, the artefacts the role owns and the one metric it is judged on. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: job description and scorecard. Check: the scorecard names one metric, never a list of vague traits.
6. **Write the honest job ad.** Write a job ad that states the real scope and the real metric, not an inflated title. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: job ad. Check: the ad matches the scorecard exactly.
7. **Set comp bands.** Set comp bands from the role library and the brand's own budget before the search opens. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: comp band. Check: the band fits inside the OPEX budget for this hire.
8. **Build the sourced interview question set.** Build a structured, sourced interview question set tied to the scorecard's one metric. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: interview question set. Check: every question maps to a scorecard item.
9. **Score candidates.** Score each candidate against the scorecard, not against gut feel. Skill: `money/hiring-scorecard-to-offer`. Output: scored candidate table. Check: every score cites the specific answer it came from.
10. **Recommend an offer.** Recommend the top-scored candidate and the comp band position for the offer. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: offer recommendation. Check: the recommendation states the score gap to the next candidate.
11. **Stop for the founder to make the offer.** Stop before any offer, comp discussion or rejection reaches a candidate. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: approval record. Check: no candidate is contacted with an offer or rejection by this agent. Ask the founder if: always: hiring decisions and conversations are human_assist.

Outputs: silo ownership map, hire trigger definitions, job description and scorecard, job ad, interview question set, scored candidate table. QA: hire trigger is always a number; scorecard has exactly one governing metric; no candidate contacted by the agent. Read point: quarterly for org review; on the trigger firing for a live search. Duration: agent: 2 to 3 hours across a full search; waiting: candidate pipeline time.

### MONEY-W15 Write SOPs, onboarding, the handbook and offboarding

Trigger: event: new hire or a repeated procedure | annually or on a hire growth milestone (handbook) | event: resignation or termination notice. Human role: the person keeping operating knowledge out of people's heads. Capabilities: MONEY-C33, MONEY-C68, MONEY-C69. Needs: brand brain: role library, procedure list, current SOP set, current handbook, exit checklist template.

1. **Spot a repeated procedure.** Spot when the same procedure has been asked for a third time without a written SOP. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: candidate procedure for an SOP. Check: the procedure has been repeated at least 3 times.
2. **Write the SOP.** Write the SOP as a live operating artefact: purpose, steps, the tool used at each step, and the check before it counts as done. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: draft SOP. Check: every step names its tool.
3. **Name one owner.** Give the SOP one named owner with a freshness date. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: owned SOP. Check: the owner is a person, not a team name.
4. **Assemble the week-1 onboarding set.** Assemble the SOPs a new hire needs in week 1 for their specific role. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: week-1 onboarding pack. Check: every pack item is an SOP that exists and is current.
5. **Keep the handbook current.** Keep one current handbook covering leave, work-from-home, code of conduct and India-specific requirements including the POSH policy. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: updated handbook. Check: the POSH policy section is present and current.
6. **Confirm acknowledgement.** Confirm every employee has acknowledged the current handbook. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: acknowledgement log. Check: no staff member is missing from the log.
7. **Run the offboarding checklist.** On a resignation or termination notice, run one checklist: same-day access revocation, asset return, knowledge transfer, exit interview, final settlement. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: completed offboarding checklist. Check: access revocation is same-day, not same-week. Ask the founder if: the exit involves a dispute needing legal review.
8. **Update SOP ownership after an exit.** Reassign SOP ownership for anything the exiting employee owned. Skill: `money/sop-and-hr-docs`. Output: reassigned ownership list. Check: no SOP is left with an owner who no longer has access.

Outputs: week-1 onboarding pack, current handbook with acknowledgement log, offboarding checklist. QA: every SOP has one owner and a freshness date; access revoked same-day on exit; handbook includes the POSH policy. Read point: on the triggering event, checked against the SOP and handbook freshness dates. Duration: agent: 30 to 90 minutes per SOP or checklist run.

### MONEY-W16 Design the management rhythm and team facilitation guides

Trigger: quarterly. Human role: the person designing how the team actually talks to each other. Capabilities: MONEY-C34, MONEY-C35. Needs: brand brain: team size, current meeting calendar, scoreboard metrics, past facilitation session notes.

1. **Design the meeting cadence.** Design the store's meeting cadence: annual reset, quarterly deep dive, weekly trading review, ad hoc, sized to the team's actual size. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: meeting cadence design. Check: no meeting is added without a stated purpose and a metric it reviews.
2. **Build the visible scoreboard.** Build the visible scoreboard the weekly trading review runs against. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: scoreboard for the review. Check: the scoreboard uses the same metric contract numbers as the finance dashboard.
3. **Design cascading messaging.** Design how a decision from the quarterly deep dive cascades down to the weekly review and to individual owners. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: cascade messaging design. Check: every cascade step names who tells whom, by when.
4. **Prepare a trust or dysfunction assessment.** Prepare a structured team dysfunction assessment or trust exercise for the founder to run. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: assessment guide. Check: the guide is a run sheet, not a theory summary.
5. **Prepare conflict and accountability tools.** Prepare conflict and accountability facilitation tools tied to a real recent incident, not a generic template. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: facilitation tool set. Check: each tool ties to a metric the session should move.
6. **Tie every guide to one metric.** Tie every facilitation guide to the one metric the session is meant to move. Skill: `money/mgmt-rhythm-and-facilitation`. Output: metric-tied guide set. Check: no guide is handed over without its metric named.
7. **Hand the guides to the founder.** Hand the run sheets to the founder to run directly in the meeting; this agent never facilitates a live session itself. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: handoff package. Check: the handoff states this agent prepared the guide, not the outcome.

Outputs: meeting cadence design, visible scoreboard, trust and facilitation guides. QA: every meeting has a stated purpose and a metric; facilitation guides are run sheets, not theory; founder runs the session, agent does not. Read point: quarterly, against whether the last cadence change stuck. Duration: agent: 1 to 2 hours per quarter.

### MONEY-W17 Evaluate and manage vendors, 3PLs and agencies

Trigger: monthly | event: an RFP is warranted. Human role: the ops manager running vendor selection like a real procurement process. Capabilities: MONEY-C36. Needs: brand brain: current vendor list and contracts, candidate vendor list, invoice history, SLA terms.

1. **Run competitive RFP.** Run vendor and 3PL selection by a competitive RFP, never a single quote. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: RFP responses. Check: at least 3 vendors respond before a selection is made.
2. **Run a facility visit or reference check.** Run a facility visit for a 3PL, or a reference check for an agency. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: visit or reference notes. Check: the notes name what was seen or confirmed, not a vendor's own claim.
3. **Put SLAs in writing.** Put SLAs in writing with a stated consequence for a miss. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: written SLA. Check: every SLA line has a numeric target and a stated consequence.
4. **Audit every invoice against the SLA.** Audit every incoming invoice against the SLA terms and the RFP-agreed rate. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: invoice audit result. Check: every overcharge or SLA miss is listed with the exact invoice line.
5. **Score the vendor.** Score the vendor on delivery, quality and responsiveness against the SLA, on a fixed scorecard. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: vendor scorecard. Check: the score is computed from the SLA data, not a subjective rating.
6. **Flag a miss for renegotiation or exit.** Flag a vendor scoring below the threshold for renegotiation or replacement. Skill: `money/vendor-rfp-evaluation`. Output: renegotiation or exit flag. Check: the flag states which SLA line triggered it.
7. **Publish the standing vendor scorecard.** Publish the standing vendor scorecard monthly so nobody re-litigates the same vendor from memory. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: published vendor scorecard. Check: every vendor has a current score and its trend.

Outputs: RFP comparison, written SLA, invoice audit result, vendor scorecard. QA: at least 3 vendors compared before selection; every SLA has a numeric target and consequence; invoice audit checks the actual agreed rate. Read point: monthly against the scorecard trend; on RFP completion. Duration: agent: 1 to 2 hours per RFP cycle; 30 minutes monthly for the scorecard.

### MONEY-W18 Govern tooling, SaaS renewals, contracts and freelancer engagements

Trigger: quarterly (tooling audit) | on every contract | before onboarding and on renewal (vendor risk) | on approaching notice-period deadline (SaaS renewal) | on engagement start, milestone, end (freelancer). Human role: the person keeping tool spend and contracts from drifting. Capabilities: MONEY-C39, MONEY-C67, MONEY-C77, MONEY-C78, MONEY-C66. Needs: brand brain: software and tooling list, revenue stage, SaaS usage data and renewal dates, contract templates, vendor risk questionnaire, freelancer scope of work.

1. **Audit tooling against revenue stage.** Audit the store's software and tooling spend against its revenue stage, never adding an ERP or a tool too early. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: tooling audit. Check: every tool is judged against the current revenue stage, not a wish list.
2. **Name a tracker owner before adding a system.** Name a tracker owner before any new system is added. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: owner assignment. Check: no new system is proposed without a named owner.
3. **Watch SaaS renewal dates and usage.** Watch every SaaS contract's auto-renew date and usage data. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: renewal watch list. Check: every contract's notice-period deadline is tracked, not just the renewal date.
4. **Draft the renegotiation or cancellation email.** Draft a renegotiation or cancellation email with the brand's own usage numbers, before the notice window closes. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: draft email. Check: the draft cites the brand's own usage, not a generic ask. Ask the founder if: always: sending the email needs founder approval.
5. **Run a vendor risk assessment.** Before a new SaaS tool, ESP, 3PL or agency gets access to customer or payment data, run a standard risk questionnaire. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: risk assessment result. Check: the assessment confirms a data-processing agreement is signed before access is granted.
6. **Redline incoming contracts.** Run every incoming contract (NDA, MSA, vendor agreement, employment agreement) through a redline checklist before it goes to the founder. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: redlined contract with notes. Check: every deviation from the template is flagged with its risk. Ask the founder if: a clause needs legal review beyond the standard checklist.
7. **Run the freelancer engagement lifecycle.** Run the scope of work, IP assignment, rate agreement and invoicing against milestones for a freelance or contractor engagement. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: freelancer engagement record. Check: IP assignment is explicit in every engagement.
8. **Handle renewal or offboarding.** At engagement end, run renewal or offboarding for the freelancer the same way an employee exit is run. Skill: `money/vendor-saas-contract-governance`. Output: renewal or offboarding record. Check: access is revoked same-day if the engagement is not renewed.
9. **Stop for the founder on any commitment.** Stop before any contract is signed, any SaaS renewal is cancelled, or any freelancer is engaged. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: approval record. Check: no contract or engagement commitment is made by this agent. Ask the founder if: always: contract, vendor and engagement decisions are founder_gate or human_assist.

Outputs: tooling audit, SaaS renewal watch list and draft emails, vendor risk assessments, redlined contracts, freelancer engagement records. QA: every new system has a named owner; no vendor gets data access without a signed DPA; IP assignment explicit in every freelance engagement. Read point: quarterly for tooling; continuous for renewal and contract triggers. Duration: agent: 1 to 2 hours per audit cycle; ongoing for contract and renewal events.

### MONEY-W19 Plan customer-service capacity and handle DPDP data-subject requests

Trigger: monthly (CS capacity) | event: a customer submits an access or erasure request. Human role: the ops manager sizing support and protecting customer data rights. Capabilities: MONEY-C38, LIFE-C66. Needs: sales forecast curve, current ticket volume and cost per ticket, system map (ESP, helpdesk, loyalty, subscription, order data).

1. **Staff CS tiers from the forecast curve.** Staff CS tiers from the sales forecast curve, not last month's headcount. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: CS staffing plan. Check: staffing scales with the forecast curve, not a flat headcount.
2. **Track cost per ticket.** Track cost per ticket by tier. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: cost per ticket by tier. Check: the cost is computed from real payroll cost, not an assumed rate.
3. **Define a complex ticket before routing.** Define what counts as a complex ticket before routing it to a higher tier. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: complex-ticket definition. Check: the definition is a checklist, not a judgment call per ticket.
4. **Trace a rising resolution time upstream.** When resolution time rises, trace the cause upstream (a product defect, a policy change, a staffing gap) rather than just re-staffing. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: root-cause note. Check: the note names the upstream cause, not only the symptom.
5. **Find every system holding the requester's data.** On a DPDP access or erasure request, find every system that holds that person's data: ESP, helpdesk, loyalty, subscription, order records. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: data location map. Check: every connected system is checked, none skipped.
6. **Assemble or erase the data.** Assemble the data for an access request, or prepare the erasure across every located system for an erasure request. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: assembled data or erasure plan. Check: an erasure plan lists every system by name. Ask the founder if: an erasure would remove data needed for an open legal or tax obligation.
7. **Respond within the statutory window.** Track the DPDP response deadline and confirm the response goes out inside it. Skill: `money/cs-capacity-and-dpdp`. Output: response confirmation. Check: the response date is logged against the statutory deadline.
8. **Log the request.** Log every DPDP request and its resolution for audit purposes. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: DPDP request log. Check: the log is queryable by requester and by date.

Outputs: CS staffing plan, cost per ticket by tier, DPDP request log with responses. QA: staffing follows the forecast curve; every DPDP request checked against every connected system; erasure never breaks a legal retention obligation. Read point: monthly for CS capacity; against the statutory deadline for each DPDP request. Duration: agent: 1 hour monthly for capacity; 30 to 60 minutes per DPDP request.

### MONEY-W20 Run the weekly fraud, bot and abuse scan

Trigger: weekly. Human role: the security owner running the weekly scan. Capabilities: MONEY-C41, MONEY-C42, MONEY-C48, MONEY-C76. Needs: server/CDN logs, endpoint list, order and payment data, chargeback log, COD refusal log, current bot and WAF policy, domain and DNS records.

1. **Rebuild the bot and agent traffic baseline.** Rebuild the bot and AI-agent traffic baseline by agent, endpoint and intent. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: traffic baseline. Check: the baseline never relies on User-Agent alone.
2. **Re-score risk tiers.** Re-score risk tiers per endpoint with continuous tiering, not a one-time label. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: risk tier table. Check: friction is placed at checkout and account changes, not at browsing.
3. **Check for new reseller, scalper or card-testing patterns.** Check for new reseller or scalper patterns and card-testing bursts. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: pattern flag list. Check: a card-testing burst is flagged by its cluster of small failed authorizations.
4. **Review AVS, CVV and 3DS settings.** Review AVS/CVV/3DS settings against the false-decline rate. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: AVS/3DS review. Check: fraud caught is tracked alongside the false-decline rate, never fraud alone.
5. **Set the chargeback alert threshold.** Set the chargeback alert threshold below the processor's own flag level. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: chargeback threshold setting. Check: the threshold sits below the processor's own flag point.
6. **Control COD refusal and prepayment triggers.** Control COD refusal rate and set prepayment triggers for repeat refusers. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: COD control list. Check: a repeat refuser is flagged by name before the next order, not after.
7. **Detect return and refund abuse.** Find wardrobing, serial high-return-rate customers, and refunds issued without a matching return. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: return abuse flag list. Check: a flagged customer shows their return rate against the store average.
8. **Watch domain, DNS and impersonation risk.** Lock-check the domain registrar account, audit DNS for dangling records, and watch for lookalike domains, counterfeit listings and Safe Browsing flags. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: domain and impersonation watch result. Check: a lookalike domain or counterfeit listing is reported with its URL and date found.
9. **Retest after any recent WAF change.** If the WAF or bot policy changed this week, retest the baseline against it. Skill: `money/security-scan-weekly`. Output: retest result. Check: the retest confirms the change did not silently block a legitimate agent.
10. **Build the weekly fix list.** Roll every finding into one ranked fix list for a human to apply to the WAF or payment settings. Skill: `shared/surface-audit`. Output: weekly fix list. Check: every accepted agent keeps scoped authorization, never standing payment access.

Outputs: bot traffic baseline, fraud control review, return abuse flags, domain and impersonation watch, weekly fix list. QA: no rule relies on User-Agent alone; false-decline rate tracked alongside fraud caught; no live WAF or gateway change made by this agent. Read point: weekly, against last week's baseline and fix list. Duration: agent: 1.5 to 2 hours.

### MONEY-W21 Run the monthly authentication, web application and backup audit

Trigger: monthly. Human role: the security owner running the monthly technical audit. Capabilities: MONEY-C43, MONEY-C44, MONEY-C47. Needs: admin login and session config, storefront and checkout code access, backup schedule and last restore test, incident runbook.

1. **Audit login and session design.** Audit login, session and multi-factor authentication design: 2FA or passkeys for admins, session-cookie attributes, password hashing and rate limiting. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: auth design audit. Check: every admin account has 2FA or a passkey confirmed, not assumed.
2. **Check enumeration prevention.** Check that login and password-reset flows do not leak whether an email exists. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: enumeration check result. Check: the check is tested against the live flow, not the settings page alone.
3. **Audit storefront and checkout code.** Audit storefront and checkout code for XSS, injection, CSRF, clickjacking, CORS and postMessage defects. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: web app defect list. Check: every defect requires parameterised queries or the equivalent fix named exactly.
4. **Produce the developer fix list.** Produce a developer fix list from the defect audit. Skill: `shared/developer-brief`. Output: developer fix list. Check: each fix names the exact file or endpoint where possible.
5. **Run daily versioned backups check.** Confirm daily versioned backups exist in 3 separated locations with a ransomware-safe copy. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: backup coverage check. Check: a ransomware-safe, offline copy is confirmed, not assumed from the vendor's claim.
6. **Test restores end to end.** Test a restore end to end this month, not just confirm the backup exists. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: restore test result. Check: the test restores a real file and confirms it opens correctly.
7. **Maintain the incident runbook.** Maintain one unified incident runbook covering breach, skimmer and ransomware scenarios. Skill: `money/security-audit-monthly`. Output: updated incident runbook. Check: the runbook names who does what in the first hour of each scenario.
8. **Publish the monthly audit result.** Publish the auth, web app and backup audit as one ranked fix list. Skill: `shared/surface-audit`. Output: monthly audit fix list. Check: every fix states whether it needs a developer or a founder decision.

Outputs: auth design audit, web app defect list and developer fix list, backup coverage and restore test result, updated incident runbook. QA: 2FA confirmed live, not assumed; restore tested end to end, not just scheduled; no code change applied by this agent. Read point: monthly, against last month's fix list. Duration: agent: 1.5 to 2.5 hours.

### MONEY-W22 Run the quarterly security audit program

Trigger: quarterly. Human role: the security owner running the four-pillar quarterly program. Capabilities: MONEY-C46, MONEY-C49, MONEY-C50, MONEY-C72, MONEY-C73, MONEY-C75. Needs: staff roster and admin role map, privacy policy and consent banner, sending domains, API key and credential inventory, staff admin-access list, prior quarter's audit.

1. **Map roles to admin areas.** Map every staff role to only the admin areas it needs, least-privilege by default. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: least-privilege map. Check: no role has an admin area it does not use.
