2. **Run the full staff access audit.** Run a full staff access audit with same-day offboarding confirmed for any exits since the last audit. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: access audit result. Check: every exited staff member's access is confirmed revoked, not assumed.
3. **Monitor admin logins.** Monitor admin logins for new locations or devices since the last audit. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: login anomaly list. Check: a new location or device is flagged even if the login succeeded.
4. **Confirm GDPR and CCPA exposure.** Confirm GDPR/CCPA exposure by customer location and publish a privacy policy covering AI-agent interactions with a compliant consent banner. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: privacy compliance check. Check: AI-agent interactions are named explicitly in the policy.
5. **Remove non-payment third-party scripts before consent.** Remove any non-payment third-party script that fires before consent is given. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: script removal list. Check: every removed script is confirmed by a live page check, not the settings page alone.
6. **Check email authentication.** Check SPF, DKIM, DMARC and BIMI on every domain the brand sends email from (transactional, marketing, support) and move DMARC from monitor-only to enforced where safe. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: email auth status by domain. Check: every sending domain is checked, none skipped because it seems minor.
7. **Rotate API keys and credentials.** Rotate API keys, tokens and service credentials on schedule and immediately on any staff exit. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: rotation log. Check: every credential shows who or what holds it and its last rotation date.
8. **Confirm platform SOC and PCI claims.** Confirm any platform's SOC or PCI claim before it is repeated in a brand document. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: confirmed claim list. Check: a claim is only repeated if the certificate or report was actually checked.
9. **Run phishing simulation and training.** Run a recurring phishing simulation and short security training for every staff member with admin access, and track who clicks, who reports, and who needs a repeat. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: training and simulation result. Check: every admin-access staff member has a tracked result, not a blanket pass.
10. **Maintain the hosted-vs-self-hosted responsibility map.** Maintain the map of which security responsibility sits with the hosting platform and which sits with the brand. Skill: `money/security-audit-quarterly`. Output: responsibility map. Check: every control is assigned to exactly one side, never left ambiguous.
11. **Assign a pillar owner and publish the scorecard.** Assign a named owner per pillar (access, privacy, email/credentials, training) and publish the quarterly security scorecard. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: quarterly security scorecard. Check: every pillar has a named owner and a score.

Outputs: least-privilege access audit, privacy and consent compliance check, email authentication status, credential rotation log, training and phishing simulation result, quarterly security scorecard. QA: same-day offboarding confirmed, not assumed; DMARC policy state stated exactly; every control assigned to one named side. Read point: quarterly, against the prior quarter's scorecard. Duration: agent: 3 to 4 hours across the program.

### MONEY-W23 Confirm PCI DSS scope and complete the SAQ

Trigger: annually | event: any checkout payment-flow change. Human role: the security owner keeping the brand out of card-data scope. Capabilities: MONEY-C74. Needs: checkout payment flow diagram, payment gateway integration method, prior SAQ if any.

1. **Map the payment flow.** Map exactly how the checkout takes payment: hosted field, redirect, or direct API. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: payment flow map. Check: the map states whether raw card data ever touches the brand's own servers.
2. **Determine the applicable SAQ.** Determine which PCI DSS self-assessment questionnaire type applies given the payment flow. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: applicable SAQ type. Check: the SAQ type is justified by the exact flow mapped, not assumed from the gateway's marketing.
3. **Confirm the checkout stays out of scope.** Confirm the checkout does not touch raw card data outside the confirmed scope. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: scope confirmation. Check: the confirmation is based on a live checkout trace, not a config read alone.
4. **Complete the SAQ questions.** Complete the SAQ questionnaire's factual items from the confirmed scope and flow. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: completed SAQ draft. Check: every answer traces to a checked fact, none guessed.
5. **Flag any item needing a person.** Flag any SAQ item needing a signature or a third-party attestation. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: flagged item list. Check: no signature or attestation is provided by this agent. Ask the founder if: always: submitting the signed SAQ needs a person.
6. **Recheck on any payment-flow change.** Recheck scope immediately after any change to how checkout takes payment. Skill: `money/pci-saq-scope`. Output: recheck result. Check: a flow change always triggers a fresh scope check, never reuses the old SAQ silently.

Outputs: payment flow map, applicable SAQ type, completed SAQ draft. QA: scope confirmed by a live trace, not settings alone; no card data claim made without a checked fact; signature step always left to a person. Read point: annually, and immediately on a payment-flow change. Duration: agent: 1 to 1.5 hours; waiting: gateway documentation confirmation.

### MONEY-W24 Correlate account-takeover signals across connected systems

Trigger: continuous, reviewed daily. Human role: the security owner watching what no single tool sees alone. Capabilities: MONEY-C81. Needs: Shopify admin login logs, ad account login logs, ESP login logs, payment processor login logs.

1. **Pull login events from every system.** Pull login geography, device and time for Shopify admin, the ad accounts, the ESP and the payment processor. Skill: `money/account-takeover-correlate`. Output: cross-system login event set. Check: every connected system's events are pulled, none skipped.
2. **Match by person and time window.** Match login events by the same admin person across systems within a short time window. Skill: `money/account-takeover-correlate`. Output: matched login sequence. Check: a match requires the same person's account across at least 2 systems.
3. **Flag geography or device mismatches.** Flag a login sequence where geography or device differs from that person's normal pattern across systems. Skill: `money/account-takeover-correlate`. Output: anomaly flag list. Check: an anomaly is flagged even if each single system's own alert stayed quiet.
4. **Rank flags by exposure.** Rank flags by what that account could touch: payment settings, ad budget, or customer data export. Skill: `money/account-takeover-correlate`. Output: ranked exposure list. Check: the ranking states the worst action that account could take, not just the login itself.
5. **Escalate the top-ranked flag same day.** Escalate any high-exposure flag to the founder the same day it is found. Skill: `shared/plain-english-card`. Output: same-day escalation. Check: the escalation names the exact systems and the exact anomaly. Ask the founder if: the flag suggests an account is currently compromised.
6. **Log the daily review.** Log the daily review even when no anomaly is found, so the absence of a signal is itself recorded. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: daily review log. Check: every day has a logged review, not only the days with a finding.

Outputs: cross-system login anomaly list, ranked exposure list, daily review log. QA: never relies on one system's own alert alone; every anomaly ranked by what the account could touch; every day logged, found or not. Read point: daily, reviewed same day for any high-exposure flag. Duration: agent: 15 to 25 minutes daily.

## Brand strategist (BRAND), 30 workflows

### BRAND-W01 Run the customer research sweep

Trigger: scheduled monthly | on demand before an avatar or positioning refresh. Human role: what a customer insights analyst does at the start of every research cycle. Capabilities: BRAND-C01, BRAND-C02, BRAND-C03, BRAND-C04, BRAND-C08, BRAND-C14. Needs: brand brain: product list and SKUs, connector: reviews export (site + marketplace), connector: support desk export (tickets, returns, calls), connector: GA4 / on-site search log, budget ledger: remaining balance for any paid deep-research pass.

1. **Pull and tag reviews by star band.** Export every review per SKU with rating and date. Read 1-2 star for the real objection under the surface complaint. Read 4-5 star for the job the product actually did. Flag Hinglish and regional-language phrasing as-is, do not translate away the voice. Skill: `brand/research-customers/reviews`. Output: tagged quote set by star band, objection frequency list. Check: every quote has SKU, star, source and date; reviewer full name stripped.
2. **Pull and tag support signal.** Export tickets, return reasons and call transcripts for the period. Tag by the underlying failure, not the customer's stated request. Flag sizing, fit or delivery-delay anxieties (India-relevant: COD refusal reasons, pincode delay complaints). Skill: `brand/research-customers/tickets`. Output: struggle theme list, quote set with consent flag. Check: consent status logged before any quote leaves the ticket system.
3. **Run forum and social listening.** Search Reddit, Quora, Instagram comments and relevant WhatsApp-forwarded review threads in the avatar's own words for language, objections and sub-avatars a review file misses. Skill: `brand/research-customers/social`. Output: language bank additions, sub-avatar candidate notes. Check: every source has a URL and date; nothing from a private group is quoted.
4. **Mine on-site search and browse-without-buy.** Read the brand's own on-site search terms and sessions that browsed a category or PDP without adding to cart, for what shoppers were actually trying to find. Skill: `brand/research-customers/onsite-behavior`. Output: unmet-search-term list, job clue notes. Check: terms are deduplicated and volume-ranked before being called a pattern.
5. **Cross-check patterns across sources.** Line up review, ticket, social and search findings. Keep only patterns that show up in 2 or more independent sources. Drop single contradictory outliers. Skill: `none`. Output: cross-verified pattern list. Check: every pattern kept lists which 2+ sources it came from.
6. **Verify one prioritized claim with AI deep research, if needed.** If a pattern is high-stakes (would change the primary avatar or a positioning claim) and thin on quotes, run one AI deep-research pass on that single candidate and verify every returned quote at its original source before it is used. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: verified addendum with original-source links. Check: budget.py checked before the call; every quote traced back to its live source page. Ask the founder if: the verification would push the run over the working stop ($160).
7. **Update the verbatim language bank.** Append new exact-quoted phrases to the per-avatar language bank, tagged by source and repetition count, anonymized. Skill: `none`. Output: updated language bank file. Check: no full name or contact detail is stored with a quote.
8. **Hand off findings for avatar work.** Package the cross-verified patterns, quote sets and language bank update as the input packet for the avatar-record or positioning workflow that runs next. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: research packet, logged in the registry. Check: packet lists every source type used and what was not available.

Outputs: tagged quote set by star band, struggle theme list, language bank update, sub-avatar candidate notes, cross-verified pattern list. QA: every quote has a source and date; no full name kept; a pattern is only real with 2+ independent sources; nothing from a private support conversation left without a consent flag. Read point: not a metric read. Freshness re-checked at the next monthly cycle; a pattern older than one quarter is re-verified before reuse.. Duration: agent time 2-4 hours across sources; add 1-2 days wait if a support desk or GA4 export has to be requested..

### BRAND-W02 Design and run a customer interview program

Trigger: on demand | event: a research packet has a gap no passive source can fill. Human role: what a founder or researcher does to hear the job in the customer's own words. Capabilities: BRAND-C05. Needs: research packet from BRAND-W01, customer list or panel access, founder availability for the incentive decision.

1. **Name the open question.** State exactly what the interview must learn that reviews, tickets and social could not (a trigger moment, a fired alternative, a hidden social dimension). Skill: `none`. Output: one-line research question. Check: the question cannot already be answered from BRAND-W01 output.
2. **Write the interview guide.** Draft 8-12 open questions in job-to-be-done order: the trigger, the struggle, the alternatives tried, the moment of choosing, the after-state. No leading or feature-fishing questions. Skill: `brand/research-customers/interviews`. Output: interview guide. Check: no question can be answered yes/no; no question names the brand's own feature first.
3. **Pick channel and incentive.** Recommend a channel (call, WhatsApp voice note thread, video) and a proportionate incentive (a discount code or small gift, not cash that could bias answers) sized to the brand's own economics. Skill: `none`. Output: channel and incentive recommendation. Check: incentive cost is computed against contribution margin, not guessed. Ask the founder if: the incentive plan needs a founder-approved budget line.
4. **Recruit the sample.** Pull 8-12 candidates across at least 2 purchase recency bands (recent buyer, lapsed buyer) from the customer list. Skill: `none`. Output: recruit list with recency tag. Check: no single cohort is more than 70% of the sample.
5. **Hand off or run the conversations.** If the founder or team runs the calls, hand off the guide and a note-taking template. If a person the agent can't be, stop here and mark this a human_assist step. Skill: `none`. Output: scheduled interviews or handoff note. Check: every scheduled interview has a guide attached. Ask the founder if: no one is available to run the conversations this cycle.
6. **Log transcripts.** File each transcript or note set with date, respondent recency band, and consent status. Skill: `none`. Output: transcript log. Check: every transcript has a consent flag before any quote is reused.
7. **Extract patterns.** Pull job statement material, forces (push, pull, anxiety, habit) and fired alternatives from the transcripts. Cross-check against BRAND-W01 patterns. Skill: `brand/research-customers/interviews`. Output: interview findings packet. Check: every finding cites which transcript it came from.
8. **Route to avatar work.** Hand the findings packet to BRAND-W05 (avatar record) with a note on what changed from the passive-data picture. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, findings routed. Check: the note names any avatar assumption the interviews overturned.

Outputs: interview guide, transcript log, interview findings packet. QA: every question is open, not leading; every transcript has a consent flag; incentive cost computed, not guessed. Read point: not a metric read; a qualitative evidence build feeding the next avatar cycle.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours to prepare and synthesize; 1-3 weeks wait for scheduling and running 8-12 conversations..

### BRAND-W03 Design and field a customer survey

Trigger: quarterly | on demand to confirm a research finding at scale. Human role: what a researcher does to check a finding against the wider customer base, never to discover a new job. Capabilities: BRAND-C06. Needs: a specific finding from BRAND-W01 or BRAND-W02 to confirm, connector: email/SMS platform (draft only), customer list segmented by recency or tier.

1. **State the confirm-or-deny question.** Write the one claim the survey exists to test (for example, 'gifting is the #1 purchase occasion for tier 2 buyers'). Reject any survey brief that instead asks 'what do you want.' Skill: `none`. Output: survey hypothesis line. Check: the hypothesis already has qualitative evidence behind it from W01/W02.
2. **Pick the survey type.** Choose identity, post-purchase, or NPS/relationship format to match the question. Keep it under 6 questions. Skill: `brand/research-customers/surveys`. Output: survey type and question set. Check: 6 questions or fewer; no double-barreled question.
3. **Draft the send, do not send it.** Draft the survey invite in the email/SMS platform (connector is read + draft only, no send permission). Skill: `none`. Output: drafted survey send. Check: draft only; no send action taken. Ask the founder if: always, before send: this connector cannot send without approval.
4. **Founder approves and sends.** Founder reviews the draft and either sends it themselves or approves the send. Skill: `none`. Output: founder decision. Check: send is logged as founder-approved, not agent-executed.
5. **Collect and clean responses.** Pull responses once the window closes. Drop straight-line and under-10-second responses. Skill: `none`. Output: cleaned response set. Check: junk-response rate reported, not hidden.
6. **Score against the hypothesis.** Compute the confirm/deny rate in code against the original hypothesis line. Skill: `none`. Output: confirm/deny result with sample size. Check: result reported with n, never as a vague 'most customers'.
7. **Route the result.** File the result to the brand brain and the workflow that raised the original finding. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: result changes or confirms a specific record, named explicitly.

Outputs: survey question set, cleaned response set, confirm/deny result. QA: 6 questions or fewer; survey confirms an existing finding, never fishes for a new one; send only after founder approval. Read point: scored the day the response window closes, with sample size reported.. Duration: agent time 2-3 hours to design and score; 1-2 weeks wait for the response window..

### BRAND-W04 Research competitors and public benchmarks

Trigger: scheduled monthly | on demand before a positioning or naming cycle. Human role: what a competitive-intelligence analyst does before any positioning claim is written. Capabilities: BRAND-C07. Needs: named competitor set (direct and adjacent), brand brain: category and price tier, budget ledger.

1. **Confirm the competitive set.** List direct rivals plus real alternatives a shopper would compare against, including doing nothing and the generic marketplace listing (Amazon/Flipkart/Meesho equivalents). Skill: `none`. Output: competitive set list. Check: list includes at least one non-obvious adjacent alternative.
2. **Pull public ad signal.** Search the Meta Ad Library for each competitor's live ads: formats, hooks, offer structure. Public library only, never the brand's own ad account data. Skill: `none`. Output: competitor ad signal notes with dates. Check: every note is labeled 'signal, not proof' per the competitor-material rule.
3. **Pull public page, review and Q&A signal.** Read each competitor's PDP, About page, and public reviews/Q&A for claims made, price anchors, and unmet complaints. Skill: `none`. Output: competitor page and review notes. Check: every claim quoted carries a URL and date.
4. **Check budget before any paid lookup.** If any step needs a paid API or tool call, check the ledger first. Skill: `none`. Output: budget clearance. Check: no paid call proceeds without a logged check. Ask the founder if: the pull would exceed the working stop.
5. **Map the mainstream avatar per competitor.** Infer who each competitor is obviously talking to, from their imagery, copy and price point. Skill: `shared/web-research-budgeted`. Output: per-competitor avatar read. Check: inference is labeled inference, not fact.
6. **Check market health signals.** Note category growth or decline signals: new entrants, price compression, review-volume trend. Skill: `none`. Output: market health note. Check: every signal dated within the last 12 months or flagged stale.
7. **File the competitor packet.** Package ad signal, page signal, avatar reads and market health into one dated packet for positioning and naming work. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: competitor research packet. Check: packet carries no competitor text, logo, product photo or face copied in.

Outputs: competitor ad signal notes, competitor page and review notes, per-competitor avatar read, market health note, competitor research packet. QA: every source has a URL and date; nothing copied, only described; labeled signal not proof throughout. Read point: refreshed monthly; a packet older than 60 days is re-pulled before it feeds a positioning statement.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours for a 5-8 competitor set..

### BRAND-W05 Build and gate a new avatar record

Trigger: event: a new avatar is needed, or the quarterly recheck finds the current one stale. Human role: what a brand strategist does to turn scattered research into one usable customer record. Capabilities: BRAND-C09, BRAND-C13, BRAND-C11. Needs: research packet from BRAND-W01 (and W02/W03 if run), competitor packet from BRAND-W04 if positioning-relevant, founder sign-off on which candidate to build.

1. **Draft the job-to-be-done statement.** Write the job in a situation: 'when [situation], I want to [motivation], so I can [outcome].' Cover functional, emotional and social sides. Skill: `brand/jtbd-writer`. Output: draft job statement. Check: passes the verb-noun test and the cross-category test (the job exists outside this exact product too).
2. **Name the 4 forces.** Identify the push (problem), pull (attraction to a solution), anxiety (what could go wrong), and habit (inertia keeping them with the status quo). Skill: `brand/jtbd-writer`. Output: 4-forces record. Check: each force has at least one verbatim quote.
3. **Name the fired alternative.** State what the customer was doing, or considering, before this product (a competitor, a DIY fix, or doing nothing). Skill: `none`. Output: fired-alternative line. Check: traces to a specific quote or competitor data point, not assumed.
4. **Build the objection map.** Pair every named objection with the real thing that removed it for a real customer (a review, a support reply, a product change). Where relevant, turn a pattern into a fit or size-confidence angle and a support macro draft. Skill: `brand/objection-map-builder`. Output: objection-remover pairs, macro drafts. Check: every objection has 2+ quoted sources; every remover is something that actually happened, not a hoped-for fix.
5. **Build the 5W profile and trigger moment.** Who, what, when, where, why, plus the specific moment that starts the buying process (a wedding date set, a monsoon starting, a festival approaching). Skill: `brand/avatar-record-builder`. Output: 5W profile, trigger moment. Check: trigger moment is a specific event, not a vague season.
6. **Assemble the full record.** Pull job, forces, fired alternative, objection map, 5W, trigger and quote set into one avatar record. Skill: `brand/avatar-record-builder`. Output: assembled avatar record. Check: every required field is present or explicitly marked missing.
7. **Set the status flag.** Mark the record 'confirmed' only if built from 2+ independent source types with at least one first-party source (for an existing brand). Otherwise mark 'provisional.' Skill: `brand/avatar-record-builder`. Output: status-flagged record. Check: status flag matches the actual source count, not optimism.
8. **Founder gate.** Present the record with its sources for founder approval before it becomes the record other specialists cite. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows sources and the provisional/confirmed flag plainly. Ask the founder if: always: this is a founder_gate capability.
9. **Write to the registry and brand brain.** Log the decision and, if approved, write the avatar record into the brand brain customer section. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, brand brain update. Check: a rejected record is logged with the founder's reason, not silently dropped.

Outputs: job statement, objection map, 5W profile, full avatar record, registry entry. QA: every field traces to a quote or is marked missing; status flag matches real source count; founder decision logged either way. Read point: revisited every quarter (BRAND-W07) or when a brief citing it stops converting.. Duration: agent time 4-6 hours once research is in hand; founder review adds 1-3 days..

### BRAND-W06 Discover, rank and prioritize avatar candidates

Trigger: scheduled quarterly. Human role: what a strategist does before picking which customer to build the next cycle's work around. Capabilities: BRAND-C10. Needs: all avatar candidates surfaced this quarter (from W01, W02, W04, W08), current primary avatar record, metric contract: revenue and repeat-rate by known segment where trackable.

1. **List every candidate.** Pull every avatar candidate surfaced across this quarter's research, including sub-avatars flagged in social listening. Skill: `brand/avatar-ranking`. Output: candidate list. Check: every candidate names which run surfaced it.
2. **Require a cross-source pattern.** Drop any candidate that appears in only one source type. Skill: `brand/avatar-ranking`. Output: filtered candidate list. Check: remaining candidates each cite 2+ source types.
3. **Score reach and fit.** Estimate each candidate's addressable size against the brand's own customer and traffic data, and its fit to current product and margin. Skill: `shared/rank-candidates`. Output: scored candidate list. Check: scores computed in code from real fields, not estimated in prose.
4. **Rank and pick the primary.** Rank by reach x fit x evidence strength. Recommend a primary avatar for the cycle and note runner-ups. Skill: `brand/avatar-ranking`. Output: ranked list, recommended primary. Check: the current primary is not kept by default without being re-scored.
5. **Founder gate.** Present the ranked list and recommendation for the founder to confirm, override, or ask for another cycle of evidence. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows why each runner-up ranked below the pick. Ask the founder if: always.
6. **Route the pick.** If the primary changed, trigger BRAND-W05 to build or refresh that avatar's full record. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, routed follow-up. Check: a changed primary always triggers a fresh record build, never reuses an old provisional one.

Outputs: ranked candidate list, founder decision, registry entry. QA: every candidate has 2+ source types; scores computed in code; decision logged either way. Read point: quarterly, alongside the general avatar audit (BRAND-W07).. Duration: agent time 2-3 hours; founder review 1-3 days..

### BRAND-W07 Segment and maintain avatars on a cadence

Trigger: quarterly | event: new price tier, channel or subscription line. Human role: what a strategist does to keep the customer record honest and correctly split over time. Capabilities: BRAND-C12, BRAND-C15. Needs: current avatar record(s), brand brain: pricing tiers, channels, B2B or subscription lines, briefs written since the last audit, to check citation.

1. **Check for a split trigger.** Check whether a new price tier, buyer-versus-user split (for example, a gifting buyer versus the person who wears the piece), B2B line, or subscription churn pattern has changed the underlying job. Skill: `brand/avatar-segmentation`. Output: split-needed flag. Check: a split is only proposed when the job itself differs, not just the SKU.
2. **Split the record if warranted.** Create a separate avatar record for the new segment, reusing shared quotes but writing a distinct job statement and objection map where they differ. Skill: `brand/avatar-segmentation`. Output: new segment record(s). Check: each split record stands on its own evidence, not copy-pasted from the parent.
3. **Audit citation.** Check which briefs and pages have cited the current avatar record since the last audit, and whether product still earns the win described in it. Skill: `brand/avatar-record-builder`. Output: citation and fit audit note. Check: note names any brief that cited a stale or wrong field.
4. **Re-check the dominant avatar.** Re-run the source-count and freshness check on the primary avatar; flag if evidence is older than 2 quarters. Skill: `brand/avatar-record-builder`. Output: freshness verdict. Check: verdict is date-stamped, not assumed current.
5. **Refresh what's stale.** If evidence is stale, route back to BRAND-W01 for a fresh research pass before editing the record. Skill: `none`. Output: refresh trigger or 'still current' note. Check: never edits a record from memory without new evidence.
6. **Founder gate on any material change.** Any split, merge or material rewrite of a live avatar record goes to the founder before it replaces what specialists are citing. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: a live record is never silently overwritten. Ask the founder if: always for a material change.
7. **Log and publish.** Write the approved version to the brand brain and note the audit date for the next cycle. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: updated brand brain, registry entry. Check: prior version is kept in history, not deleted.

Outputs: segment records, citation and fit audit note, freshness verdict, registry entry. QA: a split needs a real job difference, not just a SKU difference; no record edited without new evidence; material change always founder-gated. Read point: quarterly, or immediately on a triggering event (new tier, channel, subscription line).. Duration: agent time 2-4 hours; founder review 1-3 days if a change is proposed..

### BRAND-W08 Run the untapped avatar discovery pipeline

Trigger: on demand, when the category is saturated and the product has no real point of difference left to claim. Human role: what a strategist does to find a customer competitors have not claimed, instead of fighting for the same one. Capabilities: BRAND-C16. Needs: competitor packet (BRAND-W04) covering who every rival obviously targets, current avatar record, brand brain: product capabilities.

1. **Map who is already claimed.** List the avatar every visible competitor is obviously serving, from BRAND-W04's per-competitor avatar reads. Skill: `brand/untapped-avatar-pipeline`. Output: claimed-avatar map. Check: map covers every competitor in the set, not just the top 2.
2. **List adjacent jobs the product could also do.** From the product's real capabilities, list jobs it could serve beyond the claimed avatar's job (a gifting job, a professional-use job, a different life stage). Skill: `brand/untapped-avatar-pipeline`. Output: adjacent job candidate list. Check: every candidate job is something the product can actually do today, not a roadmap wish.
3. **Search for evidence the adjacent job is real.** Search reviews, forums and search terms (own and public) for signs someone already uses or wants the product for that adjacent job. Skill: `brand/untapped-avatar-pipeline`. Output: evidence-checked candidate list. Check: a candidate with zero found evidence is dropped, not carried forward on hope.
4. **Size the candidate.** Run a lightweight market-sizing pass on the strongest 1-2 candidates. Skill: `brand/market-sizing`. Output: sizing note per candidate. Check: sizing uses the brand's own AOV and margin, not an assumed one.
5. **Founder gate.** Present the untapped-avatar candidates with evidence and sizing for a founder decision on whether to pursue. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: recommendation names the evidence and the size, not just the idea. Ask the founder if: always.
6. **Route to avatar build.** If approved, hand off to BRAND-W05 to build the full avatar record for the new candidate. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, routed follow-up. Check: a rejected candidate is logged with the reason so it is not re-proposed unchanged.

Outputs: claimed-avatar map, adjacent job candidate list, evidence-checked candidate list, sizing note. QA: every candidate checked against real evidence; sizing done in code from the brand's own numbers. Read point: not a metric read; re-run whenever the category feels saturated again.. Duration: agent time 4-6 hours..

### BRAND-W09 Map the category ladder and the XY positioning map

Trigger: event: a new positioning cycle starts, or the competitive set materially changes. Human role: what a strategist does before any positioning claim gets written. Capabilities: BRAND-C18, BRAND-C19. Needs: competitor packet (BRAND-W04), brand brain: category, price tier, product facts.

1. **List every rung of the category ladder.** List the category itself, the sub-category, every real product alternative, and doing nothing, as rungs a shopper could pick instead of this brand. Skill: `brand/category-ladder-and-xy-map`. Output: category ladder. Check: ladder includes 'doing nothing' as a rung.
2. **List the real competitive set.** List every brand or option a shopper would actually compare against, not only the obvious direct rivals. Skill: `brand/category-ladder-and-xy-map`. Output: competitive set list. Check: list includes at least one indirect or adjacent-category alternative.
3. **Choose the two axes.** Pick two axes that matter to the avatar's actual decision (for example, price versus trust-signal, or craft versus convenience), not two axes that flatter this brand. Skill: `brand/category-ladder-and-xy-map`. Output: axis choice with reasoning. Check: axes are drawn from what the avatar record says they weigh, not picked to make an empty corner appear.
4. **Plot every alternative.** Plot the brand and every real alternative from the competitive set on the two axes, using facts from the competitor packet. Skill: `brand/category-ladder-and-xy-map`. Output: XY positioning map. Check: every plotted point cites the fact that placed it there.
5. **Identify open corners.** Mark which corners of the map have no occupant, and whether that emptiness means opportunity or means no one wants that combination. Skill: `brand/category-ladder-and-xy-map`. Output: open-corner note. Check: an empty corner is checked against demand evidence before being called an opportunity.
6. **File for positioning work.** Hand the ladder and map to BRAND-W10 for the positioning statement. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: nothing is claimed as positioning yet; this step only maps the field.

Outputs: category ladder, competitive set list, XY positioning map, open-corner note. QA: every plotted point cites a fact; axes reflect what the avatar actually weighs; doing nothing is on the ladder. Read point: refreshed whenever the competitive set changes materially, at minimum once a year.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours..

### BRAND-W10 Draft, audit and test the positioning statement

Trigger: event: new or refreshed positioning, following BRAND-W09. Human role: what a strategist does to turn a mapped field into one line a shopper reads and believes. Capabilities: BRAND-C20, BRAND-C21. Needs: category ladder and XY map (BRAND-W09), avatar record, voice sheet if one exists, claims tiers (approved / site-backed / forbidden).

1. **Answer the 6 positioning questions in writing.** Write out: for whom, for what category, against what alternative, what benefit, why believe it, why now. No skipping to the tagline first. Skill: `brand/positioning-statement`. Output: written answers to the 6 questions. Check: every answer traces to the avatar record or a verified fact, not an assertion.
2. **Draft the 3-sentence promise.** Write the promise in 3 sentences: the change, the mechanism, the proof. Skill: `brand/positioning-statement`. Output: 3-sentence promise. Check: every claim in it passes shared/claim-check against the claims tiers.
3. **Cut to the one line.** Cut the promise to the single line a scrolling shopper would actually read and remember. Skill: `brand/positioning-statement`. Output: differentiation line. Check: line passes the strip test: it fails if a competitor's name could replace this brand's and it would still read true.
4. **Run the positioning checklist.** Check the statement against the fixed checklist (clear, ownable, provable, durable, not a disputed stat). Skill: `brand/positioning-statement`. Output: checklist pass/fail with notes. Check: a fail on any item blocks moving forward until fixed.
5. **If no open slot exists, search for a creneau or reframe.** When the XY map shows every corner already occupied, search for an unclaimed dimension, take the deliberate against-position, or reframe the category around a shared frustration the avatar already voices. Skill: `brand/positioning-statement`. Output: creneau or reframe candidate. Check: the reframe is grounded in a frustration the avatar record already documents, not invented.
6. **Test 2 frames against each other.** Where two credible framings exist, design a small test (message test in an ad, a landing headline split, or an AI-persona pretest) to see which the avatar responds to before committing. Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: test design and, once run, a result. Check: test has a stated read point before it starts.
7. **Founder gate.** Present the statement, promise, checklist result and any test result to the founder. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows the checklist result plainly, pass or fail. Ask the founder if: always.
8. **Publish.** Write the approved positioning statement to the brand brain identity section. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: brand brain update, registry entry. Check: old positioning is versioned, not deleted.

Outputs: 3-sentence promise, differentiation line, checklist result, positioning statement. QA: every claim passes claim-check; strip test passed; checklist failures blocked from shipping. Read point: revisited whenever the competitive set or avatar changes materially.. Duration: agent time 4-6 hours; founder review 1-3 days; a message test adds 1-2 weeks if run..

### BRAND-W11 Size the market and audit pricing position

Trigger: event: new niche or product idea | quarterly pricing audit. Human role: what an operator does before believing a niche is worth building, and before a price drifts out of position. Capabilities: BRAND-C23, BRAND-C24. Needs: metric contract: AOV, gross margin, contribution margin, repeat_rate_90d/365d, candidate niche or product idea, if sizing one, competitor packet: category price range.

1. **Compute repeat buyers needed to sustain the business.** From this store's own AOV and margin, compute how many repeat buyers at what frequency would sustain the business or the candidate line. Skill: `brand/market-sizing`. Output: sustaining-buyer-count figure. Check: computed in code from real fields; missing fields show as unavailable, not assumed.
2. **Check parent market and sub-niche size.** Look for an independent size signal for the category and the specific niche (search volume, subreddit size, marketplace listing count, review-count proxy). Skill: `brand/market-sizing`. Output: independent size signal with source. Check: signal has a URL and date; a niche with no findable signal is flagged 'unproven,' not sized anyway.
3. **Place on the price-by-customer-count matrix.** Place the candidate on a matrix of price point against needed customer count, to see if it is a volume play or a margin play. Skill: `brand/market-sizing`. Output: matrix placement. Check: placement stated as a range, not a false-precise single number.
4. **Check current price against the category's learned range.** Compare the brand's live price to the category range found in the competitor packet. Skill: `brand/pricing-audit`. Output: in-range or out-of-range verdict. Check: verdict states which competitors set the range boundaries.
5. **Audit premium signals.** Check whether packaging, page copy, and current discount cadence still support a premium story, if one is claimed. Skill: `brand/pricing-audit`. Output: premium-signal audit note. Check: a high discount_rate next to a premium claim is flagged explicitly, not smoothed over.
6. **Founder gate.** Present sizing and pricing findings together; both are founder_gate decisions. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card separates the sizing question from the pricing question clearly. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Log the result.** Write the decision and figures to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: figures kept alongside the decision for the next quarter's comparison.

Outputs: sustaining-buyer-count figure, market sizing memo, pricing position verdict, premium-signal audit note. QA: all arithmetic in code; missing inputs marked unavailable, never assumed; a sizing claim always cites its independent signal. Read point: market sizing at idea time; pricing audit every quarter.. Duration: agent time 2-3 hours..

### BRAND-W12 Run a naming project, brand or product

Trigger: on demand | event: new SKU or collection needs a name. Human role: what a naming consultant does from a blank page to a cleared, presentable name. Capabilities: BRAND-C25, BRAND-C26, BRAND-C27, BRAND-C28, BRAND-C29. Needs: brand brain: category, product facts, target avatar, target markets and languages for clearance, scope: full brand name or a lighter product/SKU/collection name.

1. **Set the scope.** Decide whether this is a full brand-naming project or a lighter product, SKU or collection name screen. Default to a descriptive, benefit-first title for search where a brand name is not needed. Skill: `brand/name-screening-and-packaging`. Output: scope decision. Check: scope decision stated before generation starts, so effort matches the stakes.
2. **Run the category, product and buyer audit.** Review the category's naming conventions, the product's real attributes, and the avatar's language, so generated names are grounded, not generic. Skill: `brand/naming-candidate-generation`. Output: naming brief. Check: brief lists what the name must NOT do (no forbidden claim, no confusable-with-competitor sound).
3. **Generate far more candidates than feels necessary.** Generate candidates across all naming types (descriptive, suggestive, invented, founder, evocative) using AI for volume, human judgment applied at review, not at generation. Skill: `brand/naming-candidate-generation`. Output: raw candidate list (50-100+). Check: list spans multiple naming types, not one flavor repeated.
4. **Screen on the 7 core qualities.** Score every candidate: distinctive, meaningful, memorable, brief, positive, extendable, protectable. Test sound and syllable count out loud. Avoid acronym and generic-name traps. Skill: `brand/name-screening-and-packaging`. Output: scored shortlist. Check: each finalist has all 7 scores shown, not a single overall number.
5. **Clear finalists for trademark, domain and language risk.** Search the trademark database and international equivalents, check name + industry-term collisions, check domain and social handle availability, and check meaning and sound in relevant languages (Hindi, and any export-market language). Skill: `brand/trademark-domain-check`. Output: clearance report per finalist. Check: every finalist has a status: clear, risk, or needs-legal. Ask the founder if: a finalist needs a paid legal opinion or filing.
6. **Mock up the finalists.** Present the top 2-3 cleared names in realistic mock-ups (packaging, PDP title, a spoken-aloud test) rather than as a bare word list. Skill: `brand/name-screening-and-packaging`. Output: finalist mock-ups. Check: mock-ups use the real product photo, not an invented render, where accuracy matters.
7. **Write the short story and tagline (full brand names only).** For a full brand-naming project, write nomenclature notes, a short brand story, and a tagline that passes the strip test. Skill: `brand/name-screening-and-packaging`. Output: naming deliverable. Check: tagline fails the strip test if a competitor's name could swap in and it would still read true.
8. **Founder gate.** Present the finalized name package for founder decision. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows clearance status plainly next to the name. Ask the founder if: always.
9. **Publish.** Log the decision; if approved, write the name into the brand brain or product catalogue as applicable. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, brand brain or catalogue update. Check: a rejected name is logged with the reason, not silently reused later.

Outputs: raw candidate list, scored shortlist, clearance report, naming deliverable or SKU name. QA: candidates span multiple naming types; every finalist has a real clearance status; tagline passes the strip test. Read point: not a metric read; naming is a one-time deliverable per project.. Duration: agent time 1-2 days for a full brand name; 1-2 hours for a SKU/collection name; legal clearance can add 1-2 weeks if a paid opinion is needed..

### BRAND-W13 Decide brand architecture for a new line or market

Trigger: event: a new product line, category extension or market entry is proposed. Human role: what a brand architect decides before a new line ships under a name. Capabilities: BRAND-C30. Needs: proposed new line or market, current brand equity and guidelines, avatar overlap between current brand and the new line's likely buyer.

1. **Run the teeter-totter test.** Check whether the new line would pull equity away from the parent brand's core meaning if given the master brand name (does adding this make the master brand mean less, not more). Skill: `none`. Output: teeter-totter verdict. Check: verdict is written as a specific mechanism, not a feeling.
2. **Run the shopping-list test.** Check whether a shopper would naturally put the new line on the same mental shopping list as the current brand, or whether it reads as a different errand entirely. Skill: `none`. Output: shopping-list verdict. Check: verdict names the specific shopper behavior it is based on.
3. **Check avatar overlap.** Compare the new line's likely buyer against the current primary avatar for real overlap, not assumed overlap. Skill: `none`. Output: overlap read. Check: overlap stated as high, partial or low, with the evidence.
4. **Default to one master brand for a small brand.** Unless a real reason surfaces in steps 1-3 (equity dilution risk, no shopping-list overlap, real reputational separation needed), recommend the master-brand name over a new name, since a small brand cannot afford to build two names. Skill: `none`. Output: architecture recommendation (master brand vs new name). Check: a 'new name' recommendation must name the specific reason the default was overridden.
5. **Route naming if a new name is warranted.** If a new name is recommended, hand off to BRAND-W12 for the naming project. Skill: `none`. Output: routing note. Check: routing happens only after the architecture decision, never before it.
6. **Founder gate.** Present the recommendation and reasoning to the founder. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card states the default and why it was or was not overridden. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Log the decision.** Write the decision and reasoning to the registry and brand brain. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, brand brain update. Check: reasoning is kept, not just the yes/no outcome.

Outputs: teeter-totter verdict, shopping-list verdict, architecture recommendation, registry entry. QA: default to one master brand unless a named reason overrides it; every verdict is a specific mechanism, not a feeling. Read point: not a metric read; a one-time architecture decision per new line or market.. Duration: agent time 2-3 hours; founder review 1-3 days..

### BRAND-W14 Draft the brand story, voice sheet and promise

Trigger: on demand | event: new brand, voice drift found, or a new promise/tagline is needed. Human role: what a brand writer does to give every future writer and model a stable voice to sound like. Capabilities: BRAND-C31, BRAND-C32, BRAND-C34. Needs: founder's vision and values, in their own words, real customer understanding (avatar record), Kevin's liked and disliked copy examples.

1. **Draft the founder or brand story.** Write the story in a self, us, now structure: who the founder is, what the shared struggle with the customer is, why this matters right now. Keep it stable for years, not campaign-specific. Skill: `brand/brand-story-and-promise`. Output: draft brand story. Check: story contains no invented biographical fact; founder confirms every detail.
2. **Set a default voice if none exists.** If no voice sheet exists yet, set a plain, direct default voice (short sentences, no jargon, digits not words) so copy work is not blocked while the real sheet is built. Skill: `brand/voice-sheet-builder`. Output: default voice note. Check: clearly marked 'default, not final'.
3. **Collect liked and disliked examples.** Gather Kevin's liked and disliked copy examples from past work, real or reference. Skill: `brand/voice-sheet-builder`. Output: example set. Check: every example is real, sourced copy, not invented for the exercise.
4. **Derive 4-8 voice traits.** From the examples, derive 4-8 traits, each with a liked example, a disliked example, and a one-line rule. Skill: `brand/voice-sheet-builder`. Output: voice and tone sheet (short + full versions). Check: every trait has both a real liked and a real disliked example.
5. **Draft the 3-sentence promise and tagline candidates.** Write the promise (change, mechanism, proof) and 3-5 tagline candidates. Skill: `brand/brand-story-and-promise`. Output: promise, tagline candidates. Check: every claim in the promise passes shared/claim-check.
6. **Run the strip test.** Cut candidates that would still read true with a competitor's name swapped in. Skill: `brand/brand-story-and-promise`. Output: surviving tagline candidates. Check: at least one candidate is ownable and specific to this brand.
7. **Design a recall test, if a signature line is being chosen.** For a candidate meant to become a signature line, design a small recall-based test (does the shopper remember it after seeing an ad or page once). Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: test design and, once run, a recall result. Check: test has a stated read point before it runs.
8. **Founder gate.** Present story, voice sheet and promise/tagline together for founder approval. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card separates the three deliverables so each can be approved or sent back independently. Ask the founder if: always.
9. **Publish.** Write approved pieces to the brand brain identity section. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: brand brain update, registry entry. Check: a default voice is replaced, not left stacked alongside the real one.

Outputs: brand story, voice and tone sheet, promise, tagline. QA: every voice trait has real liked/disliked examples; every promise claim passes claim-check; tagline passes the strip test. Read point: voice sheet re-checked whenever drift is found in the consistency audit (BRAND-W17).. Duration: agent time 4-8 hours; founder review 2-4 days..

### BRAND-W15 Build a messaging hierarchy for a page or channel

Trigger: event: new campaign, page, or channel needs a message stack. Human role: what a messaging strategist writes so every asset on a page or channel leads with the same one idea. Capabilities: BRAND-C33. Needs: positioning statement, avatar record and objection map, the specific page or channel type (homepage, PDP, email, ads).

1. **Name the one big idea.** State the single idea this page or channel must lead with, drawn from the positioning statement, not invented fresh. Skill: `brand/messaging-hierarchy-builder`. Output: big idea line. Check: big idea traces directly to the approved positioning statement.
2. **Rank up to 3 supporting messages.** Pull the strongest supporting messages from the objection map and avatar record, ranked by how much friction each removes. Skill: `brand/messaging-hierarchy-builder`. Output: ranked supporting messages. Check: each supporting message maps to a specific objection or force.
3. **Fit the stack to the page type.** Adjust order and depth for the page or channel: homepage leads broad, PDP leads with the objection most likely at that SKU's price point, email leads with the relationship stage, ads lead with the hook. Skill: `brand/messaging-hierarchy-builder`. Output: page-fitted message stack. Check: stack respects the writing rules: short sentences, digits, no em dashes.
4. **Claim-check the stack.** Run every message through claim-check against the claims tiers. Skill: `shared/claim-check`. Output: pass/fail per message with fixes. Check: no message ships with an untraced or forbidden claim.
5. **Hand off to the executing specialist.** Route the finished hierarchy to the Site specialist (page) or Paid Creative/Copy specialist (ad, email) to execute; this agent never writes the final page or ad itself. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff packet. Check: packet is a message stack, not a finished draft, respecting the ownership boundary.
6. **Log the hierarchy.** File the approved hierarchy to the registry so future briefs for this page/channel reuse it instead of drifting. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: hierarchy is retrievable by page/channel type for reuse.

Outputs: big idea line, ranked supporting messages, page-fitted message stack. QA: big idea traces to positioning; every message passes claim-check; stack respects the writing rules. Read point: not a metric read; re-run when positioning or the avatar changes, or a page underperforms.. Duration: agent time 1-2 hours per page/channel..

### BRAND-W16 Brief visual identity and assemble the brand guidelines document

Trigger: event: new brand or a major identity refresh. Human role: what a brand designer's creative director briefs before a designer ever opens a file. Capabilities: BRAND-C35, BRAND-C36. Needs: positioning statement, voice and tone sheet, avatar record (aesthetic preferences, price-tier expectations).

1. **Write the mood and reference direction.** Describe the intended mood, palette, typography and photography direction in words and referenced mood only. This agent never generates the final visual assets. Skill: `brand/identity-brief-builder`. Output: identity direction brief. Check: brief is words and mood references, no invented final logo or asset.
2. **Hand the reference pull to the Reference Scout.** Route the mood brief to the Reference Scout for real, mood-only visual references, never competitor logos or product photos to copy. Skill: `none`. Output: reference set. Check: references are for layout and mood only, never copied.
3. **Draft logo and mark rules.** Write the rules for logo clear space, minimum size, and misuse examples, once a mark exists. Skill: `brand/brand-guidelines-builder`. Output: logo and mark rules section. Check: rules include at least 3 concrete misuse examples.
4. **Assemble visual language section.** Package palette, typography and photography direction into the visual language section. Skill: `brand/brand-guidelines-builder`. Output: visual language section. Check: every color and font choice ties back to the mood brief's reasoning.
5. **Assemble language rules section.** Fold in the voice and tone sheet as the language rules section. Skill: `brand/brand-guidelines-builder`. Output: language rules section. Check: section is the actual voice sheet, not a rewritten summary of it.
6. **Build the expressions section.** Show 3-5 applied examples (a PDP header, an email, an ad) demonstrating the rules together. Skill: `brand/brand-guidelines-builder`. Output: expressions section. Check: every applied example passes claim-check and the writing rules.
7. **Run the completeness checklist.** Check the 4 parts (mark rules, visual language, language rules, expressions) are all present before calling the document done. Skill: `brand/brand-guidelines-builder`. Output: completeness checklist result. Check: a missing part blocks publishing, it does not ship as 'to be added later'.
8. **Founder gate.** Present the assembled guidelines document for founder approval. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows the checklist result plainly. Ask the founder if: always.
9. **Publish.** Write the approved guidelines to the brand brain and file the document. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: brand brain update, registry entry. Check: prior guidelines version is kept in history.

Outputs: identity direction brief, reference set, brand guidelines document. QA: mood only, nothing copied; all 4 parts present before publishing; every applied example passes claim-check. Read point: revisited at a major refresh; drift is caught monthly by BRAND-W17 in between.. Duration: agent time 1-2 days; Reference Scout turnaround adds hours; founder review 2-5 days..

### BRAND-W17 Audit brand consistency and diagnose the perception gap

Trigger: scheduled monthly (consistency) | quarterly, run together with the perception-gap pass. Human role: what a brand manager does walking the live brand against its own rulebook, then checking the rulebook against reality. Capabilities: BRAND-C37, BRAND-C59. Needs: brand guidelines document, voice and tone sheet, live site, ad and email surfaces, recent reviews, social mentions and survey results.

1. **Walk the site against guidelines.** Crawl key site pages (home, PDP, checkout) and check against logo rules, visual language and voice. Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: site drift list. Check: every drift item cites the specific rule it breaks.
2. **Walk live ads and email against guidelines.** Check current live ad creative and recent email/SMS sends against the same rules. Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: ads/email drift list. Check: read-only check; no ad account write attempted.
3. **Score drift severity.** Rank each drift item by how visible and how far from the rule it is. Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: ranked drift list. Check: ranking is computed from visibility and distance, not gut feel.
4. **Compare stated identity to what customers actually say.** Compare the positioning statement, voice sheet and claims against what reviews, social mentions and survey results actually say about the brand. Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: perception-gap note. Check: gap note quotes real customer language, not a paraphrase.
5. **Name the gap type.** Classify each gap as the brand under-claiming (customers already believe more than the brand says) or over-claiming (customers do not believe what the brand says). Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: classified gap list. Check: an over-claim gap is flagged as higher priority than an under-claim.
6. **Draft fixes.** For each drift or gap item, draft a specific fix (a page change, an ad note, a wording change) sized to what can actually be done. Skill: `brand/brand-consistency-audit`. Output: fix list. Check: every fix names the owning specialist (Site, Media, Creative) for execution.
7. **Founder gate on any identity-level fix.** A fix that changes the voice sheet, positioning or guidelines themselves needs founder approval; a fix that is just correcting a live asset to match existing rules does not. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision where needed. Check: only identity-level fixes are gated; routine corrections are not held up. Ask the founder if: a fix would change the guidelines or positioning themselves.
8. **Route and log.** Send routine fixes directly to the owning specialist; log identity-level fixes to the registry with the founder's decision. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, routed fixes. Check: every fix is tracked to a closing status next month, not dropped.

Outputs: site drift list, ads/email drift list, perception-gap note, fix list. QA: every drift cites the specific rule broken; gap note quotes real customer language; over-claim gaps prioritized over under-claim. Read point: monthly for drift; quarterly for the perception-gap comparison.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours..

### BRAND-W18 Track brand health signals

Trigger: scheduled monthly. Human role: what a brand manager tracks separately from any single campaign's performance. Capabilities: BRAND-C48. Needs: connector: Google Search Console (branded search volume), review rating and count over time, social mention data, where collectable.

1. **Pull branded search volume.** Pull branded search query volume and trend from Search Console. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: branded search trend. Check: trend covers a rolling 12 months where available; missing months marked unavailable.
2. **Pull review rating and count trend.** Track review_rating and review_count over time from the metric contract. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: review trend line. Check: computed in code from the contract, not estimated.
3. **Pull mention and sentiment signal, where available.** Check for any collectable public mention or sentiment data; if no source exists yet, mark this field unavailable rather than guessing a sentiment score. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: mention/sentiment note or 'unavailable' flag. Check: never fabricates a sentiment score with no real source behind it.
4. **Track repeat behavior trend.** Pull repeat_rate_90d and repeat_rate_365d trend from the metric contract as a brand-strength proxy, separate from any one campaign. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: repeat behavior trend. Check: kept separate from campaign-level ROAS reporting.
5. **Assemble the brand health line.** Combine branded search, review trend, mention signal and repeat trend into one dated brand health line. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: brand health report. Check: each series shows its own freshness date, not one blended 'as of' date.
6. **Flag material moves.** Flag any series that moved more than a set threshold since last month for founder attention. Skill: `brand/brand-health-tracking`. Output: flagged moves list. Check: threshold is stated, not a subjective 'looks different'.
7. **File the report.** Log the report to the registry and the monthly review. Skill: `shared/report-section`. Output: registry entry. Check: report is retrievable month over month for trend comparison.

Outputs: branded search trend, review trend line, brand health report. QA: missing data marked unavailable, never zero; every series dated; no fabricated sentiment score. Read point: monthly, trend read over a rolling 12 months where data exists.. Duration: agent time 1-2 hours..

### BRAND-W19 Design community and loyalty cohorts for advocacy

Trigger: on demand. Human role: what a community lead designs before inviting anyone into an exclusive group. Capabilities: BRAND-C39. Needs: avatar record, current top advocates (from reviews, repeat purchase, social mentions), brand brain: values and story.

1. **Define who counts as 'us' precisely.** Write the specific, checkable criteria for belonging to the cohort (a purchase count, a referral made, a value shared publicly), not a vague 'our best customers.' Skill: `brand/community-cohort-design`. Output: membership criteria. Check: criteria are checkable against real data, not self-declared.
2. **Identify who already advocates.** Pull customers who already advocate unprompted (unsolicited reviews, repeat referrals, organic social mentions) as the seed group. Skill: `brand/community-cohort-design`. Output: seed advocate list. Check: list is built from real behavior, not assumed loyalty.
3. **Design what the group controls.** Design one real thing the cohort controls or gets first (early access, a say in a product decision, a named recognition), sized to what the brand can actually deliver. Skill: `brand/community-cohort-design`. Output: cohort privilege design. Check: privilege is something the brand can sustain past the launch month.
4. **Draft the invitation and rules.** Draft how members are invited, what is expected of them, and how membership could be lost. Skill: `brand/community-cohort-design`. Output: invitation and rules draft. Check: rules for losing membership are as clear as rules for gaining it.
5. **Founder gate.** Present the cohort design for founder approval before any invitation goes out. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card states the ongoing cost of running this cohort, not just the launch cost. Ask the founder if: always.
6. **Route execution.** Hand approved invitation copy to the email/SMS platform for the founder or Lifecycle specialist to send. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff packet. Check: this agent drafts only; it does not send.
7. **Log for scale-up tracking.** Log the cohort design to the registry so a proven cohort can be handed to the Lifecycle specialist to run at scale. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: handoff point to Lifecycle is named explicitly.

Outputs: membership criteria, seed advocate list, cohort privilege design, invitation and rules draft. QA: membership criteria checkable, not self-declared; losing-membership rules as clear as gaining them. Read point: not a metric read; reviewed once the cohort is live for whether the design is sustainable.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours; founder review 2-4 days..

### BRAND-W20 Operate the ambassador and community advocacy program

Trigger: scheduled weekly, once a program is approved. Human role: what a community manager does every week to run an approved ambassador program. Capabilities: BRAND-C55. Needs: approved ambassador program design (from BRAND-W19 or an earlier program), applicant list, brand guidelines and voice sheet.

1. **Screen new applicants.** Check new applicants against the program's membership criteria. Skill: `brand/ambassador-program-ops`. Output: screened applicant list. Check: screening applies the same criteria to every applicant, no exceptions without a founder note.
2. **Run a reputation check on new ambassadors.** Run a public reputation and controversy check on any new ambassador before onboarding. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: reputation check result. Check: a flagged applicant is routed to founder review before onboarding. Ask the founder if: a reputation flag is found.
3. **Assign tasks.** Assign this week's content or advocacy tasks to active ambassadors, matched to what each one does well. Skill: `brand/ambassador-program-ops`. Output: task assignments. Check: no ambassador is assigned a task requiring a claim outside the approved tiers.
4. **Check submitted content against guidelines.** Review submitted content against the brand guidelines and voice sheet before it is approved for reward or reposting. Skill: `brand/ambassador-program-ops`. Output: approved/rejected content list. Check: rejected content gets a specific, usable reason.
5. **Track rewards owed.** Track what each ambassador is owed (discount, product, payment) against what they delivered. Skill: `brand/ambassador-program-ops`. Output: rewards ledger. Check: ledger computed in code, checked against the program's budget cap.
6. **Flag budget or capacity issues.** Flag if the program is approaching its budget cap or if the team cannot keep up with volume. Skill: `brand/ambassador-program-ops`. Output: capacity flag. Check: flag raised before the cap is hit, not after. Ask the founder if: the program is within 10% of its budget cap.
7. **Weekly log.** Log the week's activity, approvals, rejections and rewards to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: log is retrievable week over week for a program health trend.

Outputs: screened applicant list, task assignments, approved content list, rewards ledger. QA: screening criteria applied consistently; rewards computed in code against a real budget cap; reputation check run on every new ambassador. Read point: weekly operational log; program health reviewed monthly against BRAND-W19's original design.. Duration: agent time 2-4 hours per week..

### BRAND-W21 Develop a PR angle, build the media list and source earned media

Trigger: event: a launch or newsworthy moment | monthly sweep for thought-leadership and awards. Human role: what a PR manager does from a brand fact to a pitched, placed story. Capabilities: BRAND-C40, BRAND-C53, BRAND-C60, BRAND-C61. Needs: brand story and positioning, a specific launch, newsworthy moment, or founder expertise area, target media tier (trade press, mainstream, niche community).

1. **Find the pitchable angle.** Turn the brand story, a category frustration, or an origin fact into a pitchable press angle, not a product announcement. Skill: `brand/pr-angle-builder`. Output: pitch angle. Check: angle is newsworthy to a journalist, not just interesting to the founder.
2. **Decide the earned-media lane.** Decide whether this moment fits a press pitch, a founder thought-leadership placement, or an awards/best-of submission, since each needs a different list and pitch. Skill: `brand/earned-media-sourcing`. Output: lane decision. Check: lane decision states why the other two lanes do not fit better this time.
3. **Build the media list (press lane).** Search for journalists and outlets whose beat matches this specific angle, verified as currently active on that beat. Skill: `brand/media-list-builder`. Output: verified, beat-matched media list. Check: every contact's beat match is checked against their recent bylines, not an old media database entry.
4. **Source thought-leadership slots (if that lane).** Find podcasts, panels, and bylined-article slots that fit the founder's actual expertise and the brand's story. Skill: `brand/earned-media-sourcing`. Output: thought-leadership opportunity list. Check: every opportunity matches a real, demonstrable area of founder expertise.
5. **Track award and best-of calls (if that lane).** Track industry awards and best-of list calls the brand qualifies for. Skill: `brand/earned-media-sourcing`. Output: award call tracker. Check: only calls the brand actually qualifies for are shortlisted.
6. **Seed through a few respected insiders, if applicable.** For a launch, consider seeding through a few respected insiders in a high-trust community before a broad press push. Skill: `brand/pr-angle-builder`. Output: seed-list note. Check: insiders are chosen for real relevance to the avatar's community, not just follower count.
7. **Draft the pitch or submission.** Draft the pitch email, thought-leadership pitch, or award submission copy, claim-checked and in brand voice. Skill: `brand/pr-angle-builder`. Output: drafted pitch or submission. Check: every claim in the draft passes claim-check.
8. **Founder or human sends.** This is human_assist: the founder or a person sends the actual pitch. The agent prepares everything up to the send. Skill: `none`. Output: handoff packet. Check: packet is ready to send with no missing field.
9. **Log outcomes.** Log which pitches placed, which did not, and why, so future angles improve. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: a non-placement is logged with the reason given, not left blank.

Outputs: pitch angle, verified media list, thought-leadership opportunity list, award call tracker, drafted pitch or submission. QA: media list beat-matched and freshly verified; every claim in a pitch passes claim-check; non-placements logged with reasons. Read point: tracked per pitch: placed, declined, or no response, reviewed monthly.. Duration: agent time 3-6 hours per angle; media outreach and response time adds 1-4 weeks..

### BRAND-W22 Monitor reputation risk and draft a crisis holding statement

Trigger: alert-driven: a spike in complaints, viral criticism, or a factual attack is detected | on demand. Human role: what a PR manager does the moment something starts going wrong publicly. Capabilities: BRAND-C54. Needs: public mention and review monitoring signal, brand facts to verify or refute a claim, founder availability for rapid review.

1. **Detect the spike.** Watch public mentions and reviews for a sudden spike in complaint volume, a viral criticism, or a specific factual attack. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: spike alert. Check: spike is measured against a baseline, not a single loud post.
2. **Verify the underlying claim.** Check whether the criticism is factually accurate against the brand's own records before drafting any response. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: fact verification note. Check: note states true, false, or partially true, with the evidence.
3. **Assess severity and reach.** Estimate how far the criticism has spread and how much brand or revenue risk it carries. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: severity read. Check: severity uses actual reach numbers where available, not a gut call.
4. **Draft a holding statement.** Draft a short holding statement acknowledging the issue and stating what happens next, without over-promising or admitting an unverified fault. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: draft holding statement. Check: statement makes no claim the fact verification note does not support.
5. **Draft the escalation note.** Write who inside the brand needs to see this now and what decision they need to make. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: escalation note. Check: escalation note names a specific person and a specific decision, not 'someone should look at this'.
6. **Founder gate before anything is posted.** This is always founder_gate: nothing is posted, replied to, or sent publicly without founder review. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: no public response goes out without this step. Ask the founder if: always, immediately.
7. **Log and monitor after response.** Log what was said and continue monitoring for 48-72 hours to see if the issue is resolving. Skill: `brand/reputation-risk-monitor`. Output: post-response monitoring note, registry entry. Check: monitoring continues past the first response, not a one-time check.

Outputs: spike alert, fact verification note, draft holding statement, escalation note. QA: nothing posted without founder review; statement makes no unverified claim; monitoring continues after the response. Read point: immediate at detection; re-checked at 48-72 hours after response.. Duration: agent time 1-3 hours to prepare; founder decision needed within hours for a live crisis..

### BRAND-W23 Run the partnership pipeline: source, screen and clear

Trigger: twice yearly sourcing sweep | on demand when a specific collaboration is proposed. Human role: what a partnerships lead does from a cold sourcing sweep to a cleared, briefed collaboration. Capabilities: BRAND-C70, BRAND-C41, BRAND-C71. Needs: avatar record (who the partner should also serve), current category and non-competitive adjacent categories, a specific proposal, if screening one rather than sourcing.

1. **Source candidates (sweep).** Proactively search for non-competitive brands serving the same avatar, twice a year, before any specific proposal exists. Skill: `brand/partnership-sourcing-sweep`. Output: candidate partner list. Check: every candidate serves the same avatar without competing in the same category.
2. **Screen for category overlap.** Check a candidate or a proposed partner for direct category overlap; default to non-competitive brands. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: overlap verdict. Check: overlap verdict is explicit: none, adjacent, or competing.
3. **Screen for narrative fit.** Check whether the collaboration tells a story either brand's customer would actually want, not just a co-marketing convenience. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: narrative fit verdict. Check: verdict names the specific shared story, not 'it could work'.
4. **Run the reputation check.** Run a public reputation and controversy check on the other party before any agreement moves forward. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: reputation check result. Check: a flag here stops the process until the founder reviews it. Ask the founder if: a reputation flag is found.
5. **Draft the brief, if screened favorably.** Draft what the collaboration would look like: format, duration, what each side contributes, and rough terms. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: collaboration brief. Check: brief states the specific customer value, not just brand exposure.
6. **Founder gate.** Present the screened candidate and brief for founder approval before any outreach is made. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows all 3 screen results (overlap, narrative fit, reputation) together. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Re-check yearly for ongoing relationships.** For an approved, ongoing partnership, re-run the reputation check yearly. Skill: `brand/partnership-screen`. Output: annual reputation re-check. Check: re-check happens on schedule, not only when a problem is already visible.
8. **Log the pipeline.** Log sourced candidates, screening outcomes and founder decisions to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: a rejected candidate's reason is kept so the same pitch is not repeated unchanged.

Outputs: candidate partner list, overlap verdict, narrative fit verdict, reputation check result, collaboration brief. QA: default to non-competitive brands; reputation check always run before agreement; annual re-check on ongoing partnerships. Read point: sourcing sweep twice yearly; screening as proposals arrive; reputation re-check yearly on live partnerships.. Duration: agent time 2-4 hours per candidate; founder review 2-5 days..

### BRAND-W24 Design an event or pop-up

Trigger: on demand. Human role: what an events lead designs when the exact target customer already gathers somewhere real. Capabilities: BRAND-C42. Needs: avatar record: where this customer actually gathers in person, team capacity (who can staff it), budget ledger.

1. **Name where the avatar already gathers.** Identify a real place or occasion the target avatar already gathers (a wedding expo, a college fest, a farmers' market, a festival pop-up), not a generic venue. Skill: `brand/event-popup-design`. Output: venue/occasion candidate list. Check: each candidate names a real, checkable gathering, not an assumed one.
2. **Size to team capacity.** Size the activation to what the team can actually run by hand, not a scaled-up fantasy version. Skill: `brand/event-popup-design`. Output: sized activation concept. Check: concept states exactly how many people are needed to staff it.
