3. **Design the guerrilla or pop-up format.** Design the specific format: a sampling booth, a pop-up shop-in-shop, a small guerrilla activation, matched to the occasion. Skill: `brand/event-popup-design`. Output: activation format design. Check: format matches the venue's real constraints (footfall, permission needed, timing).
4. **Budget the activation.** Cost the activation (space, staff, materials, any permit or vendor fee) against the ledger. Skill: `brand/event-popup-design`. Output: activation budget. Check: budget checked against the ledger before proceeding. Ask the founder if: the budget would exceed the working stop.
5. **Plan the read.** Define what success looks like and how it will be measured (footfall, samples given, codes redeemed, follow-on online sales in the following days). Skill: `brand/event-popup-design`. Output: read plan. Check: read plan has a specific metric and a specific measurement date.
6. **Founder gate.** Present the full plan and budget for founder approval before any booking or spend commitment. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows budget and read plan together. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Log and route execution.** Log the approved plan; hand logistics execution to the founder or team since this agent does not book vendors or handle cash. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, execution handoff. Check: handoff packet has every logistics detail the plan specified.

Outputs: venue/occasion candidate list, sized activation concept, activation budget, read plan. QA: venue is real and checkable; budget checked against the ledger before commitment; read plan has a specific metric and date. Read point: on the date set in the read plan, against the specific metric named there.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours to plan; lead time to execute varies by venue, typically 2-6 weeks..

### BRAND-W25 Run competitive intelligence: win-loss and battlecards

Trigger: quarterly win-loss research | on demand battlecard build, or when a named competitor materially changes. Human role: what a competitive-intelligence lead does to arm support and the founder with real comparison language. Capabilities: BRAND-C57, BRAND-C58. Needs: named competitor, recent near-miss and win customers, if running win-loss, competitor packet (BRAND-W04).

1. **Sample near-miss and win customers.** Pull a small sample of people who almost bought and instead bought the named competitor, or who chose this brand over it. Skill: `brand/competitive-intelligence`. Output: win-loss sample list. Check: sample includes both directions (near-miss and win), not just one.
2. **Ask why, in their own words.** Ask the sample why they chose as they did, without leading toward a feature the brand wants to hear about. Skill: `brand/competitive-intelligence`. Output: win-loss transcripts. Check: questions are open, matching the interview-guide standard.
3. **Verify the differences named.** Check every difference customers name against what is actually true today (price, feature, delivery time, policy), not what used to be true. Skill: `brand/competitive-intelligence`. Output: verified difference list. Check: every difference is checked against a current, dated source.
4. **Build the battlecard.** Turn verified differences into a short internal battlecard support or the founder can use in a real conversation. Skill: `brand/competitive-intelligence`. Output: internal battlecard. Check: battlecard is internal-facing only, never published externally as a comparison page without a separate legal check.
5. **Check for a public-claim boundary.** Confirm nothing in the battlecard copies competitor text, logos, or makes an unverifiable claim, since a battlecard can leak into public-facing use. Skill: `none`. Output: boundary check result. Check: every comparison claim traces to the verified difference list.
6. **Distribute to support.** Hand the battlecard to the support team or macro system for use in real conversations. Skill: `none`. Output: distributed battlecard. Check: distribution is logged with a version number.
7. **Refresh on trigger.** Refresh the battlecard when a named competitor materially changes price, feature set, or policy. Skill: `brand/competitive-intelligence`. Output: refresh trigger note. Check: refresh trigger names the specific change detected.

Outputs: win-loss transcripts, verified difference list, internal battlecard. QA: differences verified against current, dated sources; battlecard stays internal unless separately cleared; win-loss questions open, not leading. Read point: quarterly for win-loss; on demand for battlecard refresh.. Duration: agent time 3-5 hours; win-loss interviews add 1-2 weeks for scheduling..

### BRAND-W26 Run a wild bet from idea to stage gate to execution brief

Trigger: monthly backlog sourcing | event: a specific wild bet is proposed or approved. Human role: what a new-ventures lead does from a raw idea to a bounded, briefed bet the team can actually run. Capabilities: BRAND-C44, BRAND-C45, BRAND-C46, BRAND-C51, BRAND-C62, BRAND-C63, BRAND-C64. Needs: contribution margin and team capacity from the metric contract, brand brain: values and category norms, the wild bet's type: new line, new market, licensing, wholesale, or retail pop-up.

1. **Source and log backlog ideas.** Continuously source wild bet candidates from adjacent industries, category norms worth breaking, and gaps the brand's own research has surfaced. Keep a dated backlog. Skill: `brand/wild-bet`. Output: dated backlog entry. Check: every entry names the category norm it would break or the gap it fills.
2. **Screen a proposed bet against the backlog and brand fit.** When a bet is proposed (by the founder, or pulled from the backlog), check it against brand values and current capacity before bounding it. Skill: `brand/wild-bet`. Output: screen verdict. Check: a bet that conflicts with a stated brand value is flagged, not silently bounded anyway.
3. **Decide the bet type.** Classify the bet as a new product line, a new market, a licensing deal, a wholesale/retail entry, or a retail pop-up, since each routes to a different feasibility check. Skill: `brand/wild-bet`. Output: bet type classification. Check: classification determines exactly one routing path below.
4. **Run the type-specific feasibility pass.** New line: size against BRAND-W11 market sizing. New market: check regulatory, tax, competitor and logistics feasibility for the target country or region. Licensing: check royalty norms, score the licensee, draft deal-memo terms. Wholesale/retail: build the line sheet and buyer pitch kit, and check channel/packaging fit against DTC, marketplace and retail formats. Skill: `brand/wild-bet/new-line`. Output: feasibility packet, shaped to the bet type. Check: packet names the specific constraint each type check found, not a generic 'looks feasible'.
5. **Write the 8-field bound.** Write the hypothesis, category norm broken, budget cap, downside cap (as a share of contribution margin, computed in code), cohort and size, control design, stop rule, read-point date, and legal/consent flag. Skill: `brand/wild-bet`. Output: 8-field bound. Check: all 8 fields present and specific; downside cap computed in code, not estimated.
6. **Flag legal review if warranted.** Flag for legal review if the bet promises something, mails or texts people, or gates access in a way that carries legal exposure. Skill: `brand/wild-bet`. Output: legal-review flag. Check: flag defaults to 'needs review' when uncertain, never defaults to 'clear'.
7. **Founder gate on the bound.** Present the bound for founder approval before anything launches. No wild bet runs on the bound alone without this step. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision on the bound. Check: a bound with no read point or stop rule is never presented as ready. Ask the founder if: always.
8. **Write the type-specific execution brief.** Turn the approved bound into an execution brief sized to the smallest group the team can serve by hand, sequenced through the checks the bet's type requires (for example: retail pop-up sequences packaging and channel fit before a buyer pitch; licensing sequences the deal memo before any product ships). Skill: `brand/wild-bet/retail-popup`. Output: execution brief. Check: brief names every check completed before launch, in order.
9. **Founder gate on the brief.** Present the execution brief for a final founder go/no-go before work starts. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision on the brief. Check: go/no-go is explicit, not implied by silence. Ask the founder if: always.
10. **Log and set the read reminder.** Log the bound and brief to the registry, and set a reminder for the stated read-point date so the bet is judged on schedule, not forgotten. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry with a scheduled read-point check. Check: read point is a specific date, not 'later'.

Outputs: dated backlog entry, feasibility packet, 8-field bound, execution brief. QA: downside cap computed in code as a share of contribution margin; all 8 bound fields present and specific; legal-review flag defaults to caution; read-point date is scheduled, not left open-ended. Read point: set explicitly in the bound at approval time; this workflow does not itself score the outcome, it schedules that check.. Duration: agent time 1-3 days depending on bet type; founder review 3-7 days across the two gates; a new-market feasibility pass can add 1-2 weeks..

### BRAND-W27 Run an AI-persona pretest before a real research round

Trigger: on demand, before spending on a real survey, interview round, or paid message test. Human role: what a researcher does to catch an obvious miss cheaply before paying for a real round. Capabilities: BRAND-C66. Needs: messaging, naming or concept shortlist to pretest, avatar record to build the persona from, budget ledger.

1. **Check the budget.** Confirm the ledger has room before running any paid AI-persona pass. Skill: `none`. Output: budget clearance. Check: checked before the call, not after. Ask the founder if: this would exceed the working stop.
2. **Build the persona from real evidence.** Build the simulated persona strictly from the existing avatar record's job, forces and quotes, not from a generic stereotype. Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: persona brief. Check: every trait in the persona brief traces to the avatar record.
3. **Run the pass on the shortlist.** Run the simulated persona against the messaging, naming or concept shortlist and record its reactions. Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: simulated reaction notes. Check: notes are labeled 'simulated, not a real customer' everywhere they are shown.
4. **Flag obvious misses only.** Use the pass only to catch obvious misses (confusing wording, an unintended reading, a flat concept), never to declare a winner. Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: flagged misses list. Check: output never ranks candidates as if this were real customer data.
5. **Decide whether a real round is still needed.** State plainly that this pretest does not replace the planned real survey or interview round; it only trims the shortlist first. Skill: `brand/ai-persona-pretest`. Output: trimmed shortlist. Check: trimmed shortlist still goes to the real research round, not treated as final.
6. **Log the pretest.** Log the pretest and its cost to the registry and budget ledger. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: cost logged against the ledger, not left untracked.

Outputs: persona brief, simulated reaction notes, trimmed shortlist. QA: persona built from real avatar evidence, not stereotype; output never presented as if it were real customer data; budget checked before the call. Read point: not a metric read; this is a cheap filter before the real read comes from the survey, interview or test that follows.. Duration: agent time 1-2 hours..

### BRAND-W28 Advise the founder on brand-building operating practices

Trigger: on demand. Human role: what an operating advisor does to turn recurring patterns into short, reusable guidance. Capabilities: BRAND-C52. Needs: registry history of past brand decisions and their outcomes, founder's stated operating question (hiring sequence, ideation method, decision speed, readiness checks).

1. **Name the operating question.** State the specific recurring pattern the founder is asking about (when to hire for a brand role, how fast to decide on a name, when a brand is ready for a wild bet). Skill: `none`. Output: operating question line. Check: question is specific enough to answer with a checklist, not a philosophy.
2. **Pull the registry history.** Pull past decisions and outcomes on this pattern from the registry (past naming timelines, past wild bet readiness, past hire timing). Skill: `none`. Output: history pull. Check: history pull uses this brand's real past decisions, not general theory alone.
3. **Draw the pattern.** State what actually correlated with a good outcome versus a bad one in this brand's own history. Skill: `none`. Output: pattern statement. Check: pattern is stated with the evidence count behind it (how many past cases).
4. **Write short, reusable guidance.** Turn the pattern into a short checklist or rule of thumb the founder can reuse without re-deriving it each time. Skill: `none`. Output: guidance note. Check: guidance fits on one page and gives a clear yes/no test.
5. **Founder review.** Present the guidance for the founder to confirm it matches their own read of what has worked. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: founder can accept, edit or reject the guidance. Ask the founder if: always.
6. **Log for reuse.** File the accepted guidance where future runs of this agent (or the founder) can find it again. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: guidance is retrievable by the operating question it answers.

Outputs: operating question line, pattern statement, guidance note. QA: pattern grounded in this brand's real history, not generic advice; guidance gives a clear yes/no test. Read point: not a metric read; revisited if a later decision contradicts the guidance.. Duration: agent time 1-2 hours..

### BRAND-W29 Brief packaging artwork and mandatory label placement

Trigger: event: new SKU, new channel, or a packaging change. Human role: what a packaging coordinator briefs before artwork goes to print. Capabilities: BRAND-G01. Needs: product spec from the Product and Supply specialist (PROD-C13), target channel(s): DTC, marketplace, retail, brand guidelines (visual language section).

1. **Pull the product spec.** Pull the dieline, net quantity, and any per-channel packaging constraint from the Product and Supply specialist's spec. Skill: `brand/packaging-label-brief`. Output: spec pull. Check: spec is pulled, never re-derived from guesswork.
2. **Apply the visual language.** Apply the brand guidelines' palette, typography and logo rules to the dieline layout. Skill: `brand/packaging-label-brief`. Output: layout draft. Check: layout follows the logo clear-space and minimum-size rules.
3. **Place mandatory declarations.** Place every legally required declaration for the target market: MRP, Legal Metrology details (net quantity, manufacturer/packer/importer name and address, month and year of manufacture, consumer care contact), BIS or hallmark mark where the product category requires it, and the EPR mark where applicable. Skill: `brand/packaging-label-brief`. Output: declaration-placed layout. Check: every mandatory field for this product category is present and legible at the required minimum size.
4. **Check channel-specific rules.** Check any marketplace-specific packaging or labeling rule (for example, marketplace return-label or barcode placement requirements) beyond the general legal requirements. Skill: `brand/packaging-label-brief`. Output: channel rule check. Check: each target channel's specific rule is checked, not assumed same as DTC.
5. **Run the completeness check.** Run the full checklist of mandatory declarations against the layout before it is called ready. Skill: `brand/packaging-label-brief`. Output: completeness checklist result. Check: a missing mandatory field blocks sign-off, it does not go to print with a note to 'fix later'.
6. **Founder gate.** Present the brief and checklist result for founder sign-off before it goes to a designer or printer. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: card shows the checklist result plainly. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Hand off and log.** Hand the approved brief to the designer or print vendor; log the version to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry, handoff packet. Check: version is tracked so a later channel addition updates from the current approved version, not an old one.

Outputs: layout draft, declaration-placed layout, completeness checklist result, packaging brief. QA: every mandatory declaration present and legible; channel-specific rules checked, not assumed; missing field blocks sign-off. Read point: not a metric read; a one-time brief per SKU/channel/packaging change, re-checked if the law or channel changes.. Duration: agent time 2-4 hours; founder review 1-2 days; print lead time is separate..

### BRAND-W30 Manage a rebrand or repositioning program

Trigger: on demand, when a rebrand or repositioning is warranted. Human role: what a brand lead runs when the identity itself, not just a campaign, needs to change. Capabilities: BRAND-C49. Needs: evidence the current brand is failing to win (from BRAND-W17 perception-gap audit, BRAND-W18 brand health, or a founder call), current brand guidelines, positioning, and catalogue.

1. **Audit brand equity.** Assess what the current brand actually owns in the customer's mind (from BRAND-W17's perception-gap findings and BRAND-W18's health trend), separate from what the founder wishes it owned. Skill: `none`. Output: equity audit. Check: audit is grounded in the last 2 quarters of real findings, not a fresh guess.
2. **Decide what to keep.** Name what survives the rebrand (an equity asset, a color, a customer relationship) versus what changes. Skill: `none`. Output: keep/change list. Check: every 'keep' item names the equity reason it survives.
3. **Expect and plan catalogue pruning.** Flag that a rebrand usually forces catalogue pruning (SKUs that only made sense under the old story) and name likely candidates. Skill: `none`. Output: pruning candidate list. Check: a pruning candidate names the specific story mismatch, not just low sales.
4. **Sequence the program.** Sequence the actual work: positioning (BRAND-W10), naming if needed (BRAND-W12), voice and story (BRAND-W14), visual identity and guidelines (BRAND-W16), then a phased rollout across site, ads and packaging. Skill: `none`. Output: phased program plan. Check: plan sequences dependencies correctly (positioning before voice, voice before guidelines).
5. **Set the real trigger metric.** Set the metric the rebrand is meant to move (brand health trend, repeat rate, or a stated perception-gap closing), not vanity attention. Skill: `none`. Output: trigger metric and target. Check: metric already exists in the metric contract; nothing invented for this program.
6. **Founder gate.** Present the full program, sequencing and trigger metric for founder approval before any phase starts, since this touches every other brand workflow. Skill: `shared/founder-question-round`. Output: founder decision. Check: founder approves the sequence, not just the idea of rebranding. Ask the founder if: always.
7. **Run the sequenced phases.** Execute each phase workflow in order, routing to BRAND-W10, W12, W14 and W16 as sequenced, each with its own founder gate. Skill: `none`. Output: phase completion log. Check: no phase starts before its dependency phase is founder-approved.
8. **Measure by shipped artifacts against the real trigger.** Measure the program's success by what actually shipped and moved the trigger metric, against a real prior trend, not a launch-week spike. Skill: `none`. Output: program readout. Check: readout compares to the pre-rebrand trend, not just a single before/after point.
9. **Log the program.** Log the full program, decisions and readout to the registry. Skill: `shared/registry-entry`. Output: registry entry. Check: readout is honest about what did not move, not only what did.

Outputs: equity audit, keep/change list, phased program plan, program readout. QA: equity audit grounded in real recent findings; dependencies sequenced correctly; readout compared to a real prior trend, not a spike. Read point: set at program approval, measured against the pre-rebrand trend after the full sequence ships.. Duration: agent time spans the full program (weeks to months); each phase carries its own workflow's duration..
